The start() part also holds. start() captures `source` before `await this._pendingStopCleanup`. A switchTimeline that runs during that await deactivates and replaces `_activePositionSource`, and start() then activates the stale source. That needs stop, start and switch to interleave, which is rarer.  No test covers overlapping seeks. I grepped src/test for concurrent, overlapping or second-seek cases and found none.  I put severity at medium. The result is visibly wrong (a stale element stays on screen) and can be reached from a valid configuration through ordinary user input. It recovers on the next seek or stop, and nothing crashes.

**Repro test.** `given action C [0,3] active with an end that does not settle until released, and actions A [4,6] and B [7,9], when seek(5) is called and then seek(8) is called before C's end settles and C's end is then released, then only B is active and A is not active`

### 14. [medium] Start and end of one action are not sequenced across updates (loop wrap)

[eligius-engine.ts:1166](src/eligius-engine.ts#L1166) · area: engine-core · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** Ends now wait for nothing and starts only wait for ends of the same update. A zero-length or skipped action calls end() right after start() without waiting for start to finish. On a loop wrap, RafPositionSource emits `duration` and then `0` in the same tick, so an action [0,duration] is ended by the first update and restarted by the second while its end operations are still running. The end and start operation chains of one action then interleave.

**Failure scenario.** Loop wrap: a looping 10s timeline has action A [0,10]. End operations are [selectElement, animateWithClass 'fade-out' (async), removeElement] and start operations are [selectElement '#stage', setElementContent '<div class=a>']. On wrap, A.end starts the fade-out, and the backward update immediately runs A.start, which inserts a new .a. Then the end's removeElement runs and removes the freshly inserted element, so the element is missing for the whole next loop. Skipped frames: one update crosses both ends of [0.35,0.75]. start() is awaiting an async animate when end() runs removeClass, and the start's later addClass leaves the class applied permanently.

**Evidence.**

```ts
this._pendingStarts.delete(action);
      this._runActionMethod(action, 'start');
      if (start.endAfterStart) {
        this._runActionMethod(action, 'end');
      }
...
    if (!ends.length) {
      this._runPendingStarts(starts);
      return;
    }
```

**Fix.** Keep a per-action tail promise, `private _actionChains = new Map<ITimelineAction, Promise<void>>()`. Add a helper `_chainActionMethod(action, phase)` that does `const next = (this._actionChains.get(action) ?? Promise.resolve()).then(() => this._runActionMethod(action, phase)); this._actionChains.set(action, next); next.then(() => { if (this._actionChains.get(action) === next) this._actionChains.delete(action); }); return next;`. Use it in `_dispatchActionsBetween` (ends) and `_runPendingStarts` (start, then end when `endAfterStart`, which now chains automatically). Note that `TimelineAction.start` and `end` flip `active` synchronously only when they are invoked. When an action method is deferred, the dispatcher's `active` checks see the stale value. So either flip a dispatcher-side "scheduled active" flag at chain time, or keep the synchronous `active` flip by calling a TimelineAction method that sets the flag immediately and defers only the operations. Also route `_executeActions`, `_reconcileActions` and `_endActiveActionsReporting` (seek, stop, switch, cleanup) through the same chain, so a seek cannot overlap an in-flight tick-dispatched end. Clear the map in destroy.

Original suggestion: Track the in-flight start/end promise per action, for example a Map<ITimelineAction, Promise<void>> set by `_runActionMethod`. Chain a start or end of an action after that action's previous in-flight operation instead of firing it immediately. The endAfterStart case then becomes `start.then(() => end)`.

**Verifier.** I traced the real code and could not find anything that guards against this.  1. Loop wrap. `RafPositionSource._onTick` (raf-position-source.ts ~185-197) calls `setPosition(duration)`, then `emitBoundary('end')`, then `setPosition(0)` in the same tick when `loop` is true. `BasePositionSource.setPosition` calls the position callbacks synchronously. The engine's `onPosition` callback runs `_onTimeHandler` directly.    - Forward update (e.g. 9.98 -> 10). The end event of A [0,10] at 10 falls in (from, to]. A is active, so `ends.push(A)` and `_runActionMethod(A, 'end')` runs. `TimelineAction.end` sets `_active = false` synchronously and returns the still-running end chain. Nothing stores that promise.    - Backward update (10 -> 0). `first = upperBound(events, 0)` excludes the start event at 0. The end event at 10 is visited with `!action.active && duration.start (0) <= to (0)`, so A goes into `toStart`. That update has no ends, so `_runPendingStarts` runs `A.start()` immediately while A's async end operations (an `animateWithClass` awaiting animationend, followed by `removeElement`) are still pending.    - The boundary-end path does not intervene. `_onCompleteCallback` only emits `timelineComplete`. `_handleTimelineComplete` (which clears `_pendingStarts`) is bound only to the eventbus `request-timeline-cleanup` event, not to the wrap.  2. Skipped or zero-length range in one forward update. The start event calls `addStart`, then the end event finds the pending start and sets `endAfterStart = true`. `_runPendingStarts` then calls `_runActionMethod(action, 'start')` and right after it `_runActionMethod(action, 'end')` without awaiting the start, so the two operation chains interleave.  Nothing in the action layer serialises them either. `Action._runOperations` gives each run its own scope stack. The comment in action.ts says "overlapping executions of the same action never share scopes", which accepts overlap at the scope level but does nothing to order DOM effects. The `_dispatchActionsBetween` docblock says "Nothing is awaited here" and promises ordering only between the ends and starts of one update, not across updates or within a single action. I found no test in src/test/unit/eligius-engine.spec.ts or engine-decomposed.spec.ts that covers a looping wrap with async end operations, or a start and end of one action in the same update with async start operations.  The trigger is a valid configuration: a looping timeline with an action ending exactly at the duration (a common "whole timeline" background action) and async end operations. A coarse source skipping a short range with async start operations also triggers it. The result is wrong DOM state (a removed element or a class left applied), not a crash, so medium is the right severity.

**Repro test.** `given a looping timeline with action [0,duration] whose end operations resolve asynchronously, when the source emits duration and then 0 in the same tick, then the action's start operations do not run until its end operations have finished`

### 15. [medium] pause() during an async activate is followed by a stray 'start' event

[eligius-engine.ts:367](src/eligius-engine.ts#L367) · area: engine-core · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** start() emits 'start' whenever `source.activate()` resolves. BasePositionSource.activate resolves without throwing when it was superseded by suspend/deactivate while an async startTicking (video play()) was pending. The engine then emits 'start' (eventbus 'timeline-play') after 'pause' even though playState is 'paused'. pause() and start() also emit their events when the source state did not change (pause while stopped, start while already playing).

**Failure scenario.** Mediaplayer timeline: the user presses space (start, video play() pending) and presses space again before playback begins. pause() suspends and broadcasts 'timeline-pause'. play() then settles, activate() returns early because the state is no longer active, and the engine broadcasts 'timeline-play'. Play-button controllers show 'playing' while the video is paused.

**Evidence.**

```ts
await source.activate();
    this._emitter.emit('start');
...
  pause(): void {
    ...
    this._activePositionSource.suspend();
    this._emitter.emit('pause');
  }
```

**Fix.** In start(): `if (source.state === 'active') return;` before the await (or record the prior state). After `await source.activate()`, emit 'start' only when `this._activePositionSource === source && source.state === 'active'`. In pause(): capture `const wasActive = source.state === 'active'` before suspend() and emit 'pause' only if wasActive. Optionally do the same in stop() for the already-inactive case.

Original suggestion: After `await source.activate()`, emit 'start' only if `source.state === 'active'` (and the source is still the active one). In pause(), emit 'pause' only if the state was 'active' before suspend. In start(), skip the emit when the source was already active.

**Verifier.** I traced the scenario through the code and it happens as reported. In src/eligius-engine.ts:356-369, start() does `await source.activate(); this._emitter.emit('start');` with no check on the source state afterwards. BasePositionSource.activate (src/timelineproviders/position-sources/base-position-source.ts:110-138) sets `_state = 'active'` synchronously, then awaits an async startTicking(). If suspend() or deactivate() runs before that settles, activate() returns normally in both cases. On a resolve, lines 134-135 return early. On a reject, lines 126-128 return early without rethrowing. The base-position-source.spec.ts test at line 420 asserts this resolve-without-throwing behaviour on purpose. VideoPositionSource.startTicking (video-position-source.ts:217-222) is `async` and awaits `player.play()`, so a mediaplayer timeline takes the async path. Browsers reject a pending play() with AbortError when pause() interrupts it, and that goes through the silent-return branch.  The toggle path makes this reachable from normal use. engine-eventbus-adapter.ts:136-141 reads `engine.playState`, which maps the source state, and the source state is already 'active' synchronously after the first start(). A second toggle therefore calls engine.pause(). That suspends the source and emits 'pause' ('timeline-pause'). When play() settles, start() continues and emits 'start', which the adapter at lines 47-48 broadcasts as 'timeline-play', while playState is 'paused'.  I found no guard in the engine or the adapter, and no engine test covers this ordering. Only the source-level behaviour is tested. The secondary claims also hold. pause() emits 'pause' even when suspend() was a no-op (stopped or already paused). start() emits 'start' when activate() was a no-op because the source was already active.  Severity stays medium. It needs a quick double toggle or play/pause on a video timeline, which is ordinary user input. Any controller that tracks timeline-play and timeline-pause ends up showing the wrong state. Engine state itself is not corrupted: playState stays correct.

**Repro test.** `given a mediaplayer source whose startTicking is pending, when pause() is called before it settles, then the engine emits 'pause' and never emits 'start' afterwards`

### 16. [medium] Failing action in seek/switch reconcile aborts it and bypasses the error event

[eligius-engine.ts:1185](src/eligius-engine.ts#L1185) · area: lens-performance · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** _reconcileActions and _cleanUpTimeline use _executeActions, which awaits action.start()/end() with no catch. Unlike the tick path (_runActionMethod), one failing action rejects seek() or switchTimeline() part-way. The remaining covering actions are not started, seekComplete/time are not emitted, and no EngineActionError reaches the `error` event.

**Failure scenario.** Timeline with A=[0,10) whose start op throws, and B=[0,10) after it. engine.seek(5): A.start rejects, the loop exits, B is never started, seek() rejects (the adapter only logs 'engine.seek() failed'), and 'timeline-seeked' is never broadcast. _lastPosition is already 5, so later ticks never start B either. In switchTimeline, a failing end in _cleanUpTimeline throws after previousSource.deactivate(), before _currentTimelineUri changes. The engine is left stopped on the old timeline and no error event is emitted.

**Evidence.**

```ts
private async _executeActions(
    actions: IEndableAction[],
    methodName: 'start' | 'end'
  ): Promise<void> {
    for (const action of actions) {
      await action[methodName]();
    }
  }
...
    await this._executeActions(actionsToEnd, 'end');
    await this._executeActions(actionsToStart, 'start');
```

**Fix.** In `_reconcileActions`, iterate `actionsToEnd` and then `actionsToStart`, awaiting `this._runActionMethod(action, 'end' | 'start')` for each. In `_cleanUpTimeline`, iterate the active actions with `await this._runActionMethod(action, 'end')`. That is the same loop as `_endActiveActionsReporting`, parameterised by uri. Both then attempt every action, never reject, and report through the `error` event. Keep `_executeActions` only for init actions, where `init()` is meant to reject. Add tests for seek and for switchTimeline with a failing start and a failing end.

Original suggestion: In _reconcileActions and _cleanUpTimeline use _runActionMethod, which never rejects and reports through the `error` event, so every action is attempted. Keep _executeActions only for init actions, where rejecting init() is the documented behaviour.

**Verifier.** I traced the code in F:\projects\eligius\eligius\src\eligius-engine.ts and could not refute the report.  - **seek() path.** seek() sets `_lastPosition = position` (line 438) and then awaits `_reconcileActions` (line 439). `_reconcileActions` (lines 1177-1187) awaits `_executeActions(actionsToEnd, 'end')` and then `_executeActions(actionsToStart, 'start')`. `_executeActions` (lines 934-941) is a bare `for … await action[methodName]()` with no catch. - **Action methods do reject.** `TimelineAction.start` sets `_active = true` and returns `super.start`. `Action.start` (src/action/action.ts lines 31-36) logs and rethrows, so the returned promise rejects. When A's start operation throws, the loop exits. B (covering 5, listed after A) is never started. seek() rejects before emitting `seekComplete`/`time`, so the adapter never broadcasts `timeline-seeked`. `EngineEventbusAdapter._handleRejection` only calls console.error. - **B stays unstarted.** `_lastPosition` is already 5 and B's start key is 0. Forward ticks only dispatch events in `(from, to]`, so `_dispatchActionsBetween` never starts B again. That happens only after a backward move or another seek. - **No error event.** `_reportActionError` / `EngineActionError` is only reached via `_runActionMethod`, which this path never calls. - **switchTimeline() path.** `_switchTimeline` calls `previousSource.deactivate()`, then awaits `_cleanUpTimeline(previousUri)`, which also uses `_executeActions`. A throwing end operation rejects there. That leaves the old source deactivated, `_currentTimelineUri` unchanged, the remaining active actions not ended, the new source not activated, and no error event. - **Contradicts the documented contract.** CHANGELOG says the engine "emits `error` with an `EngineActionError` … when an action it starts or ends fails". The stop() docstring and `_endActiveActionsReporting` follow that contract. Only the seek/switch reconcile and cleanup paths do not. - **No test covers this.** The error-event tests in src/test/unit/eligius-engine.spec.ts (around line 2215) cover only the tick path (`source.report`). None covers seek or switchTimeline with a failing action.  The trigger is a valid configuration: any runtime-throwing operation in a timeline action is enough, for example a failed `loadJson`, a `customFunction` error, or a missing dependency. Medium severity is fair. The engine is not corrupted beyond recovery, but seek/switch leaves the timeline's actions half-reconciled and skips the documented error reporting.

**Repro test.** `given two timeline actions covering 5 whose first start operation throws, when engine.seek(5) is awaited, then seek resolves, the second action is active, seekComplete is emitted and one EngineActionError with phase start is emitted on the error event`

### 17. [medium] RAF clock floors elapsed while its scheduler rounds it, losing a tick on early rAF

[raf-position-source.ts:177](src/timelineproviders/position-sources/raf-position-source.ts#L177) · area: lens-performance · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** animationInterval aligns frames with Math.round, because the rAF timestamp can be slightly earlier than the target boundary (Jake Archibald's original pattern). _onTick converts the same elapsed value with Math.floor. A frame whose timestamp lands just before the boundary therefore reports the previous tick's position, which the engine deduplicates. The scheduler meanwhile rounds up and targets the boundary after next, so one position is skipped entirely and the clock runs a full tick late for that frame.

**Failure scenario.** tickInterval 1000. The frame for the 5 s boundary has rAF time - start = 4999.6 ms (frame begin time before the timer target). _onTick computes floor(4.9996) = 4, which equals the last position, so nothing is dispatched. scheduleFrame rounds to 5000 and targets 6000. The next tick reports 6, so position 5 is never reported. Actions at 5 fire about 1 s late, and 'timeline-time' consumers (progress bar, subtitles) jump by 2 s. With tickInterval 100 the same happens with a 100 ms delay.

**Evidence.**

```ts
raf-position-source.ts:177  const ticksElapsed = Math.floor(elapsedThisIteration / this._tickInterval);
animation-interval.ts:34    const roundedElapsed = round(elapsed / ms) * ms;
animation-interval.ts:35    const targetNext = start + roundedElapsed + ms;
```

**Fix.** Make the position conversion agree with the scheduler. The simplest fix is ticksElapsed = Math.round(elapsedThisIteration / this._tickInterval), the same rounding animationInterval uses. The spec at raf-position-source.spec.ts:553 then needs a different value (350 would become 0.4), and the docblock at :161-163 needs updating.  If you would rather keep floor semantics for late frames, add a tolerance instead: Math.floor((elapsedThisIteration + tol) / this._tickInterval) with tol = Math.min(this._tickInterval / 2, 20). An early frame then lands on the boundary the scheduler targeted, and a genuinely late frame still floors.  Either way, add tests that feed 999.6 and 1999.7 with tickInterval 1000 and 99.5 with tickInterval 100. Also add a test where elapsed is just below duration*1000 to cover the end boundary.

Original suggestion: Use Math.round(elapsedThisIteration / this._tickInterval) in _onTick so the position matches the boundary the scheduler targeted. Add a test that feeds elapsed values just below a multiple (999.5, 1999.7).

**Verifier.** I traced the scenario through the real code and it holds.  1. animationInterval (src/util/animation-interval.ts:25-38) calls callback(time - start), where time is the rAF timestamp. It then schedules the next frame at start + round(elapsed/ms)*ms + ms. Rounding only makes sense because a rAF timestamp can be earlier than the timer target, as in Jake Archibald's original pattern. Two things cause this:    - setTimeout truncates a fractional delay to an integer (WebIDL long), so a delay of 999.7 becomes 999.    - The rAF timestamp is the frame's begin/vsync time. That time can come before the timer task that requested the frame, when the timer runs between vsync and the main-frame callbacks.  2. RafPositionSource._onTick (src/timelineproviders/position-sources/raf-position-source.ts:176-180) converts the same elapsed value with Math.floor. With tickInterval 1000:    - A frame at elapsed 4999.6 gives floor = 4, the position already reported.    - BasePositionSource.setPosition emits it again, and EligiusEngine._onTimeHandler (src/eligius-engine.ts:1057) drops it as a duplicate.    - scheduleFrame rounds 4999.6 to 5000 and targets 6000. The next frame reports 6, so position 5 is never reported.  3. If every frame is a little early, the clock does not skip. It stays one full tick behind instead (0, 0, 1, 2, ...). Mixed jitter gives both lags and skips. The end check at :185 is also affected: for duration 5, reaching the end, the end boundary and the deactivate happen one tick (1 s) late.  What limits the impact: _dispatchActionsBetween (eligius-engine.ts:1085+) processes every start and end in (from, to], so actions at 5 are not lost. They fire about one tick late, at 6. 'time' consumers such as the progress bar and subtitles still jump by two ticks.  The tests do not cover this. raf-position-source.spec.ts only feeds exact multiples or values above a boundary (100, 200, 350, 1500, 5700, 3400/3500, 2500, 1300). The 350 case pins the floor behaviour on purpose, but no test pairs it with the scheduler's rounding. animationInterval is mocked in those tests, so the mismatch between the two functions is never exercised.  Why I did not refute it: there is no guard that keeps the elapsed value at or above the boundary. The only open question is how often browsers deliver an early timestamp. That is a known, real occurrence, and it is the reason the original pattern rounds.  Why medium severity: it affects the default RAF animation timeline under normal browser timing with any valid configuration. It costs one tick of lateness or one skipped position, not lost actions or corrupted state.

**Repro test.** `given RafPositionSource with tickInterval 1000, when animationInterval reports elapsed 999.6 (rAF frame slightly before the 1 s boundary), then onPosition is called with 1`

### 18. [medium] In-place resolvePropertyValues walks class instances and rewrites other actions' config

[resolve-property-values.ts:70](src/operation/helper/resolve-property-values.ts#L70) · area: operations-b · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** math, calc and createElement call resolvePropertyValues(operationData, this, operationData). With copy === properties, resolution runs in place, and resolveNewProperties recurses into every value that isObject() accepts. isObject() is true for class instances such as an actionInstance from requestAction, an Eventbus, or a controller. Any '$operationdata.*' string reachable through those objects is therefore resolved against the caller's data and written back into them. That includes the startOperations[].operationData of a requested action, so its resolved configuration is permanently corrupted. On the per-call path, every math/calc/createElement call also walks the whole graph, including the eventbus.

**Failure scenario.** Operations: requestAction {systemName:'ShowPanel'} -> math {functionName:'max', args:[1,2]} -> startAction {}. ShowPanel's start operation is selectElement {selector:'$operationdata.panelSelector'}. math walks operationData.actionInstance.startOperations[0].operationData and sets selector to undefined. I confirmed this in a probe: before '$operationdata.panelSelector', after undefined. startAction then fails with 'selectElement: selector is either empty or not defined', and so does every later run of ShowPanel.

**Evidence.**

```ts
resolve-property-values.ts:59-71: `if (isExternalProperty(value)) { copy[key] = resolvePropertyChain(value); } else if (Array.isArray(value)) { ... } else if (isObject(value)) { resolveNewProperties(value, copy[key], resolvePropertyChain); }` together with math.ts:42 `operationData = resolvePropertyValues(operationData, this, operationData);` and is-object.ts `return !!value && typeof value === 'object' && !Array.isArray(value);`
```

**Fix.** In resolveNewProperties, recurse only into plain objects (prototype is Object.prototype or null, the same isPlainObject check deepCopy uses) and into arrays. Class instances such as Action, Eventbus and controllers, along with Maps and similar objects, should be left alone. Separately, have math, calc and createElement resolve only their own configured keys (args; left/right; elementName/attributes/text) by passing a sub-object as newProperties instead of the whole operationData. Then resolution works on a deepCopy and can never rewrite anything outside those keys.

Original suggestion: Recurse only into plain objects and arrays: use the same isPlainObject check that deepCopy uses (prototype is Object.prototype or null) instead of isObject. Better still, have math, calc and createElement resolve only their own configured keys (args, left/right, elementName/attributes/text), not the whole operationData. Add a test that passes an EndableAction instance through math and asserts that its startOperations are unchanged.

**Verifier.** I traced the path through the code and found nothing that blocks it. math.ts:42, calc.ts:56 and create-element.ts:56 call resolvePropertyValues(operationData, this, operationData). Because newProperties === operationData, resolve-property-values.ts:22-23 skips deepCopy, so copy === properties and every write lands on the live objects. resolveNewProperties recurses into anything that isObject() accepts, and isObject() is true for any non-null, non-array object, including class instances. The only thing it skips is jQuery (`instanceof $`).  requestAction puts the live Action from EngineFactory._actionsLookup into operationData.actionInstance. startAction also returns {...newOperationData, actionInstance}, so the instance stays in the data for every later operation in the chain.  The walk reaches Action.startOperations[i].operationData. That is the resolved configuration object itself: Action.executeOperation only copies it when merging, through copyConfiguredOperationData, and never protects the source. Any '$operationdata.x' string found there is resolved against the caller's data. For a single-segment chain, getPropertyChainValue returns undefined without throwing, and the result is written back into the action's configuration permanently.  The only guard is the cycle cache, which stops infinite loops but not the walk. It also walks the protected eventbus field on the Action.  No existing test covers class instances; the resolve-property-values, math and calc specs use only plain data. I wrote a probe that builds an EndableAction whose start operation has operationData {selector:'$operationdata.panelSelector'}, then runs applyOperation(math, {functionName:'max', args:[1,2], actionInstance}). Afterwards startOperations[0].operationData.selector is undefined, so the test fails today. I deleted the probe afterwards.  Medium severity is fair. A valid configuration triggers this whenever math, calc or createElement runs after requestAction or startAction in the same chain, which is a normal sequence, and the damage persists for every later run of that action. It does not happen with ordinary data-only chains.

**Repro test.** `given an operationData whose actionInstance is an EndableAction with a start operation configured as {selector:'$operationdata.panelSelector'}, when math runs with {functionName:'max', args:[1,2]}, then the action's startOperations[0].operationData.selector is still '$operationdata.panelSelector'`

### 19. [medium] startAction/endAction leak the inner action's in-place mutations into caller data

[start-action.ts:50](src/operation/start-action.ts#L50) · area: operations-b · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** startAction passes newOperationData into actionInstance.start() and then resolves with a spread of that same object. Action.executeOperation runs Object.assign(operationData, configuredData) on the object it is given, and most operations mutate and return it (selectElement sets selectedElement, removeProperties deletes keys). Whatever the requested action's operations write in place therefore comes back as the caller's operation data. Only the keys listed in actionOperationData are removed afterwards. endAction (end-action.ts:46-58) has the same pattern.

**Failure scenario.** Caller: selectElement '#a' -> requestAction 'Highlight' -> startAction {} -> addClass 'done'. Highlight's start operations are [selectElement '#b', addClass 'hl']. After startAction resolves, the caller's selectedElement is #b (confirmed in a probe), so 'done' is added to #b instead of #a. If the caller and the action share a key in actionOperationData, the caller's original value is deleted instead of restored.

**Evidence.**

```ts
start-action.ts:43-56: `newOperationData = mergeOperationData(newOperationData, actionOperationData); actionInstance.start(newOperationData).then(() => { Object.keys(actionOperationData).forEach(key => { delete (newOperationData as any)[key]; }); internalResolve(resolve, {...newOperationData, actionInstance}); })`; action.ts:150 `const mergedOperationData = Object.assign(operationData, configuredData);`
```

**Fix.** In startAction, keep the caller's pre-merge data separate from the object handed to the action. Build `const callerData = newOperationData;` (the rest object without actionInstance/actionOperationData), then call `actionInstance.start(mergeOperationData(callerData, actionOperationData))`, so the action gets its own shallow copy. On completion, resolve with `{...callerData, actionInstance}`. This also keeps the caller's original values for keys that actionOperationData overrode, so the delete loop is no longer needed. In endAction, likewise pass `mergeOperationData(newOperationData, actionOperationData)` to `end()` and resolve with the pre-merge `newOperationData` (which already holds actionInstance), dropping the sanitizedData filter. Add regression tests using real Action instances in which the inner action changes selectedElement, plus one where actionOperationData overrides a key the caller already has.

Original suggestion: Pass a fresh object to the action (`actionInstance.start({...newOperationData})`, or a shallow copy built with mergeOperationData). Resolve with the caller's own pre-merge data plus actionInstance, keeping the caller's original values for keys that actionOperationData overrode. Apply the same change to endAction. Add a regression test in which the inner action changes selectedElement.

**Verifier.** I traced the scenario through the code and it holds.  1. In start-action.ts:31-46 the rest object `newOperationData` is rebuilt by mergeOperationData, which returns a fresh `{...a, ...b}`. That same reference is passed to `actionInstance.start(newOperationData)` at line 51, and line 56 later spreads it into the resolved value. 2. Action.start -> _runOperations -> executeOperation (action.ts:118) uses that object directly as `operationData`. Line 150 runs `Object.assign(operationData, configuredData)` on it, and sync operations such as selectElement and addClass mutate it and return it. So until an inner operation returns a different object, every write the inner action makes lands on the caller's `newOperationData`. 3. After the action finishes, startAction only deletes the keys of `actionOperationData` (lines 53-55) and resolves `{...newOperationData, actionInstance}`. The inner action's `selectedElement` (and any other key it wrote) therefore becomes the caller's operation data. If a caller key is also in actionOperationData, the caller's own value is deleted rather than restored. 4. end-action.ts:39-58 follows the same pattern: `newOperationData` is passed to `end()` and then filtered into sanitizedData.  I looked for a guard or invariant that prevents this and found none. Action does not copy `initOperationData`. copyConfiguredOperationData only protects the configured data, not the running object. The JSDoc says only that actionOperationData keys "are removed from the current operation data". It does not say that the inner action's outputs are meant to flow back.  Existing tests do not cover this. start-action.spec.ts uses a MockAction that never mutates. The integration test re-use-image-action.spec.ts gets a leaked `selectedElement=#picture1` from the first startAction, but it passes only because the second call's inner selectElement overwrites it.  I ran a probe with real Action, startAction and addClass. The caller selects #a, then runs startAction on an action that selects #b and adds 'hl', then runs addClass 'done'. The result was that #a did NOT get 'done': `expect($('#a').hasClass('done')).toBe(true)` failed. A valid configuration triggers this (requestAction + startAction followed by DOM operations that rely on the caller's earlier selectElement), and it silently targets the wrong DOM element. Medium severity is fair.

**Repro test.** `given a caller action that selects #a and then startAction on an action whose start operations select #b, when startAction resolves and the caller runs addClass 'done', then #a has class 'done' and #b does not`

### 20. [medium] setVariable inside a when/forEach block writes to a throwaway child scope

[set-variable.ts:29](src/operation/set-variable.ts#L29) · area: operations-b · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** Action._pushScope creates a child scope for every when and forEach, and that scope has no `variables` field. setVariable therefore creates a new `variables` object on the child scope. Reads of $scope.variables walk up to parent scopes, but writes do not, so any variable assigned inside an if-block or a loop disappears when endWhen or endForEach pops the scope. Inside a loop, the first write also starts shadowing the outer value.

**Failure scenario.** [setVariable {name:'flag', value:0}, when {expression:'1==1'}, setVariable {name:'flag', value:1}, endWhen, when {expression:'$scope.variables.flag==1'} ...]. After endWhen, scope.variables.flag is still 0 (confirmed in a probe), so the second when is false. Accumulator patterns inside a forEach have the same problem.

**Evidence.**

```ts
set-variable.ts:29-33: `if (!this.variables) { this.variables = {}; } this.variables[name] = value;` and action.ts:83-97, where _pushScope builds `{ get currentIndex(){...}, get eventbus(){...}, get operations(){...}, parent: parentScope }` with no variables.
```

**Fix.** Prefer the second option the auditor suggested. In Action._pushScope, add a `variables` getter and setter that forward to parentScope.variables, the same way currentIndex is forwarded. Then the whole action shares one variables store: writes inside when or forEach reach the root, and reads see every variable, both outer ones and ones set in the block.  Because the getter delegates, `this.variables` is undefined until the first write. setVariable's `if (!this.variables) this.variables = {}` then goes through the setter and creates the store on the root, so setVariable itself needs no change.  Also update the comment in getScopeChainValue (resolve-external-property-chain.ts:57-71), which documents the old write behaviour.  Add Action-level tests: - setVariable inside when is visible after endWhen. - An accumulator inside forEach keeps its value after endForEach. - An outer variable can still be read inside a block after the block sets a different variable.

Original suggestion: Make setVariable write to the scope that owns `variables`: walk `this` and its parents to the nearest scope that has `variables`, and fall back to the root scope. Alternatively, have _pushScope proxy `variables` to the parent with a getter and setter, as it already does for currentIndex. Add Action-level tests for setVariable inside when and forEach.

**Verifier.** I traced the scenario through the real code and it holds. I could not find a guard against it.  Writes: - Action.executeOperation (src/action/action.ts:155-164) calls _pushScope for every `when` and for the first pass of each `forEach`. - _pushScope (action.ts:79-100) builds a child scope. It proxies currentIndex, eventbus and operations to the parent and sets `parent`. It does not define `variables`. - setVariable (src/operation/set-variable.ts:29-33) runs with `this` set to that child scope. It finds no `variables`, creates `{}` on the child and writes to it. - endWhen, and endForEach on its last iteration, pop the child (action.ts:171-177), so the write is lost.  Reads: - getScopeChainValue (src/operation/helper/resolve-external-property-chain.ts:72-86) walks up to the nearest scope where the head property `variables` exists. - Its doc comment says that write semantics were deliberately left "untouched" and calls the result "lexical shadowing". That would be a defensible design if shadowing worked per variable name. - It does not. The head is `variables`, so once a block sets any variable, the child's whole `variables` object hides every outer variable. Inside that block, `$scope.variables.<outerName>` then resolves to undefined. This is a second symptom the auditor did not mention, and it cannot be intended. - Nothing in set-variable.ts, the when.ts docs or CLAUDE.md says that variables are block-scoped.  Tests: - src/test/unit/operation/set-variable.spec.ts only calls setVariable directly on a flat scope. - src/test/integration/for-each-loop.spec.ts:528 only covers reading a variable set outside the loop from inside it. - No test writes a variable inside a when or forEach.  Probe: I ran a temporary Vitest spec through the real Action with this sequence, then deleted it: - setVariable flag=0 and y='outer' - when 1==1, then setVariable flag=1 - a nested when on `$scope.variables.y=='outer'` - endWhen, endWhen - when on `$scope.variables.flag==1`  Results: - The final when was false, so flag was still 0. - The nested when was also false, so the outer y could not be read inside the block.  Severity: this is valid configuration that the docs advertise, such as the `$scope.variables.foo=='bar'` example in when.ts, used in common patterns: setting a flag inside an if, or an accumulator inside a loop. It fails silently, with no error. Medium is correct.

**Repro test.** `given a variable flag set to 0 before a when block and set to 1 inside it, when the action runs past endWhen, then a later when on '$scope.variables.flag==1' is true`

### 21. [medium] setData to $scope.variables.* throws inside when/forEach blocks

[set-data.ts:88](src/operation/set-data.ts#L88) · area: operations-b · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** setData resolves the write target with getPropertyChainValue against the current scope only. Inside a pushed when or forEach scope, `variables` is undefined because child scopes do not proxy it, while reads of the same chain succeed through getScopeChainValue's parent walk. The final assignment then throws a raw TypeError. Any other missing intermediate in a target chain produces the same TypeError, with no setData context.

**Failure scenario.** [setVariable {name:'sum', value:0}, forEach {collection:[1,2]}, setData {properties:{'$scope.variables.sum':'$scope.currentItem'}}, endForEach]. The action rejects with 'TypeError: Cannot set properties of undefined (setting 'sum')' (confirmed in a probe), even though '$scope.variables.sum' resolves fine as a read inside the same loop.

**Evidence.**

```ts
set-data.ts:88-94: `const propertyTarget = getPropertyChainValue(path, (targets as any)[rootTarget], 'setData') as Record<string, any>; propertyTarget[property] = propertyValue;`
```

**Fix.** In `resolveTargets`, for `rootTarget === 'scope'` with a non-empty `path`, pick the target scope the same way `getScopeChainValue` does: walk `scope`, then `scope.parent`, and so on until a scope has `path[0] in s`. If none has it, use the current scope. Then call `getPropertyChainValue(path, targetScope, 'setData')`. To avoid duplicating the walk, export a small helper from resolve-external-property-chain.ts, for example `findDeclaringScope(head, scope)`. After resolving, if `propertyTarget` is null or undefined or not an object, throw `new Error(`setData: cannot write '${item}': '${['$' + rootTarget, ...path].join('.')}' does not resolve to an object`)`. An empty `path` (for example `$scope.newIndex`) must still write to the current scope, so existing behaviour does not change.

Original suggestion: For the 'scope' target, resolve the head segment with the same parent walk as getScopeChainValue (the nearest scope that declares it). When propertyTarget is null, undefined or not an object, throw `setData: cannot write '${item}': '${path.join('.')}' does not resolve to an object`.

**Verifier.** I traced the scenario through the real code and it fails as described.  1. `setVariable` (src/operation/set-variable.ts) runs on the action's root scope and creates `rootScope.variables = {sum: 0}`. 2. When `forEach` runs, `Action.executeOperation` (src/action/action.ts:161) calls `_pushScope` (lines 79-100). The new child scope only has the `currentIndex`, `eventbus` and `operations` accessors plus `parent`. It does not have `variables`. Later loop iterations reuse this child scope (`isLoopIteration`). 3. `setData` with key `'$scope.variables.sum'` (set-data.ts:58-94): `path` becomes `['variables']` and `property` becomes `'sum'`. The value side resolves fine, because `resolveExternalPropertyChain` uses `getScopeChainValue`, which walks parent scopes. The target side calls `getPropertyChainValue(['variables'], childScope)`. Its reduce only throws when the object being indexed is null or undefined. Here that object is `childScope`, so the call returns `childScope.variables`, which is `undefined`, without an error. Line 94 then runs `undefined['sum'] = value` and throws a TypeError.  So reads use the parent walk and writes do not. The comment on `getScopeChainValue` says it "leaves write semantics untouched". That only describes `setVariable`, which writes to its own scope. It does not make `setData` throwing a TypeError intended behaviour.  I found nothing that prevents this. `assertSafePropertyChain` only rejects `__proto__`, `prototype` and `constructor`. No validation checks for this case. No test in src/test covers `setData` writing to `$scope.variables.*` inside `when` or `forEach`. The integration test in src/test/integration/for-each-loop.spec.ts:529-541 only covers reading `$scope.variables` across scopes.  One part of the report is overstated. The claim of "no setData context" is wrong after commit 68fe4110. `withOperationContext` in action.ts adds `operationSystemName` (setData) and `operationIndex` to the error, so the engine error event names the operation. The message text is still the raw "Cannot set properties of undefined".  Severity stays medium: - The configuration is valid and the pattern is natural (adding up a total in a loop, or setting a flag inside `when`). - No working write path exists. `setVariable` inside the loop writes to the child scope, and that value is gone after `endForEach` pops the scope. - The failure is loud, not silent corruption. The action rejects with an annotated error and the engine state stays intact.

**Repro test.** `given a variable set with setVariable before a forEach loop, when setData writes '$scope.variables.sum' inside the loop body, then the action resolves and the outer scope's variables.sum holds the last item instead of rejecting with a TypeError`

### 22. [medium] Scroll seek is ignored while inactive, so a switchTimeline start position is dropped

[scroll-position-source.ts:148](src/timelineproviders/position-sources/scroll-position-source.ts#L148) · area: timeline-providers-adapters · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** seek() returns the current position without doing anything when the state is 'inactive'. The engine seeks inactive sources in two places: switchTimeline(uri, position) seeks before activate(), and engine.seek() runs while stopped. In both cases it then sets _lastPosition and reconciles actions at the requested position. For scroll timelines the requested position is silently ignored, while the engine has already started the actions for it. On activation the real scroll offset is emitted and those actions are torn down again. A unit test pins the no-op, but the engine contract (CHANGELOG: switchTimeline 'honours the start position') does not hold for scroll sources.

**Failure scenario.** engine.switchTimeline('scroll-timeline', 30). The scroll source is inactive, so seek(30) returns 0. The engine sets _lastPosition = 30 and starts the actions covering 30. activate() then reads scrollTop 0, setPosition(0) dispatches backwards, and those actions end at once. The page is never scrolled to 30.

**Evidence.**

```ts
async seek(position: number): Promise<number> {
    if (!this._element || this.state === 'inactive') {
      return this.getPosition();
    }
```

**Fix.** In `ScrollPositionSource.seek`, keep only the `!this._element` early return. When `state !== 'active'`, call `this._element.scrollTo({top: targetScrollTop, behavior: 'instant'})` (or set `this._element.scrollTop = targetScrollTop`) so the offset is in place before `startTicking()` reads it on activate. Then update `_lastPosition`, `_wasAtStart` and `setPosition(clampedPosition)` as the current suspended branch does. Keep `behavior: 'smooth'` only for the active state. Rewrite the 'inactive seek does nothing' unit test to expect the instant scroll, and change the suspended-seek test to expect an instant scroll as well. Add an engine-level test that switches to a scroll timeline at position 30 and asserts the actions covering 30 are still active after `activate()`.

Original suggestion: Once _element exists, allow seeking in any state: scroll instantly to the target and update _lastPosition, _wasAtStart and the position, as the suspended branch does. Change the 'inactive seek does nothing' test to match.

**Verifier.** I traced the scenario through the current code and it holds. Nothing guards against it.  1. A scroll timeline can be reached from a valid configuration. `ScrollPositionSource` is a built-in `systemName` (src/configuration/types.ts:146-176), and the fluent editor maps `'scroll'` to it (timeline-provider-settings-editor.ts:16).  2. In `EligiusEngine._switchTimeline` (src/eligius-engine.ts:473-522), the previous source is deactivated and `_activateProvider` only calls `init()`. `BasePositionSource` starts as `'inactive'`, and `deactivate()` sets that state again, so the target source is always inactive when `_seekSource(source, target)` runs. `ScrollPositionSource.seek` (scroll-position-source.ts:147-150) therefore returns `getPosition()` without scrolling. The engine never looks at that return value: it sets `this._lastPosition = target` (30) and calls `_reconcileActions(uri, 30)`, so the actions covering 30 start.  3. Next, `source.activate()` calls `startTicking()` (lines 107-123). That reads the real `scrollTop` (0) and calls `setPosition(0)` while the source is active. `_seekDepth` is 0 by then, so `_onTimeHandler(0)` runs: `_lastPosition` (30) is not 0, and `_dispatchActionsBetween(30, 0)` does a backward reconcile that ends the actions just started. The page is never scrolled.  4. `engine.seek()` after `stop()` takes the same path. `stop()` deactivates the source, the seek does nothing on scroll while the engine reconciles at the target, and the following `start()` resyncs to the real `scrollTop`.  5. The RAF and keyboard sources call `setPosition(clamped)` in every state, and the video source seeks the player whatever its state. So scroll is the only source where the CHANGELOG line 57 claim ("switchTimeline ... honours the start position") is false.  The unit test 'given inactive source, when seek called, then returns current position without scrolling' (scroll-position-source.spec.ts:495) pins the source-level no-op. It does not protect the engine contract, and no engine test covers a scroll timeline switch.  The suggested fix is incomplete in one way. The existing suspended branch scrolls with `behavior: 'smooth'`, and `startTicking()` reads `scrollTop` synchronously on activate. A smooth scroll has not moved yet at that point, so `engine.seek()` while paused followed by `start()` has the same rollback problem. The fix should scroll instantly whenever the source is not active.  Severity stays medium. It needs a scroll timeline plus a switchTimeline position or a seek while stopped or paused, both valid uses, and it silently undoes actions the engine just started.

**Repro test.** `given initialised inactive scroll source, when seek(30) is called and the source is then activated, then scrollTop maps to 30 and the first emitted position is 30 instead of 0`

### 23. [medium] Keyboard steps accumulate float error, so fractional steps never reach the end

[keyboard-step-source.ts:335](src/timelineproviders/position-sources/keyboard-step-source.ts#L335) · area: timeline-providers-adapters · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** _stepForward adds stepSize to the previous float position without rounding. With fractional steps the position drifts, for example 0.30000000000000004 and 0.7999999999999999. The strict `newPosition === duration` check then never matches. With wrapNavigation on, the step that should land on the duration overshoots and wraps to 0. 'end' is never emitted, so timelineComplete never fires, and actions keyed at multiples of the step start one key press late. A large step near the end also wraps straight to 0, skipping the end.

**Failure scenario.** KeyboardStepSource {duration: 1, stepSize: 0.1}, wrap on (default). Ten right-arrow presses give 0.9999999999999999, and the eleventh gives 1.0999999999999999 > 1, which wraps to 0. 'end' is never emitted. An action starting at 0.8 starts on the 9th press, because the 8th press yields 0.7999999999999999.

**Evidence.**

```ts
const currentPosition = this.getPosition();
    const duration = this.getDuration();
    let newPosition = currentPosition + step;

    if (newPosition > duration) {
      if (this._wrapNavigation) {
        this.setPosition(0);
        this.emitBoundary('start');
        return;
      }
```

**Fix.** Round the computed position to millisecond precision in both `_stepForward` and `_stepBackward`: `let newPosition = Math.round((currentPosition + step) * 1000) / 1000;` (and the same with `- step`). Ten 0.1 steps then land exactly on 1. The existing `newPosition === duration` check emits 'end', and the 8th press yields exactly 0.8, so `upperBound` starts the action keyed at 0.8. Keep the documented overshoot-wraps-to-0 behaviour for large steps as it is, or change it in a separate change, because clamping to the end first changes documented semantics. Add specs with `stepSize: 0.1` covering 'end' emission and exact positions.

Original suggestion: Work in integer milliseconds as RafPositionSource does: `newPosition = Math.round((currentPosition + step) * 1000) / 1000` (same for _stepBackward). When currentPosition < duration and the step overshoots, clamp to the duration and emit 'end' first, and only wrap on the next press from the end. Add tests with stepSize 0.1.

**Verifier.** I traced the scenario through the real code and it holds.  1. `_stepForward` (keyboard-step-source.ts:332-355) computes `currentPosition + step` with no rounding. `_stepBackward` does the same. `BasePositionSource.setPosition` (base-position-source.ts:209) stores and emits the value unchanged, so nothing downstream normalises it. 2. `stepSize` can be set from configuration. `EngineFactory.createPositionSourceForTimeline` spreads every `positionSource` option except `systemName` into the constructor, so `{systemName: 'KeyboardStepSource', stepSize: 0.1}` reaches `_stepSize`. CLAUDE.md says actions are keyed at 0.1 s precision, so a 0.1 step is a natural choice. `wrapNavigation` defaults to true. 3. I ran `p = p + 0.1` eleven times in Node: 0.1, 0.2, 0.30000000000000004, 0.4, 0.5, 0.6, 0.7, 0.7999999999999999, 0.8999999999999999, 0.9999999999999999, 1.0999999999999999. The value never equals 1, so the strict `newPosition === duration` check (line 352) never fires. On the 11th press 1.0999... > 1, and with wrap on, line 337 sends the position to 0 and emits 'start'. 'end' is never emitted, so the engine's `onBoundaryReached` handler (eligius-engine.ts:629) never calls `_onCompleteCallback`, and 'timelineComplete' never fires. With wrap off, the 11th press clamps to 1 and emits 'end', one press late. 4. Action keys are not rounded either. `_createSchedule` stores the raw `action.duration.start` (0.8). `_dispatchActionsBetween` uses `upperBound(events, to)` with `events[i].position <= to`. Since 0.7999999999999999 < 0.8, the 8th press does not start an action keyed at 0.8. It starts on the 9th press. 5. Tests: keyboard-step-source.spec.ts only uses integer step sizes (the default 1 and `stepSize: 5`). Nothing covers fractional steps, so no existing test contradicts this.  What I refute: the claim that "a large step near the end also wraps straight to 0, skipping the end" is not a float bug. The method's JSDoc documents it ("Stepping past the end wraps to 0 and emits 'start' when wrapping is on"). It is a design choice and should be judged separately. The fix should not quietly change it.  Severity: medium is fair. A plausible, valid configuration (keyboard source with a 0.1 step) silently loses 'timelineComplete' and starts actions one key press late. Steps that are exact in binary (0.5, 0.25, integers) are not affected.

**Repro test.** `given KeyboardStepSource with duration 1 and stepSize 0.1, when right arrow is pressed ten times, then position is exactly 1 and the 'end' boundary is emitted`

### 24. [medium] A stale activate() rejection overwrites the state of a newer activation

[base-position-source.ts:130](src/timelineproviders/position-sources/base-position-source.ts#L130) · area: timeline-providers-adapters · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** activate() only checks `this._state !== 'active'` to decide whether its own failed start was superseded. Take the sequence activate (A) → suspend → activate (B) before A's play() promise settles. pause() rejects A's play() with AbortError, but the state is 'active' again (from B), so A reverts the state to its own previousState ('inactive') and rethrows. When B's play() then resolves, B sees the state is not 'active' and returns without firing onActivated. The video keeps playing while the source reports 'inactive'. _handleTimeUpdate drops every timeupdate, and engine.playState is 'stopped'.

**Failure scenario.** VideoPositionSource, stopped. An action broadcasts timeline-play-request, then timeline-pause-request, then timeline-play-request in the same task. The video ends up playing with state 'inactive', no timeline actions are dispatched, and the first engine.start() rejects with an AbortError that the adapter logs.

**Evidence.**

```ts
} catch (error) {
        if (this._state !== 'active') {
          // Superseded by suspend/deactivate/destroy while starting
          return;
        }
        this._state = previousState;
        throw error;
      }
```

**Fix.** Add a private `_activationToken = 0` to BasePositionSource. In activate(), set `const token = ++this._activationToken` before calling startTicking. Increment it in suspend(), deactivate() and destroy(). After `await started`, in both the catch path and the success path, check `token !== this._activationToken`. If the token is stale, return (or swallow the error) without touching state or firing callbacks. If it is current, keep the existing behaviour: revert to previousState and rethrow on failure, or fire onActivated on success. Add a regression test in base-position-source.spec.ts: activate, suspend, activate, then reject the first start and resolve the second. Expect state 'active', onActivated called once, and the first activate() to resolve rather than reject (it was superseded).

Original suggestion: Keep an activation token (an incrementing counter) per activate() call, and have suspend, deactivate and destroy bump it. A completion or rejection only touches state or fires callbacks if its token is still the current one. Add a test: activate → suspend → activate, where the first startTicking rejects after the second activate.

**Verifier.** I traced the reported sequence through the real code and found nothing that prevents it. In BasePositionSource.activate() (src/timelineproviders/position-sources/base-position-source.ts:110-143), previousState is captured before the call awaits. The catch block at 125-131 checks only `this._state !== 'active'` to decide whether a later call superseded this one. The sequence is activate (A), then suspend(), then activate (B), with A's start promise still pending. When A's promise rejects, B has already set the state back to 'active', so A reverts it to its own previousState ('inactive') and rethrows. When B's promise resolves, the check at line 134 sees 'inactive' and returns without firing onActivated. B's activate() resolves normally, so EligiusEngine.start() (src/eligius-engine.ts:358-369) still emits 'start'.  Nothing elsewhere stops this: - engine.start() calls source.activate() synchronously unless a stop cleanup is pending. - engine.pause() calls suspend() synchronously. - VideoPositionSource.startTicking (video-position-source.ts:217) returns player.play(), and stopTicking calls pause(). - Under the HTML spec, pause() rejects pending play promises with AbortError in a queued task. That task runs after the second play() call, even when the three requests are separated only by microtasks or by fast user clicks while the video is loading. - After that, _handleTimeUpdate (line 437) drops every timeupdate because the state is not 'active', and playState maps 'inactive' to 'stopped'.  The existing tests only cover suspend-while-pending (base-position-source.spec.ts:420) and a plain rejection. Neither covers re-activating before the stale promise settles.  I reproduced it with a temporary Vitest spec that I then deleted. The steps were activate, suspend, activate, reject the first start, then resolve the second. The result was state 'inactive', onActivated called 0 times, and the first activate rejected with AbortError.  Severity stays medium. It needs a play/pause/play (or play/stop/play) sequence before the first play() settles. A quick double-click on a play/pause control while a video loads produces that, and the result is a video playing while the engine dispatches no actions. Only async-start sources such as VideoPositionSource are affected; the RAF, scroll and keyboard sources start synchronously.

**Repro test.** `given activate then suspend then activate while the first startTicking is pending, when the first start rejects with AbortError and the second resolves, then state is active and onActivated is called once`

### 25. [medium] VideoPositionSource init hangs when 'sources' is missing; 'poster' is ignored

[video-position-source.ts:346](src/timelineproviders/position-sources/video-position-source.ts#L346) · area: timeline-providers-adapters · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** Only `sources` and the nested `options` reach video.js. IPositionSourceConfig documents `poster` as a video option, and the fluent `setPositionSourceType('video', {selector, poster})` accepts neither `sources` nor `options`. With no sources the HTML video element never fires 'canplay' or 'error', so `_pendingInit` never settles and engine.init() hangs. That is the silent hang the CHANGELOG says was removed. A top-level `poster` is dropped. If the error path ever runs with no sources, `this._config.sources.map` throws a TypeError.

**Failure scenario.** factory.addTimelineProviderSettings(...).setPositionSourceType('video', {selector: '#player', poster: '/p.jpg'}). VideoPositionSource gets {selector, poster, duration} and _createPlayer passes sources: undefined. player.load() emits nothing, so engine.init() never resolves or rejects, and the poster is never shown.

**Evidence.**

```ts
preload: 'auto',
        loop: this.loop,
        sources: this._config.sources,
        ...this._config.options,
```

**Fix.** In VideoPositionSource.doInit, before creating the element, reject straight away when `sources` is not a non-empty array. Use a named error, for example "VideoPositionSource: 'sources' is required (an array of {src, type})". Pass a top-level `poster` into the player options (`poster: this._config.poster`) and add `poster?: string` to VideoPositionSourceConfig. Add `sources` (as VideoSource[]) to the documented IPositionSourceConfig and fix its video example, then regenerate the schema. Add `sources` to the options type of the fluent `setPositionSourceType('video', ...)`. In the error handler, guard with `(this._config.sources ?? [])`.

Original suggestion: In doInit, reject straight away with a named error when `sources` is missing or empty. Map documented top-level options such as `poster` into the player options (or make VideoPositionSourceConfig list them and spread the remaining keys). Have the fluent video shorthand require `sources`.

**Verifier.** I traced the failure through the real code and could not find a guard against it.  1) Config path. EngineFactory.createPositionSourceForTimeline (src/engine-factory.ts ~360) strips `systemName` and passes `{...options, duration}` straight to `new VideoPositionSource(...)`. It does not check that `sources` is present, and does not rename or map any keys.  2) What the documentation says to write. IPositionSourceConfig (src/configuration/types.ts ~140-200) and the published jsonschema/eligius-configuration.json give a VideoPositionSource example containing only `{systemName, selector, poster}`. `sources` is not a declared property. It is accepted only through the `[key: string]: unknown` index signature. The fluent `setPositionSourceType('video', {selector, tickInterval, poster})` (src/configuration/api/timeline-provider-settings-editor.ts ~119) cannot express `sources` at all. Only `setPositionSourceByName` or `setPositionSource` can. So the schema and the documented example accept a video config with no sources.  3) What happens in VideoPositionSource with no sources. doInit (video-position-source.ts ~149) only rejects when the selector matches nothing. Otherwise it stores `_pendingInit` and calls `_createPlayer`. `_createPlayer` passes `sources: undefined` and spreads `this._config.options`, which is undefined. The top-level `poster` is never read, so video.js does not get the poster. In the ready callback, `_settleInit` is only called from the one-time 'canplay' and 'error' listeners, after `player.load()`. A video element with no src and no source children fires neither event. Under the HTML resource selection algorithm, networkState goes to NETWORK_EMPTY and selection aborts silently. video.js does not raise an error when no source was ever set; it only raises MEDIA_ERR_SRC_NOT_SUPPORTED after src() is called with sources it cannot play. Nothing else settles the promise: there is no timeout, and the only other path is `doDestroy`. EligiusEngine awaits `source.init()` (src/eligius-engine.ts ~645), so `engine.init()` stays pending forever. That contradicts the CHANGELOG claim that `init()` no longer hangs when a video fails to load.  4) Tests. Every test in src/test/unit/timelineproviders/position-sources/video-position-source.spec.ts passes `sources`. No test covers missing sources or `poster`.  5) Side claim about `sources.map` throwing a TypeError on the error path. This is technically true, but it is close to unreachable, because with no sources the 'error' event does not fire. It is minor.  Severity. A config that follows the documented example passes schema validation and then hangs init silently, with no error. That breaks the fail-fast rule. Medium fits. A video timeline with no sources cannot work anyway, so the defect is the silent hang and the misleading docs, not lost functionality.

**Repro test.** `given a VideoPositionSource config with selector and poster but no sources, when init() is called, then init() rejects with an error naming the missing 'sources' instead of staying pending`

### 26. [medium] Schema maps union @type tags to 'string', rejecting valid forEach/setVariable/log data

[generate-schemas.ts:359](src/tools/schema-tools/generate-schemas.ts#L359) · area: configuration-tools · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** For a tag such as `@type=ParameterType:array|ParameterType:string`, the generator strips only the first prefix. It looks up 'array|ParameterType:string' in typeMap, misses, and defaults to 'string'. The generated schema then sets type 'string' next to the anyOf that ts-json-schema-generator produced, so arrays are rejected. setVariable.value and log.logValue get type 'string' for `any` values.

**Failure scenario.** {systemName:'forEach', operationData:{collection:[1,2,3]}} fails validation against eligius-configuration.json because collection must be a string. {systemName:'setVariable', operationData:{name:'n', value:42}} also fails. Both are valid at runtime, and editors using the published schema flag them as errors.

**Evidence.**

```ts
const paramType = schemaType.replace('= ParameterType:', '');
      processedProp.type = mapParameterTypeToJsonSchema(paramType);
...
  return typeMap[paramType] || 'string';
--- generated jsonschema/operations/for-each.json
"collection":{"type":"string","anyOf":[{"type":"array","items":{}},{"type":"string"}]}
```

**Fix.** In generate-schemas.ts, do not emit `type` from the @type tag when ts-json-schema-generator already produced `anyOf` or `oneOf`, because the TS type is the source of truth. Otherwise:  1. Split the tag value (after '= ') on '|'. 2. Strip 'ParameterType:' from each part and map it with `mapParameterTypeToJsonSchema`. 3. Dedupe the results. 4. Emit a single string when there is one type and a `type` array when there are several. 5. If the union maps to every JSON type, or a part is unknown, emit no `type` at all instead of falling back to 'string'. This covers `value: any` and `logValue: any`.  Then regenerate the schemas. Add generate-schemas.spec cases that load operations/for-each.json, set-variable.json and log.json and validate them with ajv against sample data: an array collection, a numeric value, and an object logValue.

Original suggestion: Split the tag on '|', strip 'ParameterType:' from each part and map each one. Emit a `type` array, or omit `type` when ts-json-schema-generator already produced anyOf/oneOf. Emit no type for 'any'-like unions. Add generate-schemas.spec cases for forEach, setVariable and log.

**Verifier.** I traced the failure through the real code and found nothing that prevents it.  1. src/operation/for-each.ts tags `collection` with `@type=ParameterType:array|ParameterType:string`. Its TS type is `unknown[] | string`, and at runtime an array literal is used as-is while a string is resolved as a property chain. set-variable.ts (`value`) and log.ts (`logValue`) carry the five-way union `object|string|number|boolean|array`.  2. In generate-schemas.ts lines 353-360, `schemaType.replace('= ParameterType:', '')` removes only the first prefix. That leaves `array|ParameterType:string`. `mapParameterTypeToJsonSchema` (lines 475-510) has no key for it and returns the fallback `'string'`. Lines 370-371 then copy the anyOf from ts-json-schema-generator next to it. JSON Schema applies `type` and `anyOf` together, so `type: 'string'` wins and arrays fail.  3. The generated files on disk match this. jsonschema/operations/for-each.json has `collection: {type:'string', anyOf:[{type:'array'},{type:'string'}]}`. set-variable.json has `value: {type:'string'}` and log.json has `logValue: {type:'string'}`, with no anyOf at all.  4. These files are not orphaned. Lines 448-462 build the IOperationConfiguration definition in eligius-configuration.json as a `oneOf` of `$ref: operations/<file>`. An invalid forEach branch therefore makes the whole operation entry fail.  5. I compiled the three generated schemas with the repo's ajv and ran the examples:    - forEach `{collection:[1,2,3]}` fails with "must be string" at /operationData/collection/type.    - setVariable `{name:'n', value:42}` fails at /operationData/value/type.    - log `{logValue:{a:1}}` fails at /operationData/logValue/type.  6. No test covers this. src/test/unit/tools/schema-tools/generate-schemas.spec.ts only checks file presence, the oneOf references, and the endWhen, clearStorage, setOperationData, addClass and dependency-only cases. The generator exports nothing, so mapParameterTypeToJsonSchema is not unit-tested.  Severity: medium is fair. The engine does not validate against this schema at runtime, so the configurations still run. However, the schema is published and used by editors and by the Eligian tooling, and it rejects configurations that are valid and common. Looping over a literal array, or setting a variable to a number, is ordinary usage.

**Repro test.** `given the generated forEach operation schema, when validating operationData {collection: [1, 2, 3]} with ajv, then validation passes`

### 27. [medium] _prepareSource marks a source prepared before init succeeds; failed init is never retried

[eligius-engine.ts:620](src/eligius-engine.ts#L620) · area: lens-lifecycle-memory · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** The source is added to _preparedSources and its callbacks are registered before `await source.init()`. When init rejects (video load error, scroll element not found), later activations skip init, and the engine runs an uninitialised source that reports 'playing' while nothing happens.

**Failure scenario.** switchTimeline('video') rejects because the video fails to load on a network blip. The user switches to 'intro' and then back to 'video'. _prepareSource returns early, VideoPositionSource.activate() resolves with _player === null, playState becomes 'playing' and 'start'/'timeline-play' are emitted, but no video exists and no error is reported.

**Evidence.**

```ts
`if (this._preparedSources.has(source)) { return; } this._preparedSources.add(source); ... await source.init();`  video-position-source.ts startTicking: `await this._player?.play();`
```

**Fix.** In `_prepareSource`, register the callbacks once, tracked in a separate `_wiredSources` set or guarded by an existing check. Then await `source.init()` and add the source to `_preparedSources` only after it resolves. On a rejection, leave the source out of the set so it is not cached as prepared. Do the same in `_prepareContainer`.  In `_switchTimeline`, keep `previousUri`, `previousSource`, and the previous container provider and playlist. Wrap `_activateProvider` in try/catch. Commit `_currentTimelineUri` and emit `timelineChange` only after it succeeds. On failure, restore `_activePositionSource`, `_activeContainerProvider`, `_activePlaylist` and `_currentTimelineUri` to the previous values, then rethrow, so that a later `switchTimeline` with the same URI retries instead of doing nothing.  Note that the previous timeline's actions were already ended by `_cleanUpTimeline`. Either reconcile them again at `_lastPosition` on rollback, or document that a failed switch leaves the engine stopped on the previous timeline.

Original suggestion: Add the source to _preparedSources only after init() resolves (register the callbacks once in a separate set), or delete it from the set in a catch before rethrowing, so the next activation retries init.

**Verifier.** I traced this through the current src/eligius-engine.ts and the failure scenario holds.  1. `_switchTimeline` (lines 473-520) first deactivates `previousSource` and runs `_cleanUpTimeline(previousUri)`. It then sets `_currentTimelineUri = timelineConfig.uri` and emits `timelineChange`, and only after that awaits `_activateProvider`. 2. `_activateProvider` (604-614) sets `_activePositionSource` to the new source before it awaits `_prepareSource`. 3. `_prepareSource` (616-646) adds the source to `_preparedSources` on line 620, before it registers callbacks and before `await source.init()`. Nothing removes it if `init()` rejects. `_prepareContainer` follows the same pattern (line 659).  `VideoPositionSource.doInit` can reject from a valid configuration at runtime: - the container selector is not found; - video.js fails to import (it is now an optional, lazily loaded peer); - the player fails to create; - the player emits 'error' while loading media (line 369).  `BasePositionSource.init` passes the rejection straight through.  After a failed `switchTimeline('video')`, the engine state is: - `currentTimelineUri` is 'video'; - `timelineChange('video')` has already been emitted; - the active source is the video source, which never initialised; - the previous source is deactivated and its actions have ended.  A retry with `switchTimeline('video')` gets past the `previousSource` guard, because the uninitialised source is truthy. It then hits `this._currentTimelineUri === timelineConfig.uri` on line 480 and returns. The promise resolves, so the caller is told the switch succeeded when nothing happened.  `init()` can't be retried either. It resets `_currentTimelineUri` itself, but `_prepareSource` sees the source already in the set, skips `source.init()`, and runs the init actions against an uninitialised source.  I found no guard elsewhere and no test for a failed switch or a failed source init: grep over src/test finds no rejected `switchTimeline` or source-init cases.  This breaks the CLAUDE.md rule that errors must not corrupt engine state. Two things keep it at medium rather than higher. It only happens after a runtime failure, not on the normal path. And the user can recover partly by switching to a different timeline: the old source is already prepared, and deactivating the broken video source is harmless.  One addition to the proposed fix: if the source is only added to the set after `init()` resolves, the callbacks need to be registered once, separately. Otherwise a retry would register `onPosition`, `onBoundaryReached` and `onActivated` a second time and every tick would fire twice.

**Repro test.** `given a video timeline whose position source init rejects, when switchTimeline to it fails and switchTimeline to the same uri is called again, then source.init is called a second time and currentTimelineUri is not left as the failed uri after the first failure`

### 28. [medium] validateForm: required checkbox always passes; multi-select throws TypeError

[validate-form.ts:90](src/operation/validate-form.ts#L90) · area: operations-b · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** The value is read with $input.val(), which returns a checkbox's or radio's value attribute ('on') whether or not it is checked, and returns an array for <select multiple>. The required, minLength and maxLength rules then run on that value.

**Failure scenario.** Form with an unchecked <input type=checkbox name=terms> and rules {terms:{required:true}} gives isValid true and errors {} (confirmed). A form with <select multiple name=tags> and rules {tags:{required:true}} throws 'value.trim is not a function' (confirmed).

**Evidence.**

```ts
validate-form.ts:90 `const value = $input.val() as string;` and :106 `if (rules.required && (!value || value.trim() === '')) {`
```

**Fix.** Check checkbox and radio fields once per name, not once per input. If `rules.required` is set, the field counts as filled when `selectedElement.find(`input[name="${CSS.escape(name)}"]:checked`).length > 0`, and the string rules (minLength, maxLength, pattern, email) are skipped for these types. For any other control, read `const raw = $input.val()`. If `Array.isArray(raw)`, required means `raw.length > 0`, minLength and maxLength compare against the number of selected items, and pattern and email run on each item. Otherwise use `const value = raw == null ? '' : String(raw)` and apply the existing string rules. Track the names already handled so each radio or checkbox group is validated once and later group members cannot overwrite its result.

Original suggestion: For checkbox and radio inputs, treat the field as filled only when some input with that name is checked. For arrays, use the length. Only call trim and length checks on strings.

**Verifier.** I traced the code and found no guard. In src/operation/validate-form.ts, every named input, select and textarea goes through `const value = $input.val() as string` at line 90. The `as string` cast only satisfies the compiler and does not change the value at runtime.  The project uses jQuery 4.0.0 (package.json, node_modules). In that version `.val()` on a checkbox or radio returns its `value` property. When the input has no value attribute, that is the default 'on', whether or not the box is checked. On a `<select multiple>` it returns an array, which is `[]` when nothing is selected.  The only check that runs first is the HTML5 `checkValidity()` at line 98. It catches an unchecked checkbox only if the markup itself has a `required` attribute, and the custom `rules.required` exists exactly so that attribute is not needed. Line 106 tests `!value || value.trim() === ''`. For 'on' that is false, so the required rule passes. For `[]`, `![]` is false, so the code goes on to call `[].trim()`, which throws a TypeError. The same throw happens when options are selected, and the minLength and maxLength checks read `.length` on the wrong type in both cases.  No caller normalizes the value first, and validate-form.spec.ts has no checkbox, radio or multiple-select cases. I ran a temporary vitest probe in the real jsdom setup and then deleted it. An unchecked `<input type=checkbox name=terms>` with `{terms:{required:true}}` returned `isValid === true`. A `<select multiple name=tags>` with `{tags:{required:true}}` threw /trim is not a function/. Both scenarios hold.  Severity: a plain, valid configuration triggers this, using the documented validationRules API on ordinary form markup. The checkbox case silently passes something like a required "accept terms" box. Since 68fe411, the throw is reported through the engine error path instead of corrupting state, and adding a native `required` attribute avoids the checkbox case. I rate it medium rather than low, because a silent false pass on a required field is a real correctness defect that users will hit.

**Repro test.** `given a form with an unchecked checkbox named terms and validationRules {terms: {required: true}}, when validateForm is called, then isValid is false and validationErrors.terms is 'This field is required' (fails today: isValid true, errors {}); and given a form with a select multiple named tags with no selection and validationRules {tags: {required: true}}, when validateForm is called, then it does not throw and validationErrors.tags is 'This field is required' (fails today: throws 'value.trim is not a function')`

### 29. [low] forEach re-resolves a property-chain collection every iteration while loopLength is fixed

[for-each.ts:36](src/operation/for-each.ts#L36) · area: operations-a · reported severity: medium · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** When `collection` is a `$operationdata.`/`$globaldata.`/`$scope.` chain, forEach resolves it again on every iteration, but `loopLength` is set only on the first one. If the loop body changes or removes the source, later iterations index into a different array, or get no array at all. The loop still runs the original number of times and `currentItem` is silently undefined. I confirmed this with a probe run through `Action`: collection `$operationdata.items` = [1,2,3] and a body that removes `items` gave currentItem values [1, undefined, undefined].
