**Verifier.** I traced it through the real code and ran a throwaway probe test, which passed. The probe file is deleted. save-to-storage.ts:59-65 only checks `key`. Nothing at runtime enforces the `@required` tag on `value`: ConfigurationResolver does not check required properties, and the engine does not validate against the JSON schema. A schema-valid configuration can still produce an undefined value. For example, `value: '$operationdata.missing'` passes the schema because it is a string, and resolvePropertyValues -> resolveExternalPropertyChain -> getPropertyChainValue returns undefined for a missing leaf without throwing (it only throws on null or undefined intermediates). JSON.stringify(undefined) is undefined, and Storage.setItem turns it into the string 'undefined'. I checked `localStorage.getItem('k') === 'undefined'` in jsdom. A later loadFromStorage with the same key throws 'loadFromStorage: failed to parse stored value for key "k"', which I also checked. In both operations, `storageType === 'session' ? sessionStorage : localStorage` means 'Session' or 'sesion' writes to localStorage without any error, which I checked as well. The JSON schema enum would reject a typo, but only if the author validates the configuration. The engine does not. The spec 'should default to localStorage when storageType not specified' documents a fallback when storageType is missing, but the field is tagged `@required`, so that is a lenient default rather than a guard. No existing test covers an undefined value or an invalid storageType. Severity stays low: the user only gets a clear error later at load time, or data lands in the wrong storage. Nothing crashes, and engine state is not corrupted.

**Repro test.** `given saveToStorage with value resolved from a missing $operationdata chain, when it runs and loadFromStorage reads the same key, then saveToStorage throws 'value is required' instead of storing 'undefined' that loadFromStorage cannot parse`

### 65. [low] KeyboardStepSource takes over the global hotkeys-js scope and resets it to 'all'

[keyboard-step-source.ts:249](src/timelineproviders/position-sources/keyboard-step-source.ts#L249) · area: timeline-providers-adapters · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** activate() calls hotkeys.setScope(scopeId) on the shared hotkeys-js singleton, a regular dependency that npm dedupes with the host's copy. While the timeline is active, any host handlers bound in a custom scope stop firing. On suspend or deactivate, deleteScope resets the current scope to 'all' instead of restoring the previous one, so the host's scope is lost for good.

**Failure scenario.** The host app uses hotkeys-js with setScope('editor') and has 'ctrl+s' bound in that scope. After an Eligius keyboard timeline starts, ctrl+s does nothing. After the timeline stops, the scope is 'all' and the 'editor' handlers stay dead until the host calls setScope again.

**Evidence.**

```ts
// Set the scope as active so handlers trigger
    hotkeys.setScope(this._scopeId);
```

**Fix.** Prefer not touching the global scope at all. Bind the handlers in the 'all' scope (or keep the unique scope only as a tag for removal), and start each handler with `if (this.state !== 'active') return;`. In `_unbindKeys`, unbind each key from that scope by its exact method reference instead of calling `deleteScope`.  If the dedicated scope must stay, then: - In `_bindKeys`, save `this._previousScope = hotkeys.getScope()` before `setScope`. - In `_unbindKeys`, call `hotkeys.deleteScope(this._scopeId, this._previousScope ?? 'all')`.  Note: `deleteScope` only switches the scope when the current scope is still this source's scope, so it will not clobber a scope the host changed in the meantime.  Even with this fix, the host's custom-scope handlers stay dead while the source is active, which is why the first option is better.

Original suggestion: Remember hotkeys.getScope() before setScope and restore it in _unbindKeys (deleteScope(scopeId, previousScope)). Alternatively, bind in the 'all' scope and gate the handlers on `this.state === 'active'` instead of changing the global scope.

**Verifier.** I traced the reported behaviour through the real code and the installed hotkeys-js 4.0.8 (node_modules/hotkeys-js/dist/hotkeys-js.js), and it holds.  How it happens: - `KeyboardStepSource._bindKeys()` (line 249) calls `hotkeys.setScope(this._scopeId)`. That sets the library's module-level `_scope` variable (lines 177-183). - When a key fires, `dispatch` reads `getScope()`. `eventHandler` (line 332) then runs a handler only if `handler.scope === scope || handler.scope === 'all'`. - So while the source is active, a host handler bound in a custom scope such as 'editor' does not fire. Handlers in the 'all' scope still fire. - `_unbindKeys()` calls `hotkeys.deleteScope(this._scopeId)` without a second argument. `deleteScope` (line 252) then runs `if (getScope() === scope) setScope(newScope || 'all')`, so the current scope becomes 'all' and the host's previous scope is never restored. - Nothing in the class saves the earlier scope (`getScope` is never called).  Why the host's copy is the one affected: - hotkeys-js is a regular dependency (exact version 4.0.8). tsdown externalizes dependencies, and dist/index.mjs line 3 has `import hotkeys from "hotkeys-js"`. - When the host's version range allows 4.0.8, npm dedupes to one shared instance, so this global state is the host's.  Guards I looked for and did not find: - `EngineInputAdapter` deliberately binds 'space' in the 'all' scope and unbinds it from 'all'. This shows the authors know about the scope interaction, but it does nothing to protect the host's scope. - keyboard-step-source.spec.ts mocks setScope, getScope and deleteScope with an in-memory scope. No test checks that a scope set beforehand is restored.  Why the severity stays low: - `EngineFactory` and the configuration types never build a `KeyboardStepSource`. It is only exported from timelineproviders/index.ts for programmatic use, so no JSON configuration can trigger this. - It also needs a host that uses hotkeys-js scopes with a version that dedupes to 4.0.8. The failure is a lost host key binding: no crash and no damage to engine state.

**Repro test.** `given host set hotkeys scope to 'editor', when KeyboardStepSource is activated and then deactivated, then hotkeys.getScope() returns 'editor'`

### 66. [low] A throwing position callback stops the RAF clock while the state stays 'active'

[base-position-source.ts:213](src/timelineproviders/position-sources/base-position-source.ts#L213) · area: timeline-providers-adapters · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** setPosition calls the callbacks with no isolation. In RafPositionSource the call chain is animationInterval frame → _onTick → setPosition. animationInterval calls `callback(time - start)` before `scheduleFrame(time)`, so one exception means no further frame is ever scheduled. The source still reports 'active', so playState stays 'playing' while time has stopped. The engine's own callback is safe because its emitter catches, but BasePositionSource and RafPositionSource are public exports used on their own.

**Failure scenario.** A consumer registers source.onPosition(p => { if (p === 5) throw new Error('x'); }) on a RafPositionSource. At 5 s the exception escapes the RAF frame, no next frame is scheduled, the position stays at 5 forever and state is still 'active'.

**Evidence.**

```ts
protected setPosition(position: number): void {
    this._position = position;
    if (this._state === 'active') {
      for (const callback of this._positionCallbacks) {
        callback(position);
      }
    }
  }
```

**Fix.** Prefer isolating each callback in `BasePositionSource`, using the same pattern as `TypedEventEmitter`. In `setPosition`, `emitBoundary` and the activated loop in `activate()`, wrap each callback in `try { callback(...) } catch (error) { console.error('Error in position source callback:', error); }`. Changing only `animationInterval` (scheduling the next frame first, or using try/finally) is not enough on its own. If a callback throws inside `_onTick`'s end-boundary branch, it skips `emitBoundary('end')` and `deactivate()`. The next tick then re-enters the same branch and throws again on every frame. With per-callback isolation, the remaining callbacks and the state transitions still run. As a separate defensive step, `animationInterval`'s `frame` could also run `scheduleFrame` in a `finally` block.

Original suggestion: Wrap each callback in try/catch in setPosition, emitBoundary and the activated loop, and report errors with console.error (or an onError hook), as TypedEventEmitter does. Alternatively, have animationInterval schedule the next frame before calling the callback.

**Verifier.** I traced the scenario through the code and it holds.  1. `src/util/animation-interval.ts` `frame()` runs `if (signal.aborted) return; callback(time - start); scheduleFrame(time);`. There is no try/finally, so if the callback throws, `scheduleFrame` is never called and no further `setTimeout`/`requestAnimationFrame` is queued. 2. In `src/timelineproviders/position-sources/raf-position-source.ts`, `_startSession` passes `this._onTick.bind(this)` as that callback. `_onTick` calls `this.setPosition(...)` with no catch, in the normal branch and also in the end-boundary and loop branches. 3. `BasePositionSource.setPosition` (lines 209-216) calls every `_positionCallbacks` entry directly. `emitBoundary` (223-227) and the activated loop in `activate()` (140-142) do the same. None of them catch. 4. Nothing changes `_state` on that path, so `state` stays 'active' and `getPosition()` stays at the last value set. The clock has stopped but the source still reports that it is running. 5. `BasePositionSource` and `RafPositionSource` are public. They are exported from `src/timelineproviders/index.ts`, which `src/index.ts` re-exports with `export *`. `onPosition` is a public method and its JSDoc example shows consumers registering callbacks.  What limits the impact: - The engine's own callbacks (`eligius-engine.ts` `_prepareSource`, lines 624-643) go through `_onTimeHandler`, then `_dispatchActionsBetween` (which reports failures through the `error` event and awaits nothing), then `_emitter.emit`. `TypedEventEmitter.emit` catches each handler (`typed-event-emitter.ts` line 155). So a valid JSON configuration run through `EngineFactory` cannot trigger this. - It needs a consumer who uses a position source directly, or attaches an extra `onPosition` listener to one, and has a listener that throws.  Tests: the RAF spec mocks `animationInterval`, and `animation-interval.spec.ts` only checks elapsed values. No test covers a throwing callback. A grep for 'throw' in the position-source specs finds only video.js init errors.  I found nothing that refutes it: no guard, no documented "callbacks must not throw" contract, and the project's own `TypedEventEmitter` isolates handlers. It is a real robustness gap, but only reachable through direct API use. Severity stays low.

**Repro test.** `given an active RafPositionSource driven by the real animationInterval with an onPosition callback that throws at the first tick, when further animation frames elapse, then getPosition() keeps advancing and the other registered position callbacks keep receiving updates`

### 67. [low] Timeline action without duration crashes resolver with a pathless TypeError

[configuration-resolver.ts:305](src/configuration/configuration-resolver.ts#L305) · area: configuration-tools · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** resolveTimelineAction dereferences actionConfiguration.duration.end without a check. The fluent API makes this easy to hit: createTimelineAction adds the action to the timeline before addDuration is called, and nothing requires addDuration.

**Failure scenario.** factory.createTimelineAction('main','Intro').addStartOperation('selectElement',{selector:'#a'}) with addDuration forgotten. createEngine then throws "TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'end')" and the message does not name timelines[0].timelineActions[0].duration.

**Evidence.**

```ts
const duration = {
    end: actionConfiguration.duration.end ?? -1,
    start: actionConfiguration.duration.start,
  };
```

**Fix.** In resolveTimelineAction, before reading duration: `const d = actionConfiguration.duration; if (!isObject(d) || typeof d.start !== 'number') throw new Error(`Missing or invalid duration at ${path}.duration: expected {start: number, end?: number}`); if (typeof d.end === 'number' && d.end < d.start) throw new Error(`${path}.duration.end (${d.end}) must be >= start (${d.start})`);`. In TimelineActionCreator.addDuration, also change `if (end)` to `if (end !== undefined)` so that end=0 is kept.

Original suggestion: In resolveTimelineAction, throw `Missing duration at ${path}.duration` when duration or duration.start is not a number, and `${path}.duration.end must be >= start` for an inverted range. Optionally make createTimelineAction take start/end.

**Verifier.** I traced the scenario through the code and could not refute it. ActionCreatorFactory.createTimelineAction (src/configuration/api/action-creator-factory.ts:75-79) builds a TimelineActionCreator and immediately calls configurationfactory.addTimelineAction(uri, creator.actionConfig). The ActionCreator constructor (lines ~107-117) sets only name, id and startOperations, then casts the object `as unknown as T`. That cast hides the required `duration: IDuration` from ITimelineActionConfiguration (types.ts:431-433) from the compiler. The only thing that ever sets duration is TimelineActionCreator.addDuration (lines ~296-303), and nothing requires calling it. getConfiguration just deep-copies the config. EngineFactory.createEngine (engine-factory.ts:191-193) then calls ConfigurationResolver.process. That method has no schema validation; its only structural check is timelineProviderSettings. It reaches resolveTimelines -> resolveTimelineAction (configuration-resolver.ts:182-187, 293-307). resolveEndableActionConfiguration succeeds first, and then `actionConfiguration.duration.end` throws a plain TypeError that never mentions the path `timelines[i].timelineActions[j]`, even though that path is passed in as `path`. I found no guard anywhere in the chain and no test for a missing duration: configuration-resolver.spec.ts only uses configs that include a duration. Why the severity stays low: a schema-valid JSON config or a correctly typed hand-written config always has a duration, so only a fluent-API misuse (or invalid JSON) can trigger this. The result is a bad error message, not corrupted state. It still breaks the CLAUDE.md rule that "Invalid configuration must fail fast with a message naming the JSON path". A related nit in the same area: addDuration uses `if (end)`, so addDuration(0, 0) drops end=0 and the resolver turns it into -1.

**Repro test.** `given a timeline action configuration without a duration, when ConfigurationResolver.process is called, then it throws an error naming timelines[0].timelineActions[0].duration`

### 68. [low] Event actions registered before duplicate-name check stay live when process() throws

[configuration-resolver.ts:169](src/configuration/configuration-resolver.ts#L169) · area: configuration-tools · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** resolveEventActions calls actionRegistryListener.registerAction for every event action, and only afterwards does createActionsLookup throw on a duplicate name. EngineFactory registered that listener on the shared eventbus before calling process. When createEngine throws, the listener and its event actions remain subscribed.

**Failure scenario.** Config: action 'Show' plus eventAction 'Show' on eventName 'lang-changed'. createEngine throws 'Duplicate action name'. The caller fixes the config and creates a new engine on the same factory. Broadcasting 'lang-changed' now also runs the stale event action from the rejected configuration.

**Evidence.**

```ts
actionRegistryListener.registerAction(eventAction, eventName, eventTopic);
    return eventAction;
  });
...
    const actionsLookup = createActionsLookup([
      ...(resolvedConfig.actions as IAction[]).map(
```

**Fix.** Primary fix in EngineFactory.createEngine: wrap everything from the listener and interceptor registration through the adapter connects in try/catch. On failure, call every remover in engineRemovers, drop them from this._eventRemovers, and rethrow. This covers the duplicate-name throw and every other throw after registration (resolver, timeline provider creation, engine constructor). Optionally, also change ConfigurationResolver.process so that it builds the event Actions, runs createActionsLookup, and only then calls actionRegistryListener.registerAction for each one. That way the resolver never mutates the listener on a configuration it rejects.

Original suggestion: Build the event Action instances and run createActionsLookup (and any other validation) first. Register them with actionRegistryListener only after validation passes. Separately, EngineFactory should remove its listener and interceptor when process throws.

**Verifier.** I traced the scenario through the code and it holds. In EngineFactory.createEngine (src/engine-factory.ts ~176-197), when configuration.eventActions is non-empty, a new ActionRegistryEventbusListener is registered on the factory-wide this._eventbus before resolver.process is called. The interceptor is registered there too. In ConfigurationResolver.process (src/configuration/configuration-resolver.ts ~108-126), resolveEventActions builds each event Action and calls actionRegistryListener.registerAction at line 169. Only after that does createActionsLookup run, and it throws "Duplicate action name ... at actions[0].name and eventActions[0].name". createEngine has no try/catch around process or the later steps. The removers are pushed into engineRemovers and this._eventRemovers, but nothing calls them on failure, and the engine-level destroy() never reaches the caller because createEngine throws. Eventbus._callHandlers runs every registered listener on each broadcast: _getEventHandlers always returns an array, so the `if (handlers)` check is always true. A later broadcast of 'lang-changed' on the same factory's eventbus therefore reaches the orphaned listener, and ActionRegistryEventbusListener.handleEvent starts the stale event action. Nothing guards against this. The existing tests (configuration-resolver.spec.ts:567/586) only check that the duplicate error is thrown, not cleanup. The only mitigation is factory.destroy(), which removes the orphaned registrations because the removers are also in _eventRemovers. Callers who retry on the same factory do not get that cleanup. The leak is wider than the duplicate-name check. Any throw after the listener is registered leaves registrations behind: a throw later in process (createActionsLookup) or after process (_createTimelineProviders, the EngineClass constructor, adapter connect). A throw after process leaves event actions that are already fully registered. Reordering inside the resolver alone does not fix this, so the cleanup belongs in the factory. It only shows up after a rejected configuration is retried on the same factory, so severity stays low.

**Repro test.** `given a config with action 'Show' and eventAction 'Show' on eventName 'lang-changed', when createEngine throws for the duplicate name and 'lang-changed' is then broadcast on the factory eventbus, then the rejected event action's start operations are not executed`

### 69. [low] template:/json: placeholder with a missing importer entry silently becomes undefined

[configuration-resolver.ts:428](src/configuration/configuration-resolver.ts#L428) · area: configuration-tools · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** The ISimpleResourceImporter contract says that returning a record without a value for `name` is treated as an unknown name, reported with the JSON path. importPlaceholder only wraps thrown errors. For a non-throwing importer the placeholder is replaced with undefined, which contradicts that contract.

**Failure scenario.** A custom importer returns {} for unknown names, and layoutTemplate is 'template:mian' (typo). process() succeeds with layoutTemplate === undefined, and the engine later renders an empty layout with no error naming the placeholder.

**Evidence.**

```ts
try {
    return importer.import(name)?.[name];
  } catch (error) {
--- src/types.ts
   * it (`EligiusResourceImporter` does, with a "did you mean" hint). Returning
   * a record without a value for `name` is also accepted. The engine treats
   * both as an unknown name
```

**Fix.** Make importPlaceholder check the returned record: `const record = importer.import(name); if (!isObject(record) || !Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(record, name)) throw new Error(`Unable to resolve placeholder '${placeholder}' at ${path}: unknown name '${name}'`);`. Use `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call` rather than an `=== undefined` test, so that a template legitimately registered with a falsy or undefined value is not confused with a missing name. Keep the try/catch for importers that throw, and add ` at ${path}` to that message too. To get the path, give resolvePlaceholders/resolvePlaceholder a `path` string parameter that builds `parent.key` / `parent[i]` while walking. Then update importPlaceholder's doc comment, and add a spec next to the throwing-importer tests that uses an importer returning {}.

Original suggestion: After the import, throw `Unable to resolve placeholder '${placeholder}' at <path>` when the record lacks an own `name` key. Thread the JSON path through resolvePlaceholders so the message includes it.

**Verifier.** I traced the scenario through the real code and it holds. ConfigurationResolver.process() calls resolvePlaceholders(..., 'import'), which goes to resolvePlaceholder (src/configuration/configuration-resolver.ts ~398-411). For 'template:x' and 'json:x' that calls importPlaceholder (line 422). importPlaceholder returns `importer.import(name)?.[name]` and only wraps errors that the importer throws. If an importer returns {} for an unknown name, the function returns undefined. resolvePlaceholder then assigns `configFragment[key] = undefined` with no check. process() goes on and passes `layoutTemplate: configuration.layoutTemplate` (line 85) into the resolved config.  This breaks the public contract in src/types.ts:256-270 (ISimpleResourceImporter.import). That contract says returning a record without a value for `name` "is also accepted. The engine treats both as an unknown name: the configuration resolver reports the JSON path".  Nothing I found stops it: - importPlaceholder's own doc comment says "An importer that returns no entry still yields `undefined`". That makes the gap deliberate inside the resolver, but it still contradicts the exported interface doc, which is the contract custom importers are written against. - The sibling path resolveOperation (lines 194-219) does handle the missing-entry case. Its `!isFunction(instance)` check throws with the JSON path, so the inconsistency is specific to placeholders. - The tests in src/test/unit/configuration/configuration-resolver.spec.ts ('unknown placeholder imports', ~line 504) only use createThrowingImporter. No test covers a non-throwing importer returning {}.  Things that limit severity: 1. The default EligiusResourceImporter throws for unknown names, so only a custom ISimpleResourceImporter can trigger this. 2. A typo in a placeholder in an otherwise valid configuration is a configuration error, not a runtime defect. 3. Even the throwing path does not include the JSON path today. The message names only the placeholder, so the contract's "reports the JSON path" claim is unmet for placeholders either way.  Severity: low.

**Repro test.** `given an importer that returns an empty record for an unknown template, when process is called with layoutTemplate 'template:mian', then it throws naming the placeholder 'template:mian' and the JSON path 'layoutTemplate'`

### 70. [low] Fallback operation schemas drop @required properties and still count as success

[generate-schemas.ts:240](src/tools/schema-tools/generate-schemas.ts#L240) · area: configuration-tools · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** When ts-json-schema-generator fails on a data type (generic createElement data, getControllerInstance's TSystemName), the generator writes an unconstrained operationData schema and logs '✓'. The metadata marks createElement.elementName and getControllerInstance.systemName as required, but the schema accepts configurations without them.

**Failure scenario.** {systemName:'createElement', operationData:{}} validates against the published schema. At runtime createElement throws "invalid elementName 'undefined'". The schema is the documented first line of validation.

**Evidence.**

```ts
} catch (error) {
      // Create a fallback schema for complex generic types
      const fallbackSchema = createFallbackOperationSchema(
        operationName,
        typeName
      );
...
      successCount++;
```

**Fix.** In the catch branch, do not drop the type information. Use ts-morph to resolve the data interface's properties, as `findOperationExports` already locates the declaration. Emit `operationData.properties` for each property, and fill `operationData.required` from the properties tagged `@required` and not `@dependency`. When that list is non-empty, add 'operationData' to the top-level `required`, matching what `createOperationSchema` does.  Alternatively, read `required` from the generated operation metadata. For createElement, a simpler route is to give the generator a concrete type (e.g. `ICreateElementOperationData<TTagNames>`) so the normal path works.  Change the log to a warning ('⚠ fallback') and count fallbacks separately from successes. Exit non-zero when a fallback would drop a `@required` property.

Original suggestion: In the fallback, build operationData.properties/required from the @required/@dependency tags with ts-morph (as metadata-generator already does), or read them from the generated metadata. Log fallbacks as warnings, and fail when a fallback loses a @required property.

**Verifier.** I traced the code and checked the generated output, and the report holds.  1. **Code path.** In src/tools/schema-tools/generate-schemas.ts, lines 238-253 catch any `generator.createSchema(typeName)` failure. They then write `createFallbackOperationSchema(operationName, typeName)` (lines 628-663), log a '✓' line and increment `successCount`. That fallback's `operationData` is `{type:'object', additionalProperties:true}`, with no `properties` and no `required`.  2. **The fallback really runs.** The freshly generated jsonschema/operations/create-element.json (written Oct 21 23:24, after the last edit to the generator) and get-controller-instance.json are both this fallback. Both carry the text "See TypeScript definition for full type information."  3. **The tags really are required.** In src/operation/create-element.ts, `ICreateElementOperationData<T extends TTagNames>` marks `elementName` `@required` (line 14). This generic is why the generator fails. In src/operation/get-controller-instance.ts, `systemName` is `@required` (line 9).  4. **No guard elsewhere.** The only check after the loop is the "missing schema file" check, which a fallback satisfies. ConfigurationResolver and EngineFactory do no runtime check for required operation data; a grep for "required" in src/configuration and engine-factory finds nothing relevant.  5. **Runtime result.** `{systemName:'createElement', operationData:{}}` validates against the schema. At runtime, createElement.ts:62-65 then throws "createElement: invalid elementName 'undefined' ...". For getControllerInstance there is no throw: it silently requests an instance for `undefined`.  6. **No tests.** No test in src/test covers generate-schemas or the fallback, and src/tools is excluded from coverage.  **Severity: low.** This only affects build-time tooling, not the engine. The engine still fails fast with a clear message for createElement, so no valid configuration is harmed. The only effect is a weaker editor/schema check for two operations, plus a misleading '✓' in the build log.

**Repro test.** `given the generated create-element operation schema, when validating {systemName:'createElement', operationData:{}}, then validation fails because elementName is required`

### 71. [low] Event metadata drops tuple optionality and the @param descriptions

[events-metadata-generator.ts:173](src/tools/code-generator/events-metadata-generator.ts#L173) · area: configuration-tools · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** getArgMetadata reads only the name and type node of each NamedTupleMember. It ignores the question token, so optional args are emitted as plain entries. The arg description is hard-coded to '' although the event files document every arg with @param. IEventMetadata has no optional field, so tooling cannot tell `position?: number` from a required arg.

**Failure scenario.** request-video-url declares args [videoUrlIndex: number, requestedVideoPosition?: number, fromRouting?: boolean]. The generated metadata lists three args with no optional marker and no descriptions, so a DSL or editor using it (Eligian) demands all three when broadcasting the event.

**Evidence.**

```ts
if (element.getKind() === SyntaxKind.NamedTupleMember) {
        name = element.getName();
        const typeNode = element.getTypeNode();
        type = typeNode ? typeNode.getText() : 'unknown';
...
      const description = ''; // We'll add @param support later if needed
```

**Fix.** In getArgMetadata: - Emit `optional: true` when element.getKind() === SyntaxKind.NamedTupleMember && element.hasQuestionToken(). Also handle SyntaxKind.OptionalType for unnamed `T?` elements. - Take the arg description from the @param tag in interfaceDecl.getJsDocs() whose name matches the tuple label. Pass interfaceDecl into getArgMetadata, strip the leading '- ', and escape backslashes and single quotes, or emit it with JSON.stringify. - Use JSON.stringify for the name and type strings too, so a type containing quotes cannot break the generated file.  Add `optional?: boolean` to the arg entry in src/eventbus/events/metadata/types.ts.  Move the arg-extraction logic into an exported pure function, so a spec can test it with an in-memory ts-morph Project without running the generator's top-level side effects.  Rerun `npm run events-metadata`.

Original suggestion: Emit `optional: true` when element.hasQuestionToken() and add `optional?: boolean` to IEventMetadata's arg entry. Map @param tags from the interface JSDoc to arg descriptions, escaping quotes. Add a generator spec.

**Verifier.** I traced the report through the generator and its actual output, and it holds. In src/tools/code-generator/events-metadata-generator.ts, getArgMetadata (lines 153-192) reads only getName() and getTypeNode() from each NamedTupleMember. It never calls hasQuestionToken(). Line 183 hard-codes `const description = ''`, with the comment "We'll add @param support later if needed", so the description branch at line 188 can never run.  The checked-in generated file src/eventbus/events/metadata/request-video-url.ts proves it. It lists videoUrlIndex:number, requestedVideoPosition:number and fromRouting:boolean with no optional marker and no descriptions. The source interface in src/eventbus/events/request-video-url.ts has @param docs for all three args and marks the last two with `?`.  IEventMetadata (src/eventbus/events/metadata/types.ts) has no `optional` field. Its `description?` field is documented as "Optional description of the argument from JSDoc @param tag", and its @example shows an arg description. So the intended contract is visibly unmet. getDescription uses getCommentText(), which leaves out tags, so the @param text does not end up in the event description either.  I found nothing that blocks this: no post-processing step and no other generator writes these files. No test under src/test covers the event metadata generator.  Why it stays low severity: - Runtime engine behaviour is unaffected. EventMap/EventName come from the real tuple types, which keep their optionality, so TypeScript callers are type-checked correctly. - Only metadata consumers (docs, Eligian/editor tooling) lose the information. - The claim that Eligian "demands all three" args is speculation about an external consumer that I could not verify. - The missing @param support is an acknowledged TODO, not a regression.

**Repro test.** `given request-video-url declares requestedVideoPosition? and fromRouting? with @param docs, when requestVideoUrl() metadata is read, then args[1] and args[2] have optional true and every arg has a non-empty description`

### 72. [low] SubtitleImporter emits subtitles without id and joins multi-line text without a separator

[subtitle-converter.ts:25](src/configuration/api/subtitle-converter.ts#L25) · area: configuration-tools · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** Captions are created as {text: ''} and returned as ISubtitle, but `id` (required by ISubtitle) is never set. Continuation lines are appended directly. SubtitleEditor.setSubtitle finds a subtitle by id, so with all ids undefined it always matches the first one.

**Failure scenario.** importSubtitles(['00:00:01:00 00:00:03:00','Hello','world','','00:00:04:00 00:00:05:00','Bye']) returns [{text:'Helloworld',...},{text:'Bye',...}], both without id. Calling setSubtitle('en-US', {...titles[1], text:'Ciao'}) overwrites titles[0] instead of titles[1].

**Evidence.**

```ts
caption = {text: ''};
...
          caption.text += line;
--- subtitle-editor.ts:89
    const titleIdx = language.titles.findIndex(x => x.id === subtitle.id);
```

**Fix.** In subtitle-converter.ts, import `{v4 as uuidv4} from 'uuid'` and create each caption as `{id: uuidv4(), text: ''}`. For continuation lines, use `caption.text = caption.text ? `${caption.text}\n${line}` : line;`. Use '\n' rather than '<br>', so the importer never produces HTML; the output then works with the label sanitizer and with plain-text rendering. Remove the `as ISubtitle` cast by building the object fully before pushing it, so the compiler enforces the contract. Add src/test/unit/configuration/api/subtitle-converter.spec.ts covering ids, multi-line joining, leading and trailing blank lines, and @/<begin subtitles> filtering.

Original suggestion: Assign `id: uuidv4()` to each caption, and join continuation lines with '\n' or '<br>' (sanitised downstream). Add a spec for subtitle-converter, which currently has no tests.

**Verifier.** I traced the code in F:\projects\eligius\eligius\src\configuration\api\subtitle-converter.ts and found nothing that guards against either problem.  Missing id: - Line 25 creates each caption as `{text: ''}` and stores it as `Partial<ISubtitle>`. - Line 30 pushes it with `caption as ISubtitle`. That cast hides the fact that `id` is never set. - `ISubtitle` (src/types.ts:345) requires `id: string`. Every other place that builds a subtitle, `SubtitleEditor.addLanguage` and `addSubtitles`, assigns `uuidv4()`. So the importer is the only producer that breaks the contract, and nothing documents this as intended.  Joined lines: - Continuation lines are appended with `caption.text += line` (line 35), with no separator.  Tracing the auditor's input: - The first line goes in while `caption` is null, giving `{text:'', duration:{1,3}}`. - 'Hello' is added, then 'world', so the text becomes 'Helloworld'. - The empty line starts a new `{text:''}` caption. - The next timestamp line gives it a duration, then 'Bye' is added. - The result is `[{text:'Helloworld',duration:{start:1,end:3}}, {text:'Bye',duration:{start:4,end:5}}]`, and neither entry has an `id`.  Effect on `SubtitleEditor.setSubtitle`: - If those titles go into a `SubtitleEditor`, `setSubtitle` looks them up with `findIndex(x => x.id === subtitle.id)` (subtitle-editor.ts:89). - `undefined === undefined` is true for every entry, so the lookup always returns index 0. Updating titles[1] overwrites titles[0].  Tests: `SubtitleImporter` is exported through `src/configuration/api/index.ts` and `src/configuration-api.ts`. The only test that mentions it (`src/test/unit/configuration-api.spec.ts`) just checks the export name. No test covers the importer's behaviour.  Severity: low. The importer is a helper for building configurations and is never called by the engine at runtime. A valid JSON configuration cannot trigger this; only a user who builds subtitles with this helper in the fluent API can. The impact is wrong subtitle data, not engine state corruption.

**Repro test.** `given an AVID subtitle with a two-line caption followed by a second caption, when importSubtitles is called, then every subtitle has a unique id and the two lines are joined with a newline`

### 73. [low] editTimelineAction reports the action id instead of the missing timeline uri

[configuration-factory.ts:564](src/configuration/api/configuration-factory.ts#L564) · area: configuration-tools · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** When the timeline uri is unknown, the error interpolates the action id, so the message points at the wrong identifier.

**Failure scenario.** factory.editTimelineAction('mian', 'abc-123') throws 'Timeline not found for id abc-123', so the user looks for a wrong action id instead of the typo in the uri.

**Evidence.**

```ts
const timeline = this.getTimeline(uri);
    if (!timeline) {
      throw new Error(`Timeline not found for id ${id}`);
    }
```

**Fix.** In editTimelineAction, replace the throw with `throw new Error(`No timeline found for uri '${uri}'`);` so it matches addTimelineAction. Optionally, change the last throw to `Timeline action not found for id '${id}' in timeline '${uri}'` so that error names both identifiers.

Original suggestion: throw new Error(`No timeline found for uri '${uri}'`) (consistent with addTimelineAction).

**Verifier.** I traced it through the real code and it holds. In src/configuration/api/configuration-factory.ts, `editTimelineAction(uri, id)` (lines 561-571) calls `this.getTimeline(uri)`. `getTimeline` (line 484-486) is just `this.configuration.timelines.find(t => t.uri === uri)`. It does not throw and does not check anything, so an unknown uri returns undefined. The `!timeline` branch then throws `Timeline not found for id ${id}`, which puts the action id where the uri should be. `editTimelineAction` is a public method on the exported ConfigurationFactory, and the only check before it is that `find`, so any caller can hit this with a mistyped uri. Nothing in src/test/ covers this message. A grep for 'Timeline not found' in src/test finds nothing. The existing editTimelineAction tests (configuration-factory.spec.ts:340, :518 and node-environment.spec.ts:48) only use valid uris. The sibling method `addTimelineAction` (line 390) throws `No timeline found for uri '${uri}'`, and the spec at line 144 asserts that message, so the uri wording is clearly the intended convention. The impact is limited to the error message: the call still fails fast and nothing is corrupted. The severity stays low.

**Repro test.** `given a factory with timeline 'main', when editTimelineAction is called with unknown uri 'mian' and id 'abc-123', then it throws "No timeline found for uri 'mian'"`

### 74. [low] Fluent API stores caller operationData by reference; setOperationDataItem mutates it

[action-creator-factory.ts:199](src/configuration/api/action-creator-factory.ts#L199) · area: configuration-tools · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** addStartOperation and addEndOperation (here and in ActionEditor) push the caller's operationData object as is. OperationEditor.setOperationDataItem then mutates that object in place. Reusing one literal for two operations, or keeping a reference to it, leaks edits across operations.

**Failure scenario.** const d = {selector:'#a'}; editor.addStartOperation('selectElement', d); editor.addStartOperation('selectElement', d); editor.editStartOperation(firstId).setOperationDataItem('selector','#b') changes the second operation's selector to '#b' as well.

**Evidence.**

```ts
startOperations.push({
      id: uuidv4(),
      systemName: systemName,
      operationData: operationData,
    });
--- action-editor.ts:510
    operationData[key] = value;
    return this.setOperationData(operationData);
```

**Fix.** Make the API copy instead of alias in three places: 1. In all four add*Operation methods (ActionCreator/EndableActionCreator in action-creator-factory.ts and ActionEditor in action-editor.ts), store `operationData: deepCopy(operationData)`. 2. Make setOperationDataItem copy-on-write: `return this.setOperationData({...(this.operationConfig.operationData ?? {}), [key]: value} as ExtractOperationDataType<O>);`. 3. Optionally deepCopy in setOperationData as well, so a caller-held reference cannot alias the stored config later.

Original suggestion: deepCopy operationData when it is added (as getConfiguration already does), and have setOperationDataItem assign a shallow copy: `this.setOperationData({...operationData, [key]: value})`.

**Verifier.** I traced the claim through the code and it holds. Nothing in the path makes a copy: - ActionCreator.addStartOperation (action-creator-factory.ts:195-204) and EndableActionCreator.addEndOperation (:266-279) push `operationData` by reference. - ActionEditor.addStartOperation (action-editor.ts:128-152) and addEndOperation (:286-310) do the same. - ConfigurationFactory._internalAddAction and addTimelineAction push the creator's actionConfig by reference too. - editAction / editStartOperation return editors over the live objects stored in this.configuration. - OperationEditor.setOperationDataItem (action-editor.ts:502-512) mutates `this.operationConfig.operationData` in place and then calls setOperationData with the same object.  deepCopy runs only inside the getConfiguration callbacks. It does not run on add, so it does not stop the aliasing.  I ran a throwaway Vitest probe, then deleted it. The probe built two `selectElement` start operations from one literal `{selector:'#a'}`, then called `editAction(id).editStartOperation(firstId).setOperationDataItem('selector','#b')`. The second operation's selector then read '#b'. The assertion that it stays '#a' failed.  No existing test in src/test/unit/configuration/api covers aliasing.  Severity stays low: - It only affects configuration authoring through the fluent API. - It needs the caller to reuse one operationData object, or mutate one they kept a reference to. - A JSON configuration can never trigger it, and engine runtime state is not affected.

**Repro test.** `given one operationData literal passed to addStartOperation twice, when setOperationDataItem changes selector on the first operation via editAction().editStartOperation(), then the second operation's selector is still '#a'`

### 75. [low] typedoc script backgrounds the run that writes docs.json, racing generate-readmes

[package.json:43](package.json#L43) · area: configuration-tools · reported severity: low · verifier: sonnet

**Summary.** docs.json comes from typedoc-metadata.json, which the `typedoc` script starts with a single `&`. Under sh (Linux/macOS npm), that run is backgrounded, and its failure is ignored. `npm run docs` then runs copy-schema and generate-readmes, which read docs.json while it may still be missing or half written. On Windows cmd `&` is sequential, so the maintainer does not see this.

**Failure scenario.** On a clean Linux checkout `npm run docs` exits with 'docs.json not found!' or a JSON.parse SyntaxError. Or it writes READMEs from a stale docs.json when the foreground typedoc finishes first.

**Evidence.**

```ts
"typedoc": "typedoc --options typedoc-metadata.json & typedoc --options typedoc.json",
--- generate-readmes.ts
const docsJsonPath = path.resolve(__dirname, '../../../docs.json');
...
const docs = JSON.parse(contents) as JSONOutput.ProjectReflection;
```

**Fix.** Change line 43 to `"typedoc": "typedoc --options typedoc-metadata.json && typedoc --options typedoc.json"`. With `&&` the runs happen in order on sh and cmd alike, and either failure stops `npm run docs`. Optionally, make generate-readmes catch the JSON.parse error and exit with a clear message instead of a stack trace.

Original suggestion: Use `&&` ("typedoc --options typedoc-metadata.json && typedoc --options typedoc.json") so both run in order and failures propagate.

**Verifier.** The script is as quoted. package.json line 43 reads `typedoc --options typedoc-metadata.json & typedoc --options typedoc.json`. typedoc-metadata.json is the only config with `"json": "./docs.json"`, so docs.json comes only from the first command. On Linux and macOS, npm runs scripts through `sh -c`, and POSIX `a & b` starts `a` in the background. The shell's exit status is then only `b`'s. That has two effects: (1) If the metadata typedoc run fails, the failure is always ignored. (2) `npm run typedoc` can return while the backgrounded run is still writing docs.json.  `docs` (line 44) then chains `node copy-schema && npm run generate-readmes`. generate-readmes.ts (lines 20-36) checks only that docs.json exists, then calls `JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(docsJsonPath))`. On a clean checkout docs.json is gitignored (.gitignore line 9), so this path either exits with 'docs.json not found!', throws a JSON.parse SyntaxError on a partly written file, or silently reads a stale file. On Windows, npm uses cmd.exe, where `&` runs the commands one after the other, so the maintainer never sees this.  Nothing guards against it. I checked .npmrc (none, so no script-shell override), the generator's own check (existence only), and the tests (nothing touches the docs scripts).  Why the impact is small: - The race (point 2) depends on timing. The JSON-only run skips HTML rendering, so it usually finishes before the foreground HTML run. In practice the partial-read case is rare. - Point (1), the ignored failure, is certain under sh. - No workflow runs `npm run docs` (ci-build.yml runs build/typecheck/lint/test:ci; main.yml and publish.yml only reuse it). - This is local documentation tooling. No engine configuration can trigger it, and it does not affect the published package.  Severity stays low.

**Repro test.** `given the package.json typedoc script, when it is split on shell control operators, then it contains no lone '&' that would background the docs.json typedoc run under POSIX sh`

## Uncertain (needs a human look)

- [video-position-source.ts:253](src/timelineproviders/position-sources/video-position-source.ts#L253) **A video seek that never gets 'seeked' leaves the engine's _seekDepth raised for good** (low). seek() resolves only on 'seeked' or on destroy(). If the media errors while seeking (a network drop or decode error), 'seeked' never fires and the promise stays pending. EligiusEngine._seekSource increments _seekDepth before awaiting it and only decrements it in finally. _onTimeHandler then ignores every later position update, so no action dispatches again until the engine is destroyed. Verifier: The code reads as the auditor says. VideoPositionSource.seek() (src/timelineproviders/position-sources/video-position-source.ts:239-261) settles only from player.one('seeked') or from doDestroy(), which drains _pendingSeeks. There is no 'error' listener and no timeout. The only 'error' listener is the one-shot in _createPlayer, and it only settles init. EligiusEngine._seekSource (src/eligius-engine.ts:1040-1051) does _seekDepth++ / await source.seek() / finally _seekDepth--. _onTimeHandler (line 1054) returns early while _seekDepth > 0, and nothing else resets _seekDepth: stop(), switchTimeline and seek never touch it. The tests cover seeked and destroy-while-pending (video-position-source.spec.ts:527, 556, 930). None covers an error during a seek.  Whether this can actually happen, and whether it matters, depends on browser and media runtime behaviour that the code cannot settle: 1. Does 'seeked' really never fire? Under the HTML media spec, a fatal network or decode error leaves the seek pending, so 'seeked' never comes. But for HLS/DASH through video.js VHS, failed segment requests are retried or switched to another rendition, and the seek usually completes. So the scenario is realistic only for progressive sources with a fatal error. 2. A later seek clears the stuck state if it succeeds. Setting currentTime aborts the pending seek, and the next 'seeked' fires every queued one() listener, so _seekDepth drops back to 0. engine.seek(), engine.stop() (deactivate -> currentTime(0)) and loop-end all set currentTime. So it is not "for good" in general. 3. When the element is in a fatal error state, those recovery seeks also never complete. But then playback is dead anyway: no timeupdate events arrive, so there is nothing to dispatch. The claimed consequence that actions never dispatch again only shows up if something outside the engine reloads the stream (for example, calling load() on the <video> element in the container). The engine has no API for that, and load() resets 'seeking' without firing 'seeked'.  The impact that is real and specific to the engine is narrower: `await engine.seek(x)` (or a switchTimeline whose start seek hits the error) never resolves, and 'seekComplete' is never emitted. switchTimeline runs on a serial queue, so a hung switch would also block every later switch. That only needs a fatal media error during a seek, which is a runtime condition, not something a valid configuration can trigger on its own. I cannot confirm the auditor's "even after reload" scenario from the code, and the main effect only follows a media error that already breaks playback. Low severity stands.

### Unverified (merged into a neighbouring bug by the workflow de-duplication, so never checked)

- [eligius-engine.ts:499](src/eligius-engine.ts#L499) **Pending switchTimeline/init/start resume after destroy() and reactivate sources** (medium, lens-lifecycle-memory). The engine has no destroyed state. destroy() does not wait for _switchQueue, the localesReady wait in init(), or start()'s wait for _pendingStopCleanup. Those continuations run after teardown. They call _activateProvider (which sets _activePositionSource again and, now that _preparedSources is cleared, registers callbacks again and calls source.init()), _reconcileActions (which runs timeline-action starts against the emptied root) and source.activate() on a destroyed source. Nothing ever stops that source again. Scenario: A RAF timeline 'b' has loop:true. A NavigationController click broadcasts request-timeline-uri ['b'] (the adapter does not await engine.switchTimeline), and the host then awaits the factory destroy(). destroy() yields at _endActionsSettled, and the queued _switchTimeline runs in the gaps. Once destroy has destroyed the sources and cleared _preparedSources, the switch resumes: it starts b's actions on the emptied root (controllers attach to the shared eventbus and are never detached) and calls b.activate(). That starts an animationInterval whose AbortController nobody aborts, so the RAF/setTimeout chain runs forever and keeps the engine reachable. With a KeyboardStepSource the same path leaves global hotkeys bound and hotkeys.setScope stuck on the source's scope. The same happens with init() still waiting for a $ref locale fetch, or start() still waiting for a stop() cleanup. Fix: Add a private _destroyed flag that destroy() sets first. destroy() should also await this._switchQueue (and any pending init) before tearing down. Check the flag after every await in _switchTimeline, _initializeTimelineProvider and start(), and bail out before acquiring anything. Have BasePositionSource reject activate()/init() after destroy() (a destroyed guard) as a second line of defence.

## Improvements

### operations-a

- [calc.ts:56](src/operation/calc.ts#L56) **calc and createElement resolve the entire operation data in place, not just their inputs** (medium). Both operations call `resolvePropertyValues(operationData, this, operationData)`. That walks every property in the running operation data and replaces, in place, any string that looks like `$operationdata.`, `$globaldata.` or `$scope.`, including nested objects that earlier operations left behind. An unrelated leftover chain that cannot be resolved (for example `$operationdata.a.b` where a is undefined) makes calc throw 'Property chain ... cannot be resolved', and other properties are changed as a side effect. An unknown `operator` fails with the unhelpful TypeError 'calcFunctions[operator] is not a function'. Fix: Resolve only the operation's own inputs: `resolvePropertyValues(operationData, this, {left, right, operator})` and `{elementName, attributes, text}`. Validate `operator` with `Object.hasOwn(calcFunctions, operator)` and throw `calc: unknown operator '<op>'`.
- [get-controller-instance.ts:34](src/operation/get-controller-instance.ts#L34) **getControllerInstance bypasses requestRegisteredEntry, so failures are silent or vague** (low). customFunction and getImport now go through `requestRegisteredEntry`, which names the operation and the systemName and rejects undefined. getControllerInstance still calls `eventbus.request` directly. With no `request-instance` responder it stores `controllerInstance: undefined`, and the error only appears later as a TypeError in addControllerToElement. A name that resolves to a non-class (for example an operation name) throws 'X is not a constructor' without naming getControllerInstance. Fix: Wrap the request the same way requestRegisteredEntry does, for example with a variant that takes the event name. Throw `getControllerInstance: no controller registered for systemName '<name>'` when the result is null or undefined, and keep the importer's 'did you mean' hint as `cause`.
- [add-controller-to-element.ts:35](src/operation/add-controller-to-element.ts#L35) **addControllerToElement registers the controller on the element before init/attach succeed** (low). The controller is pushed into the element's controller list before `init` and `attach` run. If either throws, or if `attach()` rejects (now more likely, since LottieController's attach is async and lottie-web is an optional peer), the controller stays registered. getControllerFromElement then returns it, and detachControllersWithin/removeControllerFromElement call `detach()` on a controller that never attached. Fix: Register the controller after init and a synchronous attach succeed. For a promise result, register before awaiting (so detach can cancel) but remove it from the element's list in the rejection handler before rejecting.
- [end-action.ts:12](src/operation/end-action.ts#L12) **Metadata tags disagree with runtime (endAction @required, @output typed as input)** (low). endAction defaults `actionOperationData` to `{}`, and the changelog says omitting it is now supported, but the property is still tagged `@required`, so the generated schema and metadata require it. startAction marks the same property as optional. Several operations also declare `@output` properties as required members of the input interface (compareDate's comparison/isEqual/isBefore/isAfter, concatenateStrings.result, filterArray.filteredArray, findInArray.foundItem/foundIndex, formatDate.formattedDate, formatText.formattedText, getCurrentTime, getScrollPosition, getFocusedElement). Typed callers must supply outputs they cannot know in advance. Some operations also carry both `@required` and `@dependency` (filterArray.arrayData, formatDate.date, formatText.textContent, getFormData/getTextContent.selectedElement). The generators let @dependency win, so `@required` there is noise that misleads readers. Fix: Make `actionOperationData?` optional and drop `@required`. Make `@output` properties optional in the input interfaces (as calc and getAttributesFromElement already do) and use RequireKeys in the return type. Remove `@required` from `@dependency` properties.
- [announce-to-screen-reader.ts:48](src/operation/announce-to-screen-reader.ts#L48) **announceToScreenReader does not reliably re-announce repeated or first messages** (low). The live region is created and filled in the same task, and many screen readers ignore content inserted together with a new live region. Setting the same `textContent` twice in a row causes no DOM mutation, so a repeated message (for example 'Slide 3' after seeking back) is not announced. The region is never cleared. Fix: Clear `textContent` and then set the message on the next frame or after a microtask/timeout. When the region is new, insert it empty first and fill it after a delay. Optionally clear it again after a few seconds.
- [format-date.ts:22](src/operation/format-date.ts#L22) **formatDate doc promises Intl.DateTimeFormat; patterns only replace the first occurrence** (low). The JSDoc says 'Formats a date using Intl.DateTimeFormat or simple patterns', but Intl is never used. Each token is replaced with `String.replace` on a string, so only the first occurrence changes: 'HH:mm-HH:mm' gives '14:05-HH:mm'. Supported tokens are not documented. Fix: Use `replaceAll` or a single token regex (`/YYYY|MM|DD|HH|mm|ss/g` with a lookup map), list the supported tokens in the JSDoc, and drop the Intl claim (or add a `locale`/`options` path that actually uses Intl.DateTimeFormat).

### controllers

- [base-controller.ts:51](src/controllers/base-controller.ts#L51) **BaseController.addListener erases the eventbus handler types** (medium). IEventbus.on is typed per event (`(...args: EventMap[E]) => void`), but addListener takes `(...args: any[]) => void` and casts `as any`, and attachMultiple accepts arbitrary strings. Handler/argument mismatches in controllers (for example the timeline-duration getter vs number) are not caught at compile time. Fix: Make it generic: `protected addListener<E extends EventName>(eventbus: IEventbus, eventName: E, handler: (this: this, ...args: EventMap[E]) => void)`, and type attachMultiple entries the same way (or remove it, since no controller uses it).
- [base-controller.ts:82](src/controllers/base-controller.ts#L82) **Controllers have no attached-state guard, so re-attach duplicates listeners** (low). BaseController does not track whether it is attached. A second attach without detach in Label, Progressbar, Subtitles or Navigation registers every eventbus listener twice (Navigation also builds a second menu), and two concurrent LottieController attaches both pass the generation check and register two language listeners. A single guard in the base class would make the lifecycle idempotent for all subclasses. Fix: Add a protected `isAttached` flag. Make attach in subclasses detach first (or return early) when already attached, and clear the flag in BaseController.detach.
- [lottie-controller.ts:61](src/controllers/lottie-controller.ts#L61) **LottieController metadata tags misdescribe its inputs** (low). `url` is @required but is only parsed for [freeze,end] and never loaded. Init still dereferences it, so a config with only animationData throws a TypeError. `json` is @required although animationData is an accepted alternative. animationData's tag lacks the `@type=` prefix, so it gets no parameter type. When neither json nor animationData is given, serializedData becomes undefined and attach silently creates nothing. Fix: Make url optional (or name it `segments`), require exactly one of json/animationData with a named error in init, and fix the tag to `@type=ParameterType:object`.
- [lottie-controller.ts:302](src/controllers/lottie-controller.ts#L302) **Lottie language change restarts the animation from frame 0** (low). _handleLanguageChange rebuilds the animation via _createAnimation, which destroys the item and loads a new one with autoplay (or replays [0, freeze]). Switching language mid-animation visibly restarts it. Fix: Capture animationItem.currentFrame and the paused state before recreating, then goToAndPlay/goToAndStop that frame (respecting the freeze segment).
- [subtitles-controller.ts:143](src/controllers/subtitles-controller.ts#L143) **Dead state and paths left in Subtitles and Lottie controllers** (low). Since the range-based lookup, SubtitlesController.actionLookup and subtitleDurations are computed but read only by tests. LottieController's 'request-label-collections'/labelData path has no responder in src since the 2.2.0 labels removal, and the _isIE/iefallback branch targets browsers no longer supported. The tests pin the dead behaviour instead of the real one. Fix: Remove actionLookup/subtitleDurations (keep _titleRanges) and the IE branch, decide whether label collections stay public API (deprecate them if not), and update the specs to assert on rendered output.
- [dom-event-listener-controller.ts:68](src/controllers/dom-event-listener-controller.ts#L68) **DOMEventListenerController silently drops unknown action names** (low). When 'request-action' returns nothing for a configured name (a typo, or a missing 'end:' action), the action is skipped without any message. The rest of the codebase now fails loudly on unknown names. Fix: Throw (or at least console.warn) with `DOMEventListenerController: unknown action "<name>" for event "<eventName>"`, matching the unknown-name handling in the importer.
- [progressbar-controller.ts:42](src/controllers/progressbar-controller.ts#L42) **ProgressbarController leaves pointer-events:none behind and is loosely typed** (low). attach sets pointer-events:none on the bar but detach never restores it. init (like Navigation and Routing) takes TOperationData instead of its own data interface and never sets this.operationData, so typing and BaseController state are inconsistent across controllers. Fix: Remember the previous inline value and restore it in detach. Type init as `init(operationData: IProgressbarControllerOperationData)` (same for Navigation/Routing) and assign this.operationData.
- [mutation-observer-controller.ts:159](src/controllers/mutation-observer-controller.ts#L159) **MutationObserverController lets invalid option combinations throw a raw TypeError** (low). observe() throws a TypeError when all of attributes/childList/characterData are false, or when attributeFilter/attributeOldValue is set while observeAttributes is false. The error comes from attach with no controller name or option path. Fix: Validate in init and throw `MutationObserverController: attributeFilter requires observeAttributes` (and so on). Omit attributeFilter/oldValue keys that do not apply.

### engine-core

- [eligius-engine.ts:479](src/eligius-engine.ts#L479) **switchTimeline silently resolves for unknown URIs and before init** (medium). An unknown URI, or a call before init(), resolves successfully without doing anything. Since this round, unknown names fail loudly elsewhere (operations, imports, resolver). A typo in a 'request-timeline-uri' payload or a navigation controller config is invisible. Fix: Reject with an error naming the unknown URI and the known URIs (a did-you-mean hint, like the importer), and reject when called before init(). Keep the same-URI case as a documented no-op.
- [eligius-engine.ts:905](src/eligius-engine.ts#L905) **Remove dead scheduling and flow-controller code** (low). `sortTimelines`, `sortActionsPerPosition`, `ActionMethod`/`ActionMethodMetadata` and `sortActionMethodsHighestStartPositionFirst` are left over from the old key-based dispatch and are used only by their own tests. `Action._initializeScopeStack` and the `_scopeStack` default parameter of `executeOperation` have no callers. `EligiusFlowController` is not imported or exported anywhere and ignores its eventbus argument. Fix: Mark the public statics @deprecated in this major release and remove them in the next. Delete `_initializeScopeStack`, make the `scopeStack` parameter required, and delete eligius-flow-controller.ts (or wire it in if timelineFlow is meant to work).
- [types.ts:232](src/types.ts#L232) **IEligiusEngine.stop() returns void | Promise<void>** (low). The union return type makes every consumer (including the adapter's `_handleRejection` instanceof check) handle both shapes, even though the only implementation always returns a Promise. This release already needs a major bump, so the contract can be tightened now. Fix: Declare `stop(): Promise<void>` and drop the `instanceof Promise` check in EngineEventbusAdapter.
- [eligius-engine.ts:874](src/eligius-engine.ts#L874) **Schedule creation mutates the resolved configuration's durations** (low). `_createSchedule` rewrites `duration.end` from -1 to Infinity on the action objects. The resolver already normalises missing ends to -1, so the sentinel is translated in two places, and the engine mutates configuration that is documented as immutable after resolution. Fix: Normalise open-ended durations once, in ConfigurationResolver (end ?? Infinity) or in the TimelineAction constructor, and keep `_createSchedule` read-only.
- [eligius-engine.ts:969](src/eligius-engine.ts#L969) **Position query returns -1 or 0 depending on the request style** (low). Before init (or after destroy), the engine's callback-style 'request-current-timeline-position' returns -1, while the adapter's onRequest responder for the same event returns `engine.position`, which is 0. Callers get different sentinels depending on the API they use. Fix: Pick one sentinel and document it, for example make both read `this.position`, or have both return -1 when no source is active.

### lens-security-robustness

- [request-action.ts:33](src/operation/request-action.ts#L33) **Unknown action names fail late or silently (requestAction, DOMEventListenerController)** (medium). The `request-action` responder logs and returns null for an unknown name. requestAction stores that null without checking it, so the failure appears one operation later as 'startAction: actionInstance is required', and the misspelled name is never mentioned. DOMEventListenerController skips unknown names with no error at all. This is inconsistent with the 'unknown names fail loudly' change made for customFunction/getImport via requestRegisteredEntry. Fix: In requestAction, throw `requestAction: no action named '${systemName}'` plus a did-you-mean hint (findClosestNames over the action lookup) when the result is null/undefined. Do the same in DOMEventListenerController.attach, naming the controller and the configured action. Also consider validating DOMEventListenerController action names at resolve time.
- [configuration-resolver.ts:455](src/configuration/configuration-resolver.ts#L455) **ConfigurationResolver: config: placeholders and missing arrays fail without JSON path** (medium). A `config:` placeholder whose last segment does not exist resolves silently to `undefined`. One with a missing intermediate throws `Property chain 'a.b' cannot be resolved.`, which names neither the placeholder nor where it sits in the config. Separately, an action without `startOperations`/`endOperations`, or a timeline without `timelineActions`/`duration`, crashes with a bare TypeError ('Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'map')'). This contradicts the rule that 'invalid configuration must fail fast with a message naming the JSON path'. Fix: Thread the JSON path through resolvePlaceholders and resolveConfigPlaceholder. Throw `Unresolvable placeholder 'config:engine.systemNam' at actions[2].startOperations[0].operationData.selector` when the chain throws or yields undefined. In resolveOperations, resolveTimelines and resolveTimelineAction, check `Array.isArray(...)` and the duration first, and throw naming `${path}.startOperations` and so on.
- [set-variable.ts:33](src/operation/set-variable.ts#L33) **Configured names used as keys on plain objects (setVariable, setGlobals, globals lookup)** (low). Several operations index plain `{}` maps with names taken straight from configuration. With `__proto__` this replaces the map's prototype (setVariable name '__proto__' makes every `$scope.variables.x` lookup fall through to attacker data). `getGlobals(name)` reads inherited members (`getGlobals('constructor')` returns `Object`). The shared chain resolvers are guarded, but these single-key writers and readers are not. Fix: Create `variables` and the globals `cache` with `Object.create(null)`. In setVariable, setGlobals and getGlobals, refuse names for which `isForbiddenPropertyChainSegment` is true, and read with `Object.hasOwn`. Throw errors that name the operation.

### eventbus-locale-util

- [types.ts:447](src/eventbus/types.ts#L447) **request()/onRequest() are untyped and request names are declared nowhere** (medium). Broadcast events are checked against EventMap, but request/response names are plain strings and the response type is whatever the caller asserts. Names in use, such as 'request-translation' (LocaleEventbusAdapter, LabelController), have no file in src/eventbus/events/. A typo or a responder registered under a slightly different name silently returns undefined. The metadata and docs generators cannot see these names either. Fix: Add a RequestMap of {name: {args: [...]; returns: R}} interfaces, generated like EventMap from files in src/eventbus/events/ (for example request-translation.ts with a `returns` member). Type request<K extends RequestName>(name: K, ...args: RequestMap[K]['args']): RequestMap[K]['returns'] | undefined, and type onRequest to match.
- [locale-loader.ts:145](src/locale/locale-loader.ts#L145) **LocaleLoader has no timeout or abort, so a stalled locale fetch hangs init()** (medium). The changelog says engine.init() now rejects when a $ref locale fails to load. But a request that never completes (stalled server, captive portal) never settles, so init() waits forever, and destroy() cannot cancel it. ILocaleLoaderOptions has no signal or timeout. Fix: Add optional `timeoutMs` and `signal` to ILocaleLoaderOptions. Pass an AbortSignal (AbortSignal.any([signal, AbortSignal.timeout(timeoutMs)])) to fetch, and turn an abort into `[LocaleLoader] Timed out loading locale file '<url>' after N ms`. Let EngineFactory set a sensible default and abort the loader in destroy().
- [request-video-uri-interceptor.ts:7](src/eventbus/request-video-uri-interceptor.ts#L7) **before-request-timeline-uri is typed with no args but broadcast with [uri, position]** (low). The interceptor rebroadcasts the request-timeline-uri arguments under 'before-request-timeline-uri' and hides the mismatch with `as any`, while the event interface declares `args: []`. Typed handlers cannot see the URI and position they actually receive, and the generated metadata documents the event as having no arguments. Fix: Declare `args: [uri: string, position?: number]` on BeforeRequestTimelineUriEvent, drop the `as any` cast, and run npm run events-metadata.
- [index.ts:1](src/eventbus/events/index.ts#L1) **Nine event interfaces are not exported from events/index.ts** (low). The interfaces for before-request-video-url, highlight-navigation, navigate-to-video-url, push-history-state, request-current-navigation, request-current-video-position, request-video-cleanup, request-video-url and video-complete are in EventMap and used by NavigationController and RoutingController. They are not exported from the events barrel and not marked @private, so consumers cannot import their types, unlike the other 36 events. Fix: Export the nine interfaces from src/eventbus/events/index.ts, or mark them @private if they are meant to be internal. Consider having the events generator check the barrel against the files on disk.
- [is-object.ts:1](src/util/guards/is-object.ts#L1) **isObject accepts any non-array object but narrows to a plain record** (low). isObject returns true for Date, Map, DOM nodes, jQuery objects and class instances, yet narrows to Record<PropertyKey, unknown>. Callers use it as a plain-object test. resolvePropertyValues recurses into array items that pass isObject without the `instanceof $` check it applies to direct values, so a jQuery object or DOM node inside an array is walked property by property, including jQuery's expando data. Fix: Add an isPlainObject guard (prototype is Object.prototype or null) and use it in resolve-property-values and configuration-resolver. If isObject keeps its current behaviour, rename it or document it as 'non-null non-array object'. Add guard tests, since util/guards has tests only for isPromise.
- [prepare-value-for-serialization.ts:12](src/util/prepare-value-for-serialization.ts#L12) **Dead util code: applyOperation/defaultScope and prepareValueForSerialization** (low). prepareValueForSerialization, applyOperation and the shared mutable defaultScope are not used by production code or exported publicly. Only tests import them, although CLAUDE.md says actions use applyOperation. prepareValueForSerialization also crashes on null-prototype objects (value.constructor is undefined). defaultScope is a module singleton that operations mutate (whenEvaluation, variables, loop fields), so state can leak between test calls that omit a scope. Fix: Delete prepareValueForSerialization and its spec, or guard `value.constructor` if a caller is planned. Move applyOperation into src/test/fixtures and have it create a fresh scope per call instead of sharing defaultScope. Update the CLAUDE.md utilities list.
- [animation-interval.ts:37](src/util/animation-interval.ts#L37) **animationInterval leaves a pending timer and RAF after abort** (low). Abort is only checked when the next frame runs. The setTimeout and the requestAnimationFrame it queues still fire after RafPositionSource.destroy() aborts the signal. That conflicts with the 'release all resources in destroy()' rule and leaves timers for fake-timer tests and teardown checks to trip over. Fix: Store the timeout id and RAF id, and register signal.addEventListener('abort', () => { clearTimeout(timeoutId); cancelAnimationFrame(rafId); }, {once: true}). Return early if the signal is already aborted at call time. Add a test that no timers remain after abort (vi.getTimerCount() === 0).
- [locale-manager.ts:179](src/locale/locale-manager.ts#L179) **Debug interpolation check misses placeholders that rosetta supports** (low). rosetta's templite matches /{{(.*?)}}/, trims the key and resolves dot paths. The debug check only matches /\{\{(\w+)\}\}/, so `{{ name }}` and `{{user.name}}` are never reported when their params are missing. The check also uses `in`, so params' prototype members count as present. Fix: Use /\{\{(.*?)\}\}/g, trim each key, and resolve the dot path against params with own-property lookups. Report keys that resolve to undefined or null.
- [typed-event-emitter.ts:154](src/util/typed-event-emitter.ts#L154) **TypedEventEmitter swallows async handler rejections, unlike Eventbus** (low). emit() catches synchronous throws but ignores a returned promise, so an async engine event handler that rejects (for example engine.on('timelineComplete', async () => ...)) becomes an unhandled rejection. The Eventbus watches such promises after this round's fix, so the two emitters report handler failures differently. Fix: Capture the handler's return value, and if isPromise(result), attach .then(undefined, error => console.error(...)), mirroring Eventbus._watchResult. Add a spec for a rejecting async handler.

### lens-performance

- [action.ts:32](src/action/action.ts#L32) **Action logs every failure to console even when the engine error event handles it** (low). The changelog says that failures are reported through the `error` event and only logged when nobody listens. Action.start still always logs two console.error lines before rethrowing, and EndableAction.end logs its message without the error. A host that subscribes to `error` or `engine-error` cannot silence this noise, and with no listener each failure is logged three times. Fix: Remove the console.error calls from Action.start and EndableAction.end (endable-action.ts:23) and let callers report: the engine's _reportActionError, the eventbus onHandlerError for event actions, and init() rejection.

### operations-b

- [invoke-object-method.ts:69](src/operation/invoke-object-method.ts#L69) **invokeObjectMethod and math still fail with bare TypeErrors or only log to the console** (low). This is still open from the previous audit. invokeObjectMethod throws a raw 'Cannot read properties of undefined' when instance is missing. When the member is not a function it only calls console.error and continues, so a misspelled methodName passes silently and methodResult is never set. math indexes Math with an unchecked functionName. A method that returns a promise is also stored unresolved in methodResult. Fix: Throw `invokeObjectMethod: instance is required` and `invokeObjectMethod: '${methodName}' is not a function on the instance`. In math, check `typeof Math[functionName] === 'function'` and throw `math: unknown function '${functionName}'`. Consider awaiting a promise methodResult.
- [get-property-chain-value.ts:55](src/operation/helper/get-property-chain-value.ts#L55) **getPropertyChainValue ignores operationName in 'cannot be resolved' errors** (low). The function takes operationName but uses it only in the forbidden-segment error. The more common unresolvable-chain error names neither the operation nor the segment that was null or undefined, so with long chains it is unclear which step failed. Fix: Prefix the error with operationName when it is given, and name the failing segment, for example `${op}: property chain 'a.b.c' cannot be resolved: 'a.b' is undefined`.
- [modify-dimensions.ts:124](src/operation/helper/modify-dimensions.ts#L124) **modifyDimensions silently ignores bad operators and yields NaN** (low). An unknown operator prefix only calls console.error, and the unmodified dimensions are returned. A non-numeric amount turns every dimension into NaN, and a ratio without '=' throws a raw TypeError. The modifier comes from configuration (getElementDimensions), so typos surface far from their cause. Fix: Throw `modifyDimensions: invalid modifier '${modifier}'` for an unknown operator, a NaN amount or a malformed ratio, and let getElementDimensions add its own name.
- [load-json.ts:88](src/operation/load-json.ts#L88) **loadJson module cache is never cleared and clearCache is not exported** (low). jsonCache is module-level. clearGlobals now runs when the last engine is destroyed, but nothing clears jsonCache, and clearCache is not exported from src/index.ts or src/operation/index.ts. Cached responses therefore survive engine recreation for the life of the page, with no public way to invalidate them. The url is not validated either: loadJson without url fetches the relative URL 'undefined'. Fix: Export clearCache (as clearJsonCache) and call it next to clearGlobals in EngineFactory's last-engine teardown. Throw `loadJson: url is required` for an empty url, as the http-* operations do.

### timeline-providers-adapters

- [raf-position-source.ts:81](src/timelineproviders/position-sources/raf-position-source.ts#L81) **Default RAF tickInterval of 1000 ms contradicts the 0.1 s action precision** (medium). With the default 1000 ms tick, positions are whole seconds. Actions keyed at 0.1 s precision (the documented granularity) fire up to 0.9 s late at the next whole-second tick. A duration that is not a multiple of the tick overshoots: duration 10.5 reports its end at 11 s. Configuration docs and examples also use 1000, so users get coarse timing without knowing it.
