Fix: Default to 100 ms (or frame-rate updates with millisecond rounding), document the trade-off on IPositionSourceConfig.tickInterval, and report the end exactly when the session's elapsed time crosses durationMs instead of on the next tick.
- [keyboard-step-source.ts:165](src/timelineproviders/position-sources/keyboard-step-source.ts#L165) **KeyboardStepSource silently binds keys globally when targetElement matches nothing** (low). If targetElement is a selector that matches nothing, doInit resolves and _targetElement stays undefined. _bindKeys then binds on the document, so arrow, Home and End keys are captured and preventDefault'ed across the whole page. Scroll and video sources reject init for a missing selector, and the project rule is that invalid configuration fails fast. Fix: Reject doInit with `KeyboardStepSource: target element not found for selector '<sel>'` when a configured selector does not resolve, matching ScrollPositionSource and VideoPositionSource.
- [engine-input-adapter.ts:51](src/adapters/engine-input-adapter.ts#L51) **EngineInputAdapter's space hotkey is page-global and fires for every engine** (medium). Space is bound on the document in the 'all' scope with no element scope and no opt-out. Every engine on a page registers its own binding, so one key press toggles all of them. The handler always calls preventDefault and returns false, so space no longer scrolls the page (which matters for ScrollPositionSource timelines) and no longer activates a focused button outside the engine root. Fix: Scope the binding to the engine root (hotkeys `element` option, or ignore events whose target is outside engineRoot or is a button or link). Let configuration disable or rebind it. Skip preventDefault when the engine has no active source.
- [engine-eventbus-adapter.ts:26](src/adapters/engine-eventbus-adapter.ts#L26) **Adapter connect() is not idempotent; a second call re-creates double dispatch** (low). A second connect() subscribes every engine event and eventbus command again, so each command runs twice and each event is broadcast twice. play-toggle then cancels itself, which is the defect the breaking change in this release exists to remove. The CHANGELOG now tells direct users to call connect() themselves, which makes this easier to hit. LocaleEventbusAdapter has the same issue. Fix: Track a `_connected` flag: make connect() a no-op (or throw) when already connected, and let disconnect() clear it. Apply the same to LocaleEventbusAdapter and EngineInputAdapter, and add a 'connect twice, broadcast once' test.
- [engine-eventbus-adapter.ts:181](src/adapters/engine-eventbus-adapter.ts#L181) **Failed eventbus-driven engine commands only reach console.error** (low). Rejections from start(), seek() and switchTimeline() started through the eventbus are only logged, for example blocked autoplay, a video that fails to load on switch, or a playlist selectItem throw. The release added the engine-error event so applications can observe failures, but these command failures never reach it. UI code that sends timeline-play-request cannot learn that autoplay was blocked. Fix: Broadcast these failures through the eventbus, either as engine-error with a command-error payload (widen the event type) or as a dedicated `engine-command-error` event carrying the command name and error. Keep console.error as the fallback when there are no listeners.
- [simple-playlist.ts:118](src/timelineproviders/playlist/simple-playlist.ts#L118) **SimplePlaylist is inert, and selectItem() can abort switchTimeline partway through** (low). Nothing in the engine or factory subscribes to onItemChange, and VideoPositionSource cannot change its sources (the factory shares one video source across all mediaplayer timelines). Configuring a playlist therefore changes nothing that plays. The engine calls `selectItem(uri)` with the timeline URI, and selectItem throws on an unknown identifier. A playlist keyed by anything other than every timeline URI (the doc example uses 'id') makes switchTimeline throw after the previous timeline was cleaned up and timelineChange was emitted, but before the new source is activated. Fix: Make the contract explicit: either have the engine check the identifier before tearing down the old timeline, or make selectItem non-throwing (return a boolean) for identifiers it does not know. Document that IPlaylist has no built-in consumer, or wire onItemChange to a source API that can change media.
- [base-position-source.ts:97](src/timelineproviders/position-sources/base-position-source.ts#L97) **destroy() is not terminal and callbacks cannot be unregistered** (low). IPositionSource says 'After calling destroy, the source cannot be reused', but nothing enforces it. A destroyed RafPositionSource can be activated and ticks again. A destroyed KeyboardStepSource re-binds global hotkeys on activate. A destroyed ScrollPositionSource reports 'active' with no listener. onPosition, onBoundaryReached and onActivated return void and the arrays are never cleared, so consumers cannot detach and destroyed sources keep engine closures alive. Fix: Add a `_destroyed` flag. Make activate() and seek() reject or no-op after destroy, clear the three callback arrays in destroy(), and have the on* methods return an unsubscribe function (a compatible widening of the return type).

### configuration-tools

- [configuration-resolver.ts:70](src/configuration/configuration-resolver.ts#L70) **Resolver lacks structural validation (missing lists, empty provider settings, durations)** (low). The only structural check is isDefined(timelineProviderSettings), so `{}` passes even though the message promises 'at least one'. A JSON config without timelines, actions or initActions fails with a TypeError from `.map`. Durations are not validated: TimelineActionEditor.setDuration rejects start > end, but TimelineActionCreator.addDuration and the resolver accept it, and a timeline type with no matching provider settings is not caught here. Fix: Validate up front with JSON paths: the required arrays are arrays; timelineProviderSettings has an entry for every timelines[i].type; durations are numbers with 0 <= start <= end. Add the same start/end check to TimelineActionCreator.addDuration.
- [configuration-resolver.ts:55](src/configuration/configuration-resolver.ts#L55) **process() mutates the input config; resolved config is unfrozen and shares cssFiles** (low). Placeholder resolution rewrites the input IEngineConfiguration in place (the tests assert this). Imported json: objects are inserted by reference. Processing the same object twice, for example recreating an engine, walks into those importer-owned objects with a fresh WeakSet and rewrites their 'config:'/'template:' strings. resolvedConfig.cssFiles is the same array as the input's, and nothing is frozen, despite the documented 'immutable after resolution'. Fix: Resolve placeholders on a deepCopy of the input and deep-copy imported json values. Copy cssFiles, and consider freezing the plain-data parts of IResolvedEngineConfiguration (or typing them Readonly).
- [action-creator-factory.ts:35](src/configuration/api/action-creator-factory.ts#L35) **Fluent API allows duplicate action names that the resolver later rejects** (low). The resolver now throws on duplicate names across actions and eventActions, but createAction, createEventAction, setName and addAction do not check. The fluent API therefore builds configs that fail only at createEngine, far from the call site. Fix: In ConfigurationFactory._internalAddAction (and ActionCreator/ActionEditor.setName for those lists), throw `Action name '${name}' already exists in actions/eventActions` with the same rule as createActionsLookup.
- [action-creator-factory.ts:266](src/configuration/api/action-creator-factory.ts#L266) **Fluent typing: addEndOperation data unchecked per op; name providers mistyped** (low). addEndOperation declares a generic T but types its parameter as TOperationName, so T is never inferred. operationData is then checked against the union of all operation data types instead of the named operation's (same in EndableActionEditor.addEndOperation). ActionEditor.addStartOperation takes untyped TOperationData. OperationNamesProvider.getOperationNames(), ControllerNamesProvider.getControllerNames() and OperationEditor.getOperationDataKeys() return arrays but are typed as a single name or key. Fix: Type the parameter as `systemName: T` in both addEndOperation overloads. Give ActionEditor.addStartOperation the same generics as ActionCreator. Return TOperationName[], TControllerName[] and Array<keyof ...> from the providers and getOperationDataKeys.
- [configuration-factory.ts:47](src/configuration/api/configuration-factory.ts#L47) **ConfigurationFactory TypeDoc example calls methods that do not exist** (low). The class-level @example chains `.addProvider("animation").setSystemName(...).setVendor("eligius")`, but TimelineProviderSettingsEditor has neither method. The extension example passes `{ element: 'img', ... }` to createElement, whose property is `elementName`. Copying the published docs gives code that does not compile. Fix: Update the example to `.editTimelineProviderSettings().addProvider('animation', 'RafPositionSource').setContainer('#render-area').end()` and use `elementName: 'img'` in the createElement example.
- [metadata-generator.ts:285](src/tools/code-generator/metadata-generator.ts#L285) **Metadata generators are triplicated, leave stale outputs and do not escape descriptions** (low). metadata-generator.ts and ctrls-metadata-generator.ts duplicate toPathAlias, toImport, toOperationDataType, getTag and getPropertiesWith(out)Tags, and the copies have already drifted (the second toImport fallback exists only in the operation generator). getDescription escapes backticks but not '${' or backslashes, so a JSDoc mentioning a template literal or regex silently changes or breaks the generated code. Operations typed with TOperationData get `IOperationMetadata<undefined>`. Output files of renamed or removed operations are never deleted, and since they are gitignored but type-checked they can break typecheck. Fix: Extract the shared helpers into src/tools/code-generator/shared.ts with unit tests. Escape descriptions with JSON.stringify or a full template-literal escape (\\, `, ${). Emit `IOperationMetadata<TOperationData>` when there is no data type. Empty the metadata directory (except types.ts) before generating.
- [generate-event-interfaces.ts:105](src/tools/code-generator/generate-event-interfaces.ts#L105) **Remove obsolete one-shot scripts that can overwrite maintained sources** (low). generate-event-interfaces.ts is a leftover migration that writes event files from a hard-coded table. Running it, for example with `tsx`, overwrites the hand-maintained events with stale content: request-current-language goes back to `language: string`, and the docs are lost. uvu2vitest-codemod.ts (`npm run codemod`) rewrites every spec file and saves. Neither has a purpose any more. Fix: Delete generate-event-interfaces.ts, uvu2vitest-codemod.ts and the `codemod` npm script, or move them to an archive outside src/.
- [generate-schemas.ts:428](src/tools/schema-tools/generate-schemas.ts#L428) **Configuration schema hard-codes root `required` and forces UUID operation ids** (low). postProcessConfigSchema overwrites the required list that ts-json-schema-generator derived from IEngineConfiguration with a hand-maintained copy, so it will drift when the interface changes. Every operation schema requires `id` to match a UUID pattern but does not require `id`. The TS type requires `id: string`, and the resolver and the repo's own tests accept ids like 'op-id'. Fix: Keep the generator's `required`. Make `id` required with type string, and either drop the UUID pattern or document and enforce it consistently in the resolver.

## Optimisations

- [progressbar-controller.ts:84](src/controllers/progressbar-controller.ts#L84) **ProgressbarController rewrites the text node on every tick** (low, controllers). _positionUpdateHandler runs on every 'timeline-time' (once per RAF frame for animation timelines). It calls textElement.text(), which empties and re-appends a text node even when Math.floor(percentage) has not changed. That is about 99% of frames, and each one is a DOM mutation (it also wakes any MutationObserverController on an ancestor) on the tick path that must stay under 16ms. Fix: Cache the last rendered integer percentage (and optionally the last width rounded to 0.1%) and write to the DOM only when it changes.
- [subtitles-controller.ts:96](src/controllers/subtitles-controller.ts#L96) **SubtitlesController scans all subtitle ranges on every tick** (low, controllers). onTimeHandler runs per 'timeline-time' (every frame) and does a linear find over all ranges, although playback almost always stays in the current range or moves to the next one. Fix: Keep the index of the last matched range. Check it and its neighbour first, and fall back to a binary search over ranges sorted by start (only needed after seeks).
- [eligius-engine.ts:1090](src/eligius-engine.ts#L1090) **Per-tick allocations in _dispatchActionsBetween even when nothing is crossed** (low, engine-core). On every position update (60/s with RAF, and on every video timeupdate), dispatch allocates two arrays and a closure before finding out whether the [from, to] slice contains any events. In most ticks it contains none. Fix: Compute `upperBound(events, from)` and `upperBound(events, to)` first, return early when they are equal, and only then allocate `ends`, `starts` and the closure.
- [eligius-engine.ts:1099](src/eligius-engine.ts#L1099) **Large forward jumps run start and end chains for every skipped action in one frame** (medium, lens-performance). Hot path: the RAF frame after a background tab becomes visible again, or a big timeupdate or scroll jump. Interval crossing turns every action in (from, to] into start plus end, and all those chains start in one rAF callback. The cost is O(k) operation chains, each typically doing selectElement (two DOM queries) plus DOM writes and animations, all in one task. This easily breaks the 16 ms frame budget, and the k actions' animations run on top of each other. Fix: Treat a forward jump larger than a threshold as a seek. The threshold could be a few tickIntervals, or a configurable maxCatchUp on the timeline. For such a jump, run reconciliation (end non-covering, start covering) instead of replaying every skipped range. Make it opt-out for configurations that rely on every action running.
- [eligius-engine.ts:291](src/eligius-engine.ts#L291) **Engine root is re-queried from the document on every selectElement** (low, lens-performance). Hot path: every action start or end that begins with selectElement. Each call goes request() -> adapter responder -> engine.engineRoot, which builds a new jQuery object with a document-level query of containerSelector before the scoped find. The container element never changes after _createLayoutTemplate (only its innerHTML does). Each selectElement therefore does one avoidable document query and one jQuery allocation, which adds up to k extra queries for a burst of k actions (loop wrap, backward jump, catch-up after a skipped range). Fix: Cache the jQuery root in _createLayoutTemplate (it already computes `container`), return the cached instance from engineRoot and _handleRequestEngineRoot, and clear it in destroy().
- [select-element.ts:70](src/operation/select-element.ts#L70) **selectElement re-queries the document for the engine root on every call** (low, operations-b). Hot path: this runs on every selectElement execution, which is the first operation of most action starts and ends during playback and seeks. The request-engine-root responder returns engine.engineRoot, which runs $(containerSelector), a full document query, before root.find(selector). The root does not change after init. This is still open from the previous audit. Fix: Cache the jQuery root once in the engine after the layout template is created, and return that cached object from engineRoot and the request-engine-root responder.
