## Expansion

### Proposer assessments

- **maturity-skeptic**: Skeptic's verdict: the engine is mature. After the audit fix release it needs fixes, tests, docs and small tweaks, not new features.  Evidence that the feature set is complete: - **Catalogues.** There are about 75 operations (src/operation/index.ts) covering DOM, data, control flow (when/otherwise/forEach with break and continue), forms, storage, HTTP, scroll, focus/a11y, text, arrays and dates. There are 8 controllers (src/controllers/index.ts), about 45 typed events (src/eventbus/events/index.ts), 5 position sources (Raf, Video, Scroll, KeyboardStep and the Base class), a container provider, a playlist, a rosetta locale system, a fluent API that now also loads in Node (`eligius/configuration-api`), and a DOM-free metadata entry. - **The previous audit's expansions have landed.** The CHANGELOG "Unreleased" section covers interval-crossing dispatch, the `engine-error` event naming the failing operation, fail-fast unknown names with "did you mean" hints, lazy optional peers, per-timeline position sources and durations, and controller cleanup on destroy. These were the structural gaps. What is left is polish. - **History.** The CHANGELOG from 2.0 to Unreleased shows consolidation, not missing features. The spec dirs 001 to 005 are all refactors or catalogue fills, and none lists an unmet user story. The roughly 20 sources in specs/position-source-ideas.md (Mouse, Gyro, Gamepad, WebSocket and others) have no user story, issue or consumer behind them. Each one can be written outside the core today, because position sources, containers and playlists are resolved by `systemName` through a pluggable IResourceImporter (src/engine-factory.ts createPositionSourceForTimeline). - **The main consumer is not blocked.** Eligian (F:\projects\eligius\eligian, config-builder.ts generateTimelineProviderSettings) only emits RafPositionSource and VideoPositionSource and uses none of the newer sources. More catalogue in Eligius would not reach DSL users until Eligian catches up.  Expansions I argued away: 1. **Custom-operation registry** (the open half of last audit's #3). The extension point already exists. A custom IResourceImporter that delegates to EligiusResourceImporter takes about ten lines, and ConfigurationResolver now validates against `importer.getOperationNames()` with a JSON path (configuration-resolver.ts createUnknownOperationMessage). `customFunction` covers app-specific code from pure configuration. The only gap is that the fluent API checks names against generated metadata (operation-lookup.ts isKnownOperationName), but Eligian validates against its own generated registry anyway, so a registry in Eligius alone would not help DSL authors. It would change my mind if an integrator shows a real configuration that `customFunction` cannot express, or Eligian adds a custom-operation import. 2. **URI on timeline-complete / branching.** A pure-configuration workaround exists. An event action on `timeline-current-timeline-change` can store the URI with setGlobalData, and the complete handler can test it with `when`. 3. **Progress-driven actions and the speculative sources.** No demand signal, and per-tick author code puts the 16ms budget at risk.  What remains is fix and cleanup work, not expansion: - `ITimelineFlow = Record<string, never>` (configuration/types.ts:132) and the do-nothing EligiusFlowController are dead configuration surface. Deprecate them. - The README timeline provider section does not list KeyboardStepSource. - The `type` doc comment says `'animation' (RAF-based)`, but animation can also be Scroll or Keyboard. - About 25 low-severity items from the report, and the stale test-gap list.  I list one real expansion (the per-timeline source override). I cannot argue it away, because the config model makes a class of documented sources unusable in multi-chapter stories. I also list one small host tweak that was deferred last audit and never landed. Neither is urgent. The engine can ship the current release as is.

- **author-first**: The engine is mature in breadth, and the core loop is now sound. The catalogue is about 75 operations (src/operation/index.ts), 8 controllers (src/controllers/index.ts), about 45 typed events, and 4 position sources plus a container provider and a playlist. The Unreleased section of CHANGELOG.md fixes the dispatch problems that blocked authors last time: interval-crossing dispatch, the error event, fail-fast names, per-timeline durations and lazy peers. More operations, controllers or speculative position sources would not remove a real pain. Nothing in specs/position-source-ideas.md has a user story behind it, and every source there can be built outside the core because sources resolve by systemName.  What remains for authors comes from how configuration is shaped, not from missing features. I checked this against the Eligian compiler in F:\projects\eligius\eligian. Three gaps are real and keep coming up:  (1) Position source settings exist only per timeline TYPE (src/configuration/types.ts:43, src/engine-factory.ts:514). Video file, scroll container and keyboard chapter markers belong to a single timeline, but authors can only set them once per type. Effects: - A config cannot play two different videos. All mediaplayer timelines share one VideoPositionSource built from one `sources` list (engine-factory.ts:521). Nothing connects SimplePlaylist.onItemChange to the player, even though README.md:97-99 shows a two-video playlist. - A config cannot mix a scroll chapter with an autoplay chapter. - Eligian works around this. It writes `selector: ''` with a TODO and no `sources` (eligian config-builder.ts:341-350). It also puts the video file into timeline.uri, which Eligius never reads (timeline-transformer.ts:131), and it quietly maps its `custom` provider to RAF.  (2) `when` only takes one binary comparison (src/operation/when.ts:16, :97). Eligian's grammar allows &&, ||, ! and arithmetic (eligian.langium:622-628) and turns non-literal expressions into strings that `when` cannot parse. Separately, Eligian sends the condition under the key `condition`, not `expression` (operation-transformer.ts:402-407). That is an Eligian bug to fix in that repo.  (3) Timeline actions only fire at their start and end. Nothing ties a visual property to timeline progress, so ScrollPositionSource and video overlays can only toggle state. Smooth scrubbing needs customFunction or a heavy eventAction on timeline-time. Even then, customFunction cannot be reached through the default importer (src/importer/eligius-resource-importer.ts:20).  Verdict: mature, plus a small, targeted expansion. Proposal 1 is the priority because it fixes things that are documented but do not work. Proposals 2 and 4 are small. Proposal 3 is the only new capability, and it is built as a controller with no engine change.  Considered and not proposed: - Adding the URI to timeline-complete. A timeline action placed at the end of each timeline already chains chapters. - Engine-state read operations. The request events already exist. - New position sources and new operations.  One cleanup worth doing: deprecate the dead `timelineFlow` key (ITimelineFlow = Record<string, never> at configuration/types.ts:132; EligiusFlowController does nothing). It shows authors a schema key that has no effect.

- **integrator-first**: Mature in features, not yet mature for embedding. I do not recommend new operations, controllers, position sources or subsystems. The catalogue is complete for its purpose: about 72 operations, 8 controllers, 4 position sources, a typed eventbus, a fluent API and DOM-free metadata and configuration-api entries. The Unreleased section already fixes the big integration problems from the last audit: lazy optional peers, the engine error event, fail-fast unknown names, the Node-safe configuration-api entry, controller cleanup on destroy, and globals cleared when the last engine is destroyed.  What still makes embedding brittle is a small set of host-coexistence and lifecycle gaps. I checked each one against the current code and, where I could, with a short probe spec that I ran and then deleted.  (1) Calling destroy() while init() is still pending does not cancel init. A factory-built RAF engine destroyed right after init() (React StrictMode, HMR, a fast route change) carries on and runs its initActions after teardown. In one probe init resolved as if it had succeeded. In another, init().then(start) rejected with "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'html')".  (2) The engine finds its root with a document-wide jQuery selector (eligius-engine.ts:290-291, :546-548, :723, :754, :815). Providers also use document-wide selectors (dom-container-provider.ts:75, scroll-position-source.ts:85, video-position-source.ts:151/273/411, keyboard-step-source.ts:166). So a host cannot mount into a React ref or a shadow root, and two instances of one presentation collide on IDs from the layoutTemplate.  (3) EngineInputAdapter always binds a page-wide space key (engine-input-adapter.ts:51), and IEngineFactoryOptions accepts only `eventbus` (types.ts:148-150). In the probe, a space keydown on document.body started playback. The previous judge planned to fold this into the adapter rewiring, but the rewiring shipped without it.  (4) There is still no supported way to register custom operations or functions. The fluent API rejects every name that is not in the generated metadata (operation-lookup.ts:25). The README (line 38) does not say how to make customFunction work.  (5) The published .d.mts files are not self-contained. All three entries import types from the optional peer lottie-web, and they use the global JQuery type 44 to 51 times while @types/jquery is only a devDependency. A minimal Node consumer of 'eligius/configuration-api' with skipLibCheck:false fails with TS2307 (lottie-web) and many TS2304 (JQuery) errors. With skipLibCheck:true those types silently become any.  Things I considered and do not propose: - An SSR-safe main entry. jQuery 4 throws "jQuery requires a window with a document" when imported in Node. Fixing that means a lazily bound jQuery factory, which splits the host's and the engine's jQuery instances. The workaround is cheap (a dynamic import or next/dynamic with ssr:false), and the README documents it. - Removing jQuery. Large and breaking, for little gain. - React/Vue wrappers. A thin useEffect around createEngine/destroy. They would only be safe after proposal 1. - Tree-shaking. Configuration-driven name resolution needs the whole catalogue, and a host can already pass a curated importer. - Action-level lifecycle events. Devtools and diagnostics were removed on purpose in 2.0, and broadcastEvent plus engine-error cover the real needs. - A per-engine globals store. Clearing on the last destroy is already in.  Verdict: mature. Ship the five small-to-medium proposals below as integration hardening. No new feature surface.

### Recommended by the judge

1. **Optional per-timeline positionSource override, merged over the type default** (medium; source: author-first #1 and maturity-skeptic #1 (merged); author-first's video src-swap half deferred)
   - Why: Verified: settings exist only per timeline type (configuration/types.ts:43) and the factory builds every timeline's source from timelineProviderSettings[timeline.type] (engine-factory.ts:514). So a configuration cannot mix a RAF intro with a scroll or keyboard chapter, cannot scroll two different containers, and all mediaplayer timelines share one video list even though README shows a two-video playlist. Scroll and Keyboard sources are documented as first-class but are effectively single-use per config. The main consumer (Eligian) picks a provider per timeline and has to collapse it to the type. This is documented-but-unusable functionality, not a speculative feature. Scope for this cycle: add `positionSource?: Partial<IPositionSourceConfig>` to ITimelineConfiguration, merge it over the type settings in createTimelineProvidersByUri, keep the intrinsic-container sharing rule for video, add a fluent setter, regenerate the schema. Defer the video.js src-swap (setSources) to a later cycle; it rides on a switch path that is still being fixed.
   - Prerequisites: All engine-core switch/seek bugs: fail-fast reconcile in seek/switchTimeline (eligius-engine.ts:1177, :1185), unserialised seek/switch/stop (:438), failed source init cached as prepared and URI switched before activation (:620), timelineChange emitted before the provider is active (:497), stray 'start' after pause during activate (:367). Provider bugs: stale activate() rejection overwriting newer state (base-position-source.ts:130), scroll seek ignored while inactive (scroll-position-source.ts:148), scroll smooth-scroll seek/loop (:157, :200), keyboard hotkeys scope takeover (keyboard-step-source.ts:249). Also the destroy-during-pending-init/switch cancellation from lens-lifecycle-memory. Integration tests through a factory-built engine switching RAF->Scroll->RAF and back must exist before this lands.
2. **Configurable input bindings: disable, rebind or scope the global space toggle and the resize listener** (small; source: integrator-first #3 and maturity-skeptic #2 (merged))
   - Why: Verified: _registerHotkeys always calls hotkeys('space', ...) in the page-wide 'all' scope (engine-input-adapter.ts:51), EngineFactory always connects the adapter, and IEngineFactoryOptions accepts only `eventbus` (types.ts:148-150). A host embedding Eligius as a widget loses space for page scroll and focused buttons, and N engines on a page all toggle on one press. This was deferred by the previous judge into the adapter rewiring and never shipped. Default behaviour stays identical; additive option on IEngineFactoryOptions.
   - Prerequisites: KeyboardStepSource global hotkeys scope reset (keyboard-step-source.ts:249), so the two hotkeys users agree on scope handling. Check hotkeys-js `element`/`scope` option semantics against the version in package.json via Context7 before relying on them.
3. **Supported registration of custom operations, functions, templates and JSON on EligiusResourceImporter and the fluent API** (small; source: author-first #4 and integrator-first #4 (merged))
   - Why: Verified: EligiusResourceImporter has a no-argument constructor and its own JSDoc (eligius-resource-importer.ts:21) says customFunction, getImport, template: and json: need a whole custom IResourceImporter; operation-lookup.ts:25 throws `Unknown operation` for any name outside generated metadata, so the fluent API and eligius/configuration-api cannot author a custom operation at all. This is the unshipped half of last audit's expansion 3; the fail-fast half only makes the gap louder. Additive: optional constructor map checked after built-ins (no shadowing without explicit override), reflected in getOperationNames() so the resolver's did-you-mean includes custom names, plus an optional extra-names parameter on ConfigurationFactory and a README 'Extending Eligius' section.
   - Prerequisites: Resolver accepting any function export, including classes, as an operation (configuration-resolver.ts:211) must be fixed so registered entries are shape-checked the same way; createEngine leaking its interceptor and listener when resolution throws (engine-factory.ts:184); event actions staying live when process() throws (configuration-resolver.ts:169).
4. **Self-contained type declarations for all three package entries** (small; source: integrator-first #5)
   - Why: Verified: dist/index.d.mts, dist/metadata.d.mts and dist/configuration-api.d.mts each begin with `import {...} from "lottie-web"`, while lottie-web became an optional peer this release; the files also rely on the global JQuery type with @types/jquery only a devDependency. A Node consumer of the DOM-free entries with skipLibCheck:false gets TS2307/TS2304 errors, and with skipLibCheck:true every JQuery-typed member silently becomes any. This hits exactly the DOM-free entries that were just added for Node build tools and the Eligian compiler. Fix: local structural renderer types in controllers/types.ts with the lottie import confined to lottie-controller.ts, a jquery types reference plus optional @types/jquery peer for the main entry, structural aliases in the DOM-free entries, and a CI type-check of a fixture consumer per entry.
   - Prerequisites: None functional. Must diff `npm run metadata` and `npm run generate-schema` output before and after to prove the generated metadata and JSON schema are unchanged.
5. **Compound conditions in `when`: &&, ||, !, parentheses via a small safe parser** (small; source: author-first #2)
   - Why: Evidence: TExpression is `${TValue}${TOperator}${TValue}` (when.ts:16) and splitExpression throws on anything but one binary comparison (when.ts:97-117), so a range check or a two-flag branch needs nested when/endWhen blocks and an 'or' needs duplicated operation lists or customFunction. The Eligian grammar already accepts && || and ! and serialises non-literal compounds into strings the runtime cannot parse, so the DSL can express conditions the engine rejects. The parser reuses resolveExternalPropertyChain so the prototype-pollution guards hold; no eval. Arithmetic is explicitly out of scope because '-' is ambiguous with property chains. Ranked last because it is a convenience, not a correctness gap, and the when operation itself has an open bug.
   - Prerequisites: The `when` operand bug where true/false/null/@var/typos are compared as literal strings (when.ts:134) must be fixed first and its operand grammar folded into the new parser so there is one tokenizer. The when/forEach child-scope bugs for setVariable (set-variable.ts:29) and setData (set-data.ts:88) must land so conditions on variables written inside blocks actually see the values. The Eligian `condition` vs `expression` key mismatch is an Eligian bug and is not an Eligius prerequisite.

### Rejected by the judge

- **Destroy-safe async lifecycle: destroy() cancels pending init/start/switch (integrator-first #1)**: Real and important, but it is a lifecycle defect already described under lens-lifecycle-memory, not a feature; it goes into the fix pass as a prerequisite for the per-timeline override, with the exported EngineDestroyedError as the one API addition.
- **Mount into a host element and resolve provider selectors within the engine root (integrator-first #2)**: The gap is genuine (document-wide $(containerSelector) at eligius-engine.ts:291, :548, :723, :754, :815), but it is a medium-effort change across every provider whose main payoff, several engines on one page, is already defeated by the confirmed shared-responder (engine-eventbus-adapter.ts:168) and shared _actionsLookup (engine-factory.ts:197) bugs; fix those and revisit next audit.
- **Scrub controller mapping timeline progress to a CSS custom property (author-first #3)**: No user, issue or consumer asked for it, Eligian does not emit anything like it, and the per-tick listener cost is best weighed after the RAF clock timing bugs (raf-position-source.ts:177, :193) are fixed and measured.
- **VideoPositionSource.setSources src-swap on a shared player (author-first #1, video half)**: Deferred, not dropped: it depends on the switch path and on video init settling that still have open medium bugs (video-position-source.ts:346, eligius-engine.ts:620), so it should follow the per-timeline override in a later cycle once that path is tested.
- **Deprecate ITimelineFlow and EligiusFlowController (noted by author-first and maturity-skeptic)**: Correct observation but it is a cleanup for the low-severity list, not an expansion; do it alongside the README fixes (list KeyboardStepSource, stop calling 'animation' RAF-based).

### Full proposal details

<details><summary>maturity-skeptic (2 proposals)</summary>

#### Optional per-timeline positionSource override (finish expansion 5)

- Problem: Position source settings are still keyed only by timeline type: `TTimelineProviderSettings = Partial<Record<TimelineTypes, ITimelineProviderSettings>>` (src/configuration/types.ts:43). The fix release gives each non-intrinsic timeline its own source instance, but `createTimelineProvidersByUri` (src/engine-factory.ts, around line 505) builds every one from the same `timelineProviderSettings[timeline.type]`. ScrollPositionSource takes its scrollable `selector` from those type-level settings (scroll-position-source.ts:12, :85). As a result, (a) one configuration cannot mix an autoplay RAF intro with a scroll-driven or keyboard-stepped chapter, because all three are 'animation', and (b) a scroll story whose chapters scroll different containers is impossible, because every animation timeline scrolls the same selector. ScrollPositionSource and KeyboardStepSource are presented as first-class in README.md:75 and CHANGELOG 2.1.0, but in practice each config can use only one of them, and only for every animation timeline. The only workaround is two engines on one page, which share hotkeys-js space bindings and the global data store. The main consumer's DSL already chooses the provider per timeline (`'timeline' name 'in' selector 'using' provider` in eligian/packages/language/src/eligian.langium:479, with raf|video|audio|custom). Eligian has to collapse that to the type, and it maps `custom` silently to RAF (config-builder.ts mapProviderToTimelineType).
- Who benefits: Authors of multi-chapter, scroll-driven or presentation-style stories, and Eligian, which could map `using scroll` or `using custom` per timeline instead of collapsing to the type.
- Design: Add an optional `positionSource?: IPositionSourceConfig` to ITimelineConfiguration in src/configuration/types.ts, and possibly `containerProvider?` later, not now. In EngineFactory.createTimelineProvidersByUri, when `timeline.positionSource` is present, build that timeline's source from it via the existing createPositionSourceForTimeline, passing a synthetic settings object. Otherwise keep today's fallback to the type settings. Keep the existing sharing rule for sources with an intrinsic container (hasIntrinsicContainer, used by VideoPositionSource), so one video.js player is still shared. ConfigurationResolver resolves the systemName through the importer and fails fast with the JSON path. createTimelineProvidersForConfig still requires type settings for at least the first timeline of each type, or relax it so a type whose timelines all carry overrides needs no type entry. Run generate-schema. Add an `addTimeline` option or `setTimelinePositionSource` on ConfigurationFactory. No new operations, events or controllers. The engine already looks providers up by URI.
- Effort: medium; breaking: false; risk: Low to medium. The switchTimeline path between sources of different kinds (RAF to Scroll) has to be covered by integration tests through a factory-built engine: activate, suspend and deactivate on switch-back, and hotkeys scopes when KeyboardStepSource is involved. Evidence that would make me drop it: Eligian decides not to expose scroll or keyboard sources, and no Eligius user asks for mixed-clock stories. Then the right change is only the doc fixes (README lists KeyboardStepSource; the `type` doc comment stops saying RAF-based).

#### Host-configurable input adapter: disable or scope the space toggle (tweak)

- Problem: This is a tweak, not an expansion. Last audit's judge deferred it to the fix pass, but it never landed. src/adapters/engine-input-adapter.ts _registerHotkeys always binds `hotkeys('space', ...)` in hotkeys-js's page-wide 'all' scope. The handler calls `preventDefault()` and toggles playback. IEngineFactoryOptions (src/types.ts:148) only accepts `eventbus`, and EngineFactory.createEngine always connects the adapter. A host page therefore loses the space key for page scroll and for activating focused buttons, and with two engines on one page a single press toggles both. The resize listener cannot be turned off either.
- Who benefits: Integrators embedding Eligius as one widget in a larger app, pages with more than one engine, and hosts with their own keyboard handling.
- Design: Extend IEngineFactoryOptions with `input?: {togglePlayKey?: string | false; scope?: string; element?: HTMLElement; resize?: boolean}`. Pass it to the EngineInputAdapter constructor. Skip the binding when the key is `false`, and use hotkeys-js's scope or element options when given. The default stays exactly as today. Add tests in src/test/unit/adapters/ and a CHANGELOG 'Added' line.
- Effort: small; breaking: false; risk: Low. The default behaviour does not change. Element scoping relies on hotkeys-js's element option, so check the installed version's docs via Context7 first. It must coexist with KeyboardStepSource's own hotkeys scope switching.

</details>

<details><summary>author-first (4 proposals)</summary>

#### Per-timeline position source settings (merged over the type default)

- Problem: Settings that belong to a single timeline can only be set per type. TTimelineProviderSettings = Partial<Record<TimelineTypes, ...>> (src/configuration/types.ts:43), and EngineFactory.createTimelineProvidersByUri reads timelineProviderSettings[timeline.type] for every timeline (src/engine-factory.ts:514). Mediaplayer timelines all share one VideoPositionSource (engine-factory.ts:521, hasIntrinsicContainer) built from one `sources` list (video-position-source.ts:67, :346). Consequences: (a) Two mediaplayer timelines cannot play two different videos. switchTimeline only calls playlist.selectItem (eligius-engine.ts:504), nothing subscribes SimplePlaylist.onItemChange to the player, and README.md:97-99 nevertheless shows a two-video playlist. (b) Two scroll chapters cannot scroll different containers, and a scroll chapter cannot sit next to a RAF chapter, because both are 'animation'. (c) KeyboardStepSource `markers` (keyboard-step-source.ts:48) are chapter positions of one timeline, but they can only be set once per type. The Eligian compiler shows the pain directly. It emits mediaplayer settings with `selector: ''` and a TODO and no `sources` (eligian config-builder.ts:341-350). It puts the `from "x.mp4"` file into timeline.uri, which Eligius never reads (timeline-transformer.ts:131). Its `custom` provider silently becomes RAF (config-builder.ts:300-308). The DSL cannot offer `using scroll` or `using keyboard` per timeline.
- Who benefits: Authors of multi-chapter stories: video chapters with different files, a scroll-driven section next to an autoplay intro, and per-chapter keyboard markers. The Eligian DSL could then compile `using video from "x.mp4"` correctly and add scroll and keyboard providers.
- Design: Configuration: add optional `positionSource?: Partial<IPositionSourceConfig>` (and optionally `container?: IContainerProviderConfig`) to ITimelineConfiguration and IResolvedTimelineConfiguration. Factory: in createTimelineProvidersByUri, when a timeline has an override, build its source from `{...typeSettings.positionSource, ...timeline.positionSource, duration}` through the existing createPositionSourceForTimeline. A timeline may also define `positionSource.systemName` with no type-level setting at all. Timelines without an override keep today's behaviour exactly, including the shared video player. Video: when an override changes only `sources` and keeps the shared player's selector, give VideoPositionSource a `setSources(sources): Promise<void>` that swaps `src` on the existing player. The engine calls it from _activateProvider or switchTimeline. When the selector differs, build a separate instance. No new events or operations. Fluent API: add setPositionSource/setContainer to the timeline editor in src/configuration/api. Run npm run generate-schema. Document the merge rule in CHANGELOG and README. Eligian can then emit per-timeline `sources`.
- Effort: medium; breaking: false; risk: Medium. The switch path has needed several fixes already. Swapping the source on a shared video.js player has to keep the lazy-load, readiness ('playing') and seek-settling fixes. Lifecycle tests must cover switching back and forth between overridden and default timelines through a factory-built engine. Per-timeline video instances use more resources, which is why the shared player with a src swap is the default.

#### Compound conditions in `when`: &&, ||, !, parentheses (safe parser, no eval)

- Problem: `when` accepts exactly one comparison. Its type is `${TValue}${TOperator}${TValue}` (src/operation/when.ts:16), and splitExpression splits on the first operator and throws on anything else (when.ts:97-117). A range check (position between 10 and 12) or a multi-flag branch (answered and score >= 5, or skipped) needs nested when/endWhen blocks. An 'or' needs duplicated operation lists or a customFunction. The Eligian grammar gives authors LogicalOr, LogicalAnd and unary ! (eligian.langium:622-628). For non-literal operands, expression-transformer.ts:186 serialises these as `("left" op "right")` strings with double quotes and parentheses, which the runtime cannot parse. So the DSL's if statements can describe conditions the engine cannot evaluate. Separately, Eligian emits `{condition}` instead of `{expression}` (operation-transformer.ts:402-407). That is an Eligian bug to fix in that repo.
- Who benefits: Authors of quizzes, branching narratives and form-driven flows, and anyone gating behaviour on a position range or several flags. The Eligian DSL could compile `if (a && b || !c)` into one `when` instead of rejecting or mis-compiling it.
- Design: Replace splitExpression and parseExpression in src/operation/when.ts with a small recursive-descent parser over the same operand forms: quoted constants, numbers, and $operationdata/$globaldata/$scope chains resolved through resolveExternalPropertyChain, so the prototype-pollution guards stay. Grammar: or := and ('||' and)*; and := not ('&&' not)*; not := '!' not | cmp; cmp := operand (op operand)? | '(' or ')'. Accept both single- and double-quoted literals. Evaluation short-circuits and never uses eval or Function. Error messages name `when`, the expression and the character offset. Every existing single-comparison expression parses to the same result. Widen the TExpression type to string with a documented grammar, update the @type=ParameterType:expression docs, then run npm run opers-metadata and npm run generate-schema. Optional, and could come later: + - * / on numeric operands.
- Effort: small; breaking: false; risk: Low to medium. The parser must match today's handling of quoted strings that contain operator characters. If arithmetic is added later, property chains containing '-' would become ambiguous, so arithmetic is out of the first step. Per-tick cost is negligible, and parsed expressions can be cached by string.

#### Scrub controller: map timeline progress to a CSS custom property

- Problem: Timeline actions only run startOperations and endOperations. No operation or controller ties a visual property to timeline position. `animate` (src/operation/animate.ts:66-75) and `animateWithClass` run on wall-clock time, so they keep running when the video pauses or the user scrolls back. ScrollPositionSource (scroll-position-source.ts:8-25) exists to map scroll to time, but authors can only toggle state at discrete points with it. The only declarative workaround is an eventAction on `timeline-time`. It has to read `$operationdata.eventArgs.0`, use two nested `when` blocks for the range (when.ts allows only one comparison), chain several `calc` operations for normalisation, then selectElement and setStyle. The whole Action machinery runs on every tick for every effect, it fires for every timeline, and the result is wrong after a seek past the range. In practice this ends in customFunction.
- Who benefits: Authors of scroll-driven stories, scrubbed explainers and video overlays that should follow playback continuously, such as fades, progress fills and parallax tied to position.
- Design: Add a new controller, ScrubController (alternative name: ProgressStyleController), extending BaseController and following ProgressbarController (src/controllers/progressbar-controller.ts:33-37). It listens to `timeline-time`, and optionally to `timeline-current-timeline-change` to filter by an optional `timelineUri`. Operation data: `selectedElement` (@dependency), `start` and `end` in seconds (@required), optional `property` (default '--eligius-progress'), optional `easing` ('linear'|'easeIn'|'easeOut'|'easeInOut'), optional `timelineUri`. On each position it writes the clamped value (position - start) / (end - start) as a CSS custom property on the element. Authors then write CSS such as `opacity: calc(1 - var(--eligius-progress))` or `transform: translateY(calc(var(--eligius-progress) * -200px))`. This avoids units and per-property interpolation in the engine. The value is a pure function of position, so seeks, backward scroll and pauses are handled for free. Authors attach it with addControllerToElement in an initAction or in a timeline action that spans the effect. On attach it reads the current position with request-current-timeline-position so it is correct straight away. Register it in src/controllers/index.ts and run npm run ctrls-metadata. No engine, schema or event changes.
- Effort: small; breaking: false; risk: Low. It is one setProperty call per tick per attached controller, well inside the 16ms budget. CSS custom properties need a browser that supports them, which covers every evergreen target. The scope is deliberately narrow: no progressOperations on TimelineAction, so the engine's tick path does not change.

#### Register custom functions, templates and JSON on EligiusResourceImporter

- Problem: customFunction is the escape hatch authors fall back to, but the default importer cannot serve any custom function. EligiusResourceImporter says so in its own docs (src/importer/eligius-resource-importer.ts:20-23), and so does custom-function.ts. EngineFactory answers `request-function` only through importer.import (src/engine-factory.ts:133-135). The `template:` and `json:` placeholders and getImport have the same restriction. Every project that needs one function, template or JSON blob therefore has to write a complete IResourceImporter (import plus three name-listing methods, src/types.ts:256-281) that delegates to the built-in importer. The Unreleased name validation and did-you-mean messages do not change this; they only make the missing registration fail more loudly.
- Who benefits: Authors whose configuration or Eligian program needs one custom function, template or JSON import, and the host developer who currently has to hand-write an importer to support it.
- Design: Give EligiusResourceImporter an optional constructor argument `{functions?: Record<string, Function>; templates?: Record<string, string>; json?: Record<string, unknown>; operations?: Record<string, TOperation<any>>}`. import() checks these maps (own keys only) after the built-ins, and the did-you-mean candidates include them. Built-in names cannot be shadowed: a clash throws at construction. Registered operations are added to getOperationNames(), so ConfigurationResolver and the fluent API accept them. ConfigurationFactory's metadata-based name check gains an optional `extraOperationNames` parameter. Add a short README section. No schema or event changes.
- Effort: small; breaking: false; risk: Low. The change is additive and the default behaviour stays the same. Registered custom operations have no generated metadata or JSON schema, so document that they validate by name only.

</details>

<details><summary>integrator-first (5 proposals)</summary>

#### Destroy-safe async lifecycle: destroy() cancels pending init/start/switch

- Problem: EligiusEngine.init() (src/eligius-engine.ts ~526-586) and _initializeTimelineProvider have no destroyed or abort guard after their awaits. destroy() (src/eligius-engine.ts:694-760) neither waits for nor cancels a pending init. A probe spec created a factory engine, called init(), then awaited destroy() straight away (React StrictMode mount/unmount/remount, HMR, a fast route change). In one run init() still resolved as success after teardown. In another, `init().then(() => start())` rejected with `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'html')`: the initActions ran after destroy, when request-engine-root no longer had a responder. The host gets either a destroyed engine that claims to be initialised or a cryptic TypeError. Neither tells it what happened, and the remount is left racing a half-torn-down engine on the same container. No existing test covers destroy during init.
- Who benefits: Every React (StrictMode is on by default in dev), Vue, Svelte and SPA-router integration, and anyone using HMR. It is also the precondition for any safe framework wrapper.
- Design: In EligiusEngine, add a private `_lifecycle = new AbortController()` that destroy() aborts first. Add a small `_checkAlive(step)` that throws a named `EngineDestroyedError` (exported next to EngineActionError, with `name = 'EngineDestroyedError'`). Call it after every await in init(), _initializeTimelineProvider, start(), seek(), switchTimeline() and _activateProvider, and before each initAction runs. destroy() also records the pending init promise and awaits it settled, ignoring the rejection, before it ends active actions. Then initActions that already ran get their end operations, and those that did not are skipped. Position sources are already destroyed in destroy(). Pass the signal into _executeActions so a long initAction chain stops between actions. Public API: one new exported error class. The behaviour change is that a call pending at destroy time rejects with EngineDestroyedError instead of resolving or failing randomly. Document this in the changelog together with a 6-line React useEffect example showing the pattern `init().catch(e => { if (e.name !== 'EngineDestroyedError') throw e; })`. Integration spec: a factory-built engine with a real RafPositionSource and an async $ref locale, destroyed at each await point.
- Effort: small; breaking: false; risk: Low. The new checks sit only on async boundaries in the engine, not in the tick path, so the 16ms budget is unaffected. Some care is needed so destroy() awaiting a pending init cannot deadlock when init is itself awaiting a locale fetch: race it against the abort signal.

#### Mount into a host element and resolve provider selectors within the engine root

- Problem: The engine finds its root with a global selector every time it needs it: the `engineRoot` getter does `$(this.configuration.containerSelector)` (src/eligius-engine.ts:290-291), as do _createLayoutTemplate (:546-548), destroy (:723, :754) and the request-engine-root responder (:815, :944-947). Providers query the whole document: DomContainerProvider (dom-container-provider.ts:75), ScrollPositionSource (scroll-position-source.ts:85), VideoPositionSource (video-position-source.ts:151, :273, :411) and KeyboardStepSource (`document.querySelector`, keyboard-step-source.ts:166). This has three consequences. (a) A React or Vue host holds an element ref, not a selector, so it has to invent a unique id and rewrite configuration.containerSelector for each instance. (b) A web-component wrapper with a shadow root cannot work at all, because a document-level query never reaches inside the shadow root. (c) Two instances of the same presentation on one page share the IDs in their layoutTemplate, such as `#stage`, so the second instance's DomContainerProvider and ScrollPositionSource bind to the first instance's elements. selectElement is already scoped to the engine root (select-element.ts, `root.find`), so only the engine and the providers are at fault.
- Who benefits: Component-based hosts (React/Vue/Svelte refs), web-component and shadow-DOM embedders, and pages that show several Eligius players (course platforms, CMS blocks, Eligian previews).
- Design: 1. Add `container?: HTMLElement` to the createEngine call, either as a third `options` argument or on IEngineFactoryOptions per engine. EngineFactory passes it to the engine. The engine resolves its root once in init(), from the element if given and otherwise from `$(containerSelector)`, and caches it as `_root`. Every `$(this.configuration.containerSelector)` call site, request-engine-root included, then uses `_root`. 2. Give the position source and container provider configs an optional root. EngineFactory passes `getRoot: () => engine.engineRoot` to their constructors. BasePositionSource and DomContainerProvider get a `resolveElement(selector)` helper that looks in the root first and falls back to the document only when nothing matches, so existing configurations whose provider selectors point outside the container keep working. KeyboardStepSource uses the same helper for its target element. 3. Update the README embedding section and the IEngineConfiguration JSDoc. No schema change is needed, because the element is a runtime option and not configuration. Not covered here: the module-level globals store stays shared, so two instances that use setGlobal still share data. That is documented, and a per-engine store stays deferred.
- Effort: medium; breaking: false; risk: Medium. It touches every provider that looks up a selector. Root-first-then-document lookup changes which element is chosen only when the same selector matches both inside and outside the root, which is exactly the collision case this fixes, but it has to be called out in the changelog. Needs integration specs with two engines on one page and one engine inside a shadow root in jsdom.

#### Configurable input bindings: disable or scope the global space toggle

- Problem: EngineInputAdapter._registerHotkeys (src/adapters/engine-input-adapter.ts:50-56) always calls `hotkeys('space', ...)` in hotkeys-js's page-wide 'all' scope. The handler calls preventDefault and toggles playback. EngineFactory.createEngine always connects the adapter, and IEngineFactoryOptions has only `eventbus` (src/types.ts:148-150), so the host has no way to turn it off. In a probe spec, dispatching a space keydown on document.body moved a factory-built engine from stopped to playing. Every engine on a page reacts to every space press outside form fields. That includes space on a focused host button, so one press both clicks the button and toggles the engine. With N engines, one press toggles all of them. The resize listener (:78-86) cannot be turned off either. The last audit's judge accepted this as real and planned to fold it into the adapter rewiring. The rewiring shipped (Unreleased, Breaking Changes) without the option.
- Who benefits: Hosts that embed Eligius as one widget among others, pages with several engines, apps with their own keyboard handling or media controls, and accessibility work, where space has to activate focused controls only.
- Design: Extend IEngineFactoryOptions with `input?: {togglePlayKey?: string | false; scope?: string; element?: HTMLElement; resize?: boolean}`. EngineFactory passes it to the `new EngineInputAdapter(engine, eventbus, window, options.input)`. The adapter skips binding when `togglePlayKey === false`, binds the given key otherwise, and uses hotkeys-js's existing `scope`/`element` options. When `element` is set, it should default to the engine root once proposal 2 lands. It unbinds with the same scope it bound with. If `resize === false`, the resize listener is skipped. The default stays exactly as today. TypeDoc, a changelog entry under Added, and one unit spec per option.
- Effort: small; breaking: false; risk: Low. The default behaviour does not change. hotkeys-js's `element` option has to be checked against the version pinned in package.json (Context7) before it is relied on.

#### Supported registration of custom operations and functions

- Problem: Hosts nearly always need a few app-specific operations or functions, for example dispatching to their store, sending analytics or calling their API. Today there is no supported path. EligiusResourceImporter (src/importer/eligius-resource-importer.ts) resolves only the built-in namespaces and has no extension point. Its JSDoc says customFunction, getImport, template: and json: need you to write a whole custom IResourceImporter. The README says only that one importer is supported (README.md:38) and never explains how to make customFunction work. The fluent API, which is the authoring path for TypeScript hosts and the configuration-api entry, rejects any operation name that is not in the generated metadata: `throw Error(`Unknown operation: ${name}`)` (src/configuration/api/operation-lookup.ts:25). So a host can run a custom operation through a hand-written importer, but it cannot author one through the fluent API at all. This was expansion 3 in the last audit. Only its fail-fast half shipped.
- Who benefits: Integrators who extend the engine with domain logic, TypeScript hosts using ConfigurationFactory, and build tools using eligius/configuration-api.
- Design: 1. Give EligiusResourceImporter an optional constructor argument `{operations?, controllers?, positionSources?, containerProviders?, functions?, imports?}`. It is checked before the built-ins, rejects names that shadow built-ins unless `override: true`, and is reflected in getOperationNames(), getControllerNames() and getProviderNames(), so the resolver's did-you-mean hint and the import() error include custom names. `functions` and `imports` cover customFunction, getImport, template: and json: without a subclass. 2. Give ConfigurationFactory an optional `{customOperations?: string[] | Record<string, Partial<IOperationMetadata>>}`, which is passed to the ActionEditor and OperationEditor name checks. This keeps eligius/configuration-api DOM-free, since only names or metadata are passed and no implementations. 3. Add a README section, 'Extending Eligius', with a single example covering a custom operation, a custom function used by customFunction, and both the fluent and the JSON path. No new subsystem, and ISimpleResourceImporter stays the contract.
- Effort: small; breaking: false; risk: Low. The change is additive. The shadowing rule has to be strict, so that a custom 'selectElement' cannot silently replace the built-in one. Custom operations get no generated JSON-schema entries. The README must say that schema validation treats their operationData as untyped.

#### Self-contained type declarations for all three package entries

- Problem: The published declarations type-check only if the consumer happens to have the project's devDependencies installed. dist/index.d.mts, dist/metadata.d.mts and dist/configuration-api.d.mts all start with `import { AnimationItem, CanvasRendererConfig, HTMLRendererConfig, SVGRendererConfig } from "lottie-web"`, but lottie-web is now an optional peer (package.json peerDependenciesMeta). The files also use the global `JQuery` type 51, 44 and 44 times, with no `/// <reference types="jquery" />`, and @types/jquery is only a devDependency (jquery 4 ships no types). I copied dist into a clean consumer and type-checked `import {ConfigurationFactory} from 'eligius/configuration-api'` with tsc, strict and skipLibCheck:false. It fails with TS2307 "Cannot find module 'lottie-web'" and a list of TS2304 "Cannot find name 'JQuery'" errors. With skipLibCheck:true it compiles, but every JQuery-typed member (engineRoot, selectedElement and so on) silently becomes `any`. That hits hardest the DOM-free entries, which are meant for Node build tools that have neither package. Neither requirement is documented (README.md:393 says only `npm install eligius`).
- Who benefits: Every TypeScript consumer: build tools and the Eligian compiler using the DOM-free entries, hosts that do not use Lottie, and teams that keep skipLibCheck off.
- Design: 1. Remove the lottie-web types from the shared configuration and operation-data types that metadata and configuration-api pull in. Define minimal local structural types in src/controllers/types.ts (renderer name union, a loose renderer settings record), and keep `import type ... from 'lottie-web'` only inside lottie-controller.ts, which only the main entry reaches. 2. For JQuery, add a `/// <reference types="jquery" />` that tsdown keeps in index.d.mts, and declare @types/jquery as an optional peer next to jquery. In the DOM-free entries, swap the JQuery-typed dependency fields for a structural alias such as `type TSelectedElement = unknown`, or a re-export only the main entry widens, so they need nothing installed. 3. Add a CI step, run with tsx or as a vitest typecheck project, that type-checks a fixture consumer for each entry with skipLibCheck:false and only the packages that entry requires installed. 4. README: state the type requirements for each entry.
- Effort: small; breaking: false; risk: Low to medium. Changing how dependency fields are typed in the DOM-free entries must not change the generated metadata or JSON schema. Run `npm run metadata` and `npm run generate-schema` and diff the output. For main-entry consumers the types do not change.

</details>

## Duplicates

None.


## Refuted reports

<details><summary>Refuted</summary>

- [lottie-controller.ts:242](src/controllers/lottie-controller.ts#L242) **LottieController passes `renderer` as rendererSettings, so canvas/html is never used** (sonnet): The claim misreads what `renderer` is for. In src/controllers/lottie-controller.ts (line 57), `IInnerMetadata.renderer` is typed `SVGRendererConfig | CanvasRendererConfig | HTMLRendererConfig`. Those are lottie-web's renderer *settings* objects, not the renderer-type string. So passing it as `rendererSettings` (line 242) matches its declared type.  The controller is also deliberately SVG-only: - It types its config as `AnimationConfigWithData<'svg'>` (line 238). - It post-processes the output by setting `viewBox` on `.find('svg')` (lines 251-255).  No code path has ever offered canvas or html, so nothing is "silently not met". Choosing canvas would be a new feature, not a regression.  The tests confirm this is the intended meaning. Every LottieController test passes `renderer: {className: 'svg'}`, an `SVGRendererConfig` settings object, never a type string. That holds for the ~20 cases in src/test/unit/controllers/LottieController.spec.ts and for src/test/unit/performance/memory-leak-detection.spec.ts:99.  The auditor's failure scenario needs a configuration that breaks the TypeScript type: `renderer: 'canvas'` is not assignable to that union. Even then, lottie-web reads fields off a string config and finds nothing, so it renders SVG exactly as the controller documents through its typing.  What is really wrong is smaller: - The `@type=ParameterType:string` doc tag on `renderer` is incorrect and the name `renderer` is misleading. Both can push editor or DSL users to type a renderer name. - That is a metadata and documentation mismatch, not a rendering bug. It deserves low severity.
- [engine-factory.ts:254](src/engine-factory.ts#L254) **Calling engine.destroy() directly leaves the factory's adapters connected** (sonnet): The mechanics are described correctly. EligiusEngine.destroy() (src/eligius-engine.ts:694-766) does not know about the adapters, the interceptor or liveEngineCount. Only the closure returned by createEngine (src/engine-factory.ts:254-280) disconnects them and decrements the counter. So if a consumer calls engine.destroy() directly, the space hotkey, the resize listener and the adapter responders stay registered.  That is the documented ownership split, not a defect. The factory creates and owns the adapters and the per-engine registrations. The documented teardown is the factory result's destroy(): - IEngineFactoryResult.destroy in src/types.ts:120-123 is documented as "Destroy everything - engine, adapters, and cleanup". - IEligiusEngine.destroy in src/types.ts:205-208 only promises to clean up the engine's own resources and listeners. - README.md:311-319 and the CLAUDE.md lifecycle example both show `const {engine, destroy} = factory.createEngine(...)` followed by `await destroy()`. - CHANGELOG.md:56 describes the lifecycle guarantees ("Factory destroy() runs end operations before disconnecting adapters ... shared global data store is cleared when the last engine is destroyed") as properties of the factory destroy.  The auditor says the EngineFactory TypeDoc example "leads to" engine.destroy(). That is a misread. The example at src/engine-factory.ts:56-66 never shows teardown at all.  The two integration specs that call engine.destroy() test different things: - end-in-correct-order.spec.ts:84 tests the order of end operations. - engine-smoke.spec.ts:363 builds an EligiusEngine by hand without the factory, so there are no adapters to leak. Neither spec relies on adapter teardown, and each spec runs in its own vmThreads context, so nothing leaks across files.  The consequences are also mild. The space handler on a dead engine calls start(), and any rejection is caught and logged (engine-input-adapter.ts:63-66). Calling the factory destroy() afterwards still runs the full idempotent teardown.  No valid configuration can trigger this. It only happens when a consumer bypasses the documented teardown API, so it is at most a low-severity API-hardening suggestion.
- [create-element.ts:71](src/operation/create-element.ts#L71) **createElement lets configuration set on* attributes and create script/iframe elements** (sonnet): The code reading is right. `src/operation/create-element.ts` line 70-76 copies every truthy attribute with `template.attr(key, String(value))`. The only check on `elementName` is `/^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$/i`, so `img` with `onerror`, and `script` or `iframe`, all get through. `src/operation/set-element-attributes.ts` line 27 (`selectedElement.attr(attributes)`) has the same gap.  It is still not a vulnerability, because configuration is already trusted to supply raw HTML. Two existing paths show this: 1. `src/eligius-engine.ts` line 557 runs `container.html(layoutTemplate)` on the configured `layoutTemplate` string with no sanitizing. 2. `src/operation/set-element-content.ts` lines 48/51/54 pass a configured `template` string straight to `.html()`, `.append()` or `.prepend()`. So `setElementContent {template:'<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>'}` already runs script with no `createElement` involved.  The CHANGELOG makes this the intended design. It says "`createElement` treats `text` as plain text. Use `setElementContent` for HTML." That points users to the unsanitized HTML path. Its Security section limits sanitizing to translation and subtitle text in `LabelController` and `SubtitlesController`. That text is locale data, which can come from external `$ref` files and is treated as less trusted. `FORBIDDEN_ATTRIBUTE_PATTERN` and `isSafeUrl` belong to that locale-text policy, not to configured operation data.  The report's own second scenario goes through `setElementContent`, which inserts script on its own, so it shows nothing about `createElement`. Anyone who can write the configuration can already run code through `layoutTemplate` or `setElementContent`. Blocking `on*` attributes in `createElement` and `setElementAttributes` would therefore protect nothing; it would only make the code more consistent. The CLAUDE.md rule against executing code from configuration is about evaluating configured code (`eval` or Function), which these operations do not do. At most this is a low-severity hardening or consistency note, not a medium bug.
- [set-element-content.ts:48](src/operation/set-element-content.ts#L48) **setElementContent executes <script> from templates (jQuery domManip)** (sonnet): The mechanics are real: `selectedElement.html(template)` in src/operation/set-element-content.ts:48 does run `<script>` and inline handlers through jQuery. That is how the operation is meant to work, so it is not a defect.  1) **Documented intent.** The operation's purpose is to insert author-supplied markup into the DOM. The CHANGELOG says so explicitly. Under "Behaviour changes" (CHANGELOG.md line 43) it reads: "`createElement` treats `text` as plain text. Use `setElementContent` for HTML." The project made `createElement` text-only on purpose and named `setElementContent` as the raw-HTML path. The CHANGELOG Security section lists which surfaces sanitize: LabelController and SubtitlesController. Those render translation and subtitle text, which counts as content data. `setElementContent` is not on that list, and leaving it off was deliberate. EligiusEngine also writes `layoutTemplate` with `container.html()` (eligius-engine.ts:557), so author markup is consistently treated as trusted.  2) **Configuration is the trust boundary.** The `template` value comes from the configuration author. The CLAUDE.md rule "sanitize user-provided content" covers end-user data, not author templates. The query-param scenario only happens if the author explicitly writes `getQueryParams` followed by `setOperationData {template: '$operationdata.queryParams.name'}` into `setElementContent`. The engine does not do this on its own. It is an author choice, the same as writing `el.innerHTML = location.search` by hand.  3) **The suggested fix would break the operation.** `sanitizeHtml` (src/util/sanitize-html.ts) keeps only inline formatting tags (`ALLOWED_TAGS`) and unwraps everything else, including `div`, `img`, `button`, `ul` and `section`. The existing tests insert `<div class="init-el"></div>` and `<span class="tl-el"></span>` and then select `.init-el` (engine-factory-lifecycle.spec.ts). Integration specs such as create-language-selector, re-use-image-action and end-in-correct-order build structural markup the same way. Sanitizing by default would remove the div and its class, so later `selectElement` calls would find nothing. The tests and fixtures confirm that raw HTML insertion is the intended behaviour.  4) **Unknown insertionType.** `insertionType` is typed as the union `'overwrite' | 'append' | 'prepend'` and defaults to `'overwrite'`. The generated metadata and JSON schema constrain it to those values, so a schema-valid configuration cannot reach the silent fallthrough. At most it is a minor robustness nit.  **Overall:** this is intended, documented behaviour with a trusted-author input model. It is not a bug. At most it is a low-severity hardening or documentation suggestion: add a TypeDoc note that `template` is inserted as raw HTML and must not carry untrusted runtime data.
- [action-creator-factory.ts:61](src/configuration/api/action-creator-factory.ts#L61) **Fluent API event actions have no eventName and silently never fire** (sonnet): The report's central claim, that a fluent-built event action is accepted everywhere and never runs, is wrong.  1. The action can still run by name. ConfigurationResolver (src/configuration/configuration-resolver.ts lines 107-125) puts every resolved event action into the shared actions lookup, next to `actions`. createActionsLookup even says names are "unique across actions and eventActions". So an event action with no eventName can still be started by name, for example from DOMEventListenerController or through the action lookup that startAction-style flows use.  2. A test relies on exactly this. src/test/integration/create-language-selector.spec.ts lines 111-127 build 'BroadcastLanguageChange' with factory.createEventAction() and never set an eventName. A DOMEventListenerController then triggers it by name (`actions: ['BroadcastLanguageChange']`), and the test checks that 'language-change' is broadcast. The suggested fix would break this valid, tested pattern: making eventName required in createEventAction, or throwing in resolveEventActions when it is missing.  3. The fluent API can set eventName. Both ActionCreator.getConfiguration and ActionEditor.getConfiguration (action-editor.ts line 64) accept a callback whose returned config is synced in place into the factory's configuration. A caller can return `{...c, eventName: 'x', eventTopic: 'y'}`, with a cast for the IActionConfiguration typing. That is clumsy, but the field can be reached.  4. Configurations validated against the JSON schema already need eventName, because IEventActionConfiguration.eventName is a required `string` in types.ts line 442.  What is true: there is no dedicated setEventName/setEventTopic on the creator or editor. registerAction(action, undefined) files the action under the key 'undefined', so it never fires on an eventbus broadcast. That is an ergonomics gap, not a silent failure. The action is still reachable, and a valid configuration (one that follows the schema) never hits the case. Severity: low.
- [subtitles-controller.ts:50](src/controllers/subtitles-controller.ts#L50) **SubtitlesController detach leaves the current subtitle on screen** (sonnet): The report makes two claims. Neither holds up as a bug.  1. Engine destroy (the "indefinitely visible" case) is already handled. In `src/eligius-engine.ts` (around lines 719-755), `destroy()` first calls `detachControllersWithin($(containerSelector), eventbus)`. Later, after providers are destroyed, it runs `$(containerSelector).empty()`. A subtitle element inside the engine container is wiped along with everything else, so no subtitle text survives destroy.  2. `removeControllerFromElement` mid-subtitle (`src/operation/remove-controller-from-element.ts`). It splices the controller out of the element's data and calls `detach`. `SubtitlesController.detach` only removes the eventbus listeners via `BaseController.detach`, so the last rendered text stays in the element. That much is true. But it is the convention every controller follows: a controller stops reacting on detach and does not undo the DOM output it already produced.    - `LabelController.detach` (`src/controllers/label-controller.ts` line 120) leaves its rendered label HTML in place.    - `ProgressbarController.detach` leaves the bar width it set.    - Clearing an element is the job of the explicit `clearElement` operation (`src/operation/clear-element.ts`). A configuration that removes the subtitles controller and wants the area blank adds `clearElement` in the same end operations.    - Nothing in the controller docs says detach clears the rendered subtitle.    - The existing `SubtitlesController.spec.ts` detach test only asserts that listeners are removed, which matches this contract.  The "lastFunc keeps the closure" leak claim is also wrong. Once `removeControllerFromElement` (or `detachControllersWithin`) splices the controller out of the element's jQuery data and the listeners are removed, nothing references the controller. The controller, `lastFunc` and the bound `removeTitle`/`setTitle` closures all become garbage together.  What remains is a design preference (detach could also blank the container). It is not a defect, because it is unreachable after engine destroy and consistent with the other controllers. If changed at all, it is low severity.
- [eligius-engine.ts:529](src/eligius-engine.ts#L529) **init() throws synchronously instead of rejecting when the container is missing** (sonnet): The code is as quoted. `EligiusEngine.init()` in src/eligius-engine.ts (lines 528-544) is not async, and `_createLayoutTemplate()` (line 546) throws `Container selector not found` synchronously before any promise exists. Both pieces of evidence used to call this a bug fall apart, though.  1) The CHANGELOG line is about a different selector. Line 48 says `engine.init()` "rejects when ... a container selector matches nothing ... All of these used to hang or fail silently." The engine's top-level `containerSelector` never hung or failed silently. It has thrown synchronously since the original chrono-trigger-engine (git log -L shows `this._createLayoutTemplate()` as the first statement of init() since commit 7258e0b1). What this release changed is the timeline container provider. The new `_prepareContainer()` (added on this branch, see `git diff main..HEAD -- src/eligius-engine.ts`) now awaits `containerProvider.init?.()`. `DomContainerProvider.init()` (src/timelineproviders/container-providers/dom-container-provider.ts:70) is `async` and throws `Container element not found: <selector>`, so that failure really does reach `init()` as a rejection through the `_initializeTimelineProvider()` chain. The CHANGELOG entry describes that path, and it matches the code.  2) The sync throw for the engine container is deliberate and documented in the tests. src/test/integration/engine-smoke.spec.ts:171 has the comment "error is thrown synchronously during _createLayoutTemplate", and src/test/unit/eligius-engine.spec.ts:616 has "init() throws synchronously when container not found". It also matches the project rule in CLAUDE.md that invalid configuration must fail fast.  3) The failure scenario does not hide anything. With `engine.init().then(...).catch(showError)`, a typo in `containerSelector` raises an exception at the call site with a clear message, so the error still surfaces. With the more common `await engine.init()` inside try/catch, the error is caught either way. A valid configuration never reaches this path.  What remains is an API-consistency nit: a function returning a Promise that can also throw synchronously. That is a cleanup item at most, not a bug that breaks behaviour or contradicts the documentation. If the maintainers still want it, the fix is to make `init()` `async`, update the two tests to `await expect(engine.init()).rejects.toThrow(...)`, and add a CHANGELOG note, because this changes behaviour for callers who wrap `init()` in a synchronous try/catch.
- [eventbus.ts:271](src/eventbus/eventbus.ts#L271) **Handler unregistered during a broadcast is still invoked later in that broadcast** (sonnet): The mechanism is real: `_callHandlers` in src/eventbus/eventbus.ts (around line 271) iterates `handlers.slice()`, so if one handler calls `off()` on a later handler, the later one still runs in that broadcast. But the report's failure scenario does not hold in this engine.  1. The snapshot is deliberate and commented ("Iterate a snapshot..."). Node's EventEmitter documents the same behaviour: listeners removed during an emit still run in that emit. The test in src/test/unit/eventbus/eventbus.spec.ts (around line 252) asserts this contract: a removed handler is "not called again" on the *next* broadcast.  2. A configuration has only one way to detach a controller: the `removeControllerFromElement` operation, run by an event action. Event actions run through `ActionRegistryEventbusListener.handleEvent`, which is in the listener phase (eventbus.ts around lines 255-266). That phase runs before the handler snapshot is taken at line 271. Any controller handlers it removes on the same event are therefore not in the snapshot and do not run.  3. `EligiusEngine.destroy()` calls `detachControllersWithin` only after `await _endActionsSettled(...)`. So destroy never detaches controllers synchronously inside a handler loop.  4. No built-in controller registers a handler for 'timeline-complete', the event in the scenario. The engine adapter only broadcasts it.  5. Controller handlers already guard against running after detach. LabelController `_setLabel` returns early when `_eventbus` is null. ProgressbarController uses `selectedElement?.` and `textElement?.`. NavigationController uses `eventbus?.` and an anchor-map lookup. RoutingController uses `eventbus?.`. So the TypeError the report predicts does not happen.  The only path that reaches the behaviour is contrived. A handler for event X would have to synchronously broadcast event Y. An event action on Y would have to run selectElement plus removeControllerFromElement synchronously. And a controller handler on X would have to be registered after the first handler. Even then, the worst outcome is one harmless extra UI update from an already-detached controller. This is a design choice that matches common emitter semantics, not a defect. At most it is a low-severity, optional hardening idea.
- [select-element.ts:70](src/operation/select-element.ts#L70) **selectElement leaves selectedElement unset when nothing answers request-engine-root** (sonnet): I could not refute the code path itself. In src/operation/select-element.ts:70-73, selectElement calls `this.eventbus.request('request-engine-root')`. If that returns undefined, the operation returns with no selectedElement and logs nothing. The only onRequest responder is in src/adapters/engine-eventbus-adapter.ts:168. EligiusEngine._addEventbusListeners (src/eligius-engine.ts:810) registers 'request-engine-root' only through `eventbus.on`, the callback style, and `request()` never reaches that.  The scenario is still not a bug in a supported setup: (1) EngineFactory.createEngine always connects EngineEventbusAdapter before the engine runs (src/engine-factory.ts, `engineEventbusAdapter.connect()`). The factory's destroy() comment names this exact dependency: end operations need 'request-engine-root' for selectElement. That is why it disconnects the adapter only after engine.destroy(). (2) For direct `new EligiusEngine(...)` construction, the breaking-change entry in CHANGELOG.md (line 11) tells users to call `new EngineEventbusAdapter(engine, eventbus).connect()` themselves. Skipping the adapter goes against the documented migration. (3) An engine built without the factory is also missing the other onRequest responders that operations depend on, such as 'request-function', 'request-action' and 'request-instance'. getControllerInstance, requestAction and named functions would fail the same way. selectElement has no special flaw here; the setup itself is unsupported.  The auditor's quote from the changelog is about `broadcast(name, [callback])` queries, not `eventbus.request()`. The sentence is ambiguous, and a reader could take it to mean that selectElement works without the adapter. That is a wording problem in the docs, not an engine defect.  What remains is a diagnostics issue: no valid factory-built configuration hits it, and the misattributed error appears only with a misconfigured manual setup. Severity is low at most.

</details>
